# Projective Gravity — Version 26.10 (draft)

### The Canonical Space First: a complex Hopf lift of source shells, a derived middle space, and two bases — with branches on charge and extremality, and on rendering and canonical optics

> **A note on scale — one arena, galactic, and no cosmological slot.** The model is deliberately a *galaxy-scale toy*. Its gravitational arena is static, $B_{\rm grav}^{\rm static}\simeq\mathbb{R}_t\times\Sigma_{\rm grav}$, with spatial slice $\Sigma_{\rm grav}\equiv\mathbb{RP}^3(\ell)$ around one galaxy — ours — double-covered by $\Sigma_{\rm rep}\equiv S^3(\ell)$, the slice of the static representational bisection; and **the frozen core has no cosmological scale input**; Part XIV asks whether an apparent Hubble scale can emerge from the optical readout (§XIV.7). The $\Lambda = 1/\ell^2$ of Law I is an internal constant of that arena, never compared with the observed cosmological constant; passages written for an earlier cosmological reading are flagged or corrected where they occur. At galactic $\ell$ the plateau is of the right *order* for the Milky Way: a compact source reaches 220 km/s at $\ell = 16$ kpc, and the Galaxy's own profile, taken isotropically, at $\ell\approx13$ kpc. Two problems internal to the toy stay open. The Einstein-static arena needs a uniform closure source of about $2\times10^{17}M_\odot$ on the gravity base — some four million times the Galaxy's baryons — which Law I's source map must somehow generate (§XI.2, item 0′). And a galactic sphere used literally as optical space caps angular-diameter distances, which observation forbids (§XI.2, item 0). **Other galaxies are a detached wing** (Part XII): Tully–Fisher for them is scoped, as an optical rescaling rule, but deliberately not attached to the model.
>
> **What is new in version 26 — from the one-particle bundle to a local matter field, Part XXI.** A new branch for a problem different in kind: Part XX's one-particle spin bundle over the mass shell is kept apart from the field-theoretic object, a rank-four Dirac bundle $S_D\to B_{\rm rep}$ whose free solutions mode-decompose over the mass shell after quantization. Its first question — does the shell construction canonically produce $S_D$ and a local action on its sections — has a topological half answered now: on the cover $\Sigma_{\rm rep} = S^3(\ell)$ the spin structure is unique and the Dirac bundle trivializable, so the spacetime spinor bundle is topologically unambiguous, with the global gluing of the shell construction to it the remaining step; on the gravity base $\mathbb{RP}^3(\ell)$ there are exactly two spin structures, distinguished by the sign under the twin, which classify the spinorial descents of matter from the cover — for spinors the twin theorem's same-sign pair becomes a choice of descent, spectrally visible in the asymmetric Dirac spectra of the two structures. The action half is open, and the statistics firewall is stated: no Grassmann variables by fiat — the architecture is first searched for a reason for antisymmetry, and if none is found spin–statistics is imported and said to be. And a prospective clue is tested rather than merely mentioned: a Koide-type charged-lepton spectrum written as a product of two dual factors a quarter turn apart, $m_n = m_*(\cos\theta_n+1/\sqrt2)^2$, gives $K = 2/3$ from the $\mathbb Z_3$ identities with nothing inserted; the operator form lives exactly on the theory's spin-one carrier with the weight states of the selected axis as its sectors, each factor is twice a Born overlap, and the physical leptons sit inside the positive branch — but the order-three element is available, not forced, the quarter turn's use is a postulate, the coherent-state and operator forms are two constructions agreeing only on values, and the cyclic reassignment of masses is a unitary outside the shell: a structural clue, not a derivation. The three itself has a structural candidate (§XXI.4a): the carrier restricted to a third turn is the regular representation of $\mathbb Z_3$, every character once, which no other order achieves on a three-dimensional carrier — the three being $\dim H_u$ through ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2\simeq\mathbb C\otimes H_u$ — so that, under an occupancy premise not yet derived, exactly three generations would follow; and the carrier's own conjugation exchanges the two transverse sectors covariantly while fixing the axial one, the candidate $\tau$ sector to which the fitted near-$2/9$ phase would then belong. The descent that makes the label internal is constructed (§XXI.4b): the generation space is the multiplicity space of the selected axis's stabilizer on the native carrier, three weight lines on which the residual symmetry acts trivially, covariant under rotations of the rendering and sector-preserving under the canonical transport — with the transverse sectors carrying opposite geometric phases $\mp\Omega$ around a loop of the axis and the axial sector none, so that a moving axis would be visible only in generation-coherent superpositions, the neutrino sector — so that $S_D\otimes G_{\rm gen}$ is three Dirac copies and not a spinor times a vector; and the reduction has a candidate origin that is not a spatial choice (§XXI.4d): every real rendering of the complex canonical geometry selects a split real form, which is an unoriented axis, universal to renderings, non-preferred and orientation-free — its stabilizer giving the $\tau$/$(e,\mu)$ pattern directly and its noncompact generators, Hermitian on the carrier, supplying the sector-breaking that mixing requires — the compact class being every operator diagonal in the weight basis, which is why $M_\ell$ can be non-mixing while Koide lives in its functional form; the degree-one split class excluded; and a generalized CP symmetry making every operator built from one split direction, at any angle and degree, conserve oscillation CP exactly, $J_{\rm CP} = 0$ with the three angles fitted, so that established Dirac-type CP violation would require two independent non-collinear split directions — irreducible two-direction structure in the split plane, carried by the relative orientation of successive shear steps and not by any net boost (§XXI.4e); Part XVII's own caution kept, the selection being its proposal and not a theorem; and why matter refers its generations to the ambient selected form stays the hypothesis; the three is then $\dim H_u$ with no $\mathbb Z_3$ chosen, the third turn being exactly the equal spacing of the sector phases that channel-before-role would demand; and among the axis's own dual pairings the quarter turn is the unique nontrivial one with an invariant Koide ratio, still selected only by its value. The sectors are the three critical rays of a Rayleigh functional on the projective generation space, Morse indices $0$, $2$, $4$, fixed points of the projective flow with coherent oscillation off them (§XXI.5); reducing a deeper internal variable to a stationary branch commutes exactly with varying the matter action afterwards; and neutrino mixing requires the neutrino operator to break the charged-lepton axis's residual symmetry, a single tilted axis being excluded by the shape of the observed mixing. Parts 0–XX unchanged; Part XX frozen at 25.14, which records the author's question of what spin becomes when the legs separate (§XX.12) and the Kerr–fermion directional-slot dictionary (§XX.4a).

> **What is new in version 25 — projective spin histories, Part XX.** An experimental branch asks whether the zero-width directional geometry first exposed by the dual-history and Jacobi construction has a massive analogue whose Hopf lift is the state geometry of spin-$\tfrac12$. The proposal does not let the Galaxy choose a particle's spin: canonical geometry supplies the state slot and local physics occupies it. No new internal coordinate is allowed — Kerr's complex construction is the sanity rule. Live claims, as of 25.14, at which Part XX is frozen: the fiberwise blow-up of the spatial-separation bundle along a massive history has divisor $\mathbb P(H_u)\simeq\mathbb{RP}^2$, whose orientation cover $S(H_u)\simeq S^2$ — the whole sphere, never one history's choice — is the candidate slot, ubiquitous kinematically; Part I's own construction equivariantly identifies the rest space's spin group with the shell, ${\rm Spin}(H_u)\simeq SU(2)_{\rm shell}$ with $\mathbb{CP}^1_{\rm shell}\simeq S(H_u)$; the conjugate structure is the canonical orthogonal-partner involution; the shell's antipodal involution is the $2\pi$ spinor sign locally; the unoriented axis space $\mathbb P(H_u)$ is canonically, and uniquely $SO(3)$-equivariantly, identified with the split real-form family $\mathcal R_{\rm split}$ of Part XVII, so Kerr's angular-momentum direction and the electron's Bloch direction are two readings of one shell-derived directional slot, with the representation firewall kept; the canonical Hopf connection is the Berry connection exactly, $-i\langle\psi\vert d\psi\rangle = \mathcal A_H$, which becomes the constraint $\iota_{\rm spin}^*F_H = \tfrac12\sin\theta\,d\theta\wedge d\phi$ on the connection's angular deformations; the $2\pi$ sign, the Born overlaps and the Wigner rotation hold on the shell's groups, and Fermi–Walker transport in its $SL(2,\mathbb C)$ gives exactly the Thomas precession; the Weyl sectors are algebraically available with a derived chirality index and no new coordinate, the identification of the conjugate operation with physical chirality conditional; and a counterfactual is killed: were the meridian an electromagnetic Kaluza–Klein circle, which the frozen architecture and the charge branch both decline, its own transport of a spin vector would leak spin into the meridian, its horizontal induction would not be a BMT law, and on null five-dimensional geodesics it would give the classical Kaluza–Klein $g = 1$, half of Dirac's — the minimal meridian-KK geometrization does not supply the physical spin interaction, so the shell gives the state space, the rest-space connection gives Wigner and Thomas transport, and the electromagnetic Hamiltonian gives the magnetic-moment dynamics, one structure per job. And the connection on that bundle is now constructed from existing structure — the Levi-Civita spin lift along $x$, the pure-boost horizontal distribution of $SL(2,\mathbb C)/SU(2)$, the $-1$ eigenspace of the compact real structure, along $u$ — and passes the benchmark: with the minimally coupled matter term and nothing else it reproduces Bargmann–Michel–Telegdi to $5\times10^{-5}$ degrees per period for $g = 0, 1, 2, 2.5$ and $2.00232$ at three speeds. One naturally associated bundle built from the theory's existing geometry has the correct state space, Lorentz transport, geometric phase and local spin dynamics of a massive spin-$\tfrac12$ degree of freedom. And it extends: carrying one rest-frame spinor into both Weyl sectors by the lift and its Cartan twist gives $u(p) = \sqrt m(B^{-1}\xi,B\xi)$, for which the positive-energy momentum-space Dirac constraint is the identity $B^2 = p\cdot\bar\sigma/m$ of the pure boost — the Cartan pair supplying the representation content of the mass coupling, $m$ remaining input, the construction Lorentz-covariant with the second sector the Cartan twist of the first — and the negative-frequency $v$ sector is the graded lift of the $j$-conjugate rest spinor, $v = \sqrt m(B^{-1}j\xi,-Bj\xi) = M_Cu^*$, with the completeness relations holding — the complete free Dirac plane-wave spinor data, positive- and negative-frequency, parameterized by future $p$, from the shell representation without a canonical coordinate, quantization being what would make the $v$ modes antiparticles. The internal discrete-symmetry matrices are the theory's own two structures — parity's intertwiner the sector exchange $\rho$, time reversal the conjugate lift $j$, charge conjugation the chirality-graded composite — with their actions on the coupled equation verified; the internal c-number $CPT$ composite is the linear $-\gamma^5$, and the spacetime parts and the quantum-field operator await the Pin extension and the field theory. The meridian-sign intuition does not survive as charge conjugation. Proving that physical massive matter occupies the bundle is the central identification bridge; the spacetime Dirac law as dynamics, quantization and statistics, the full spacetime $P$, $T$ and $CPT$, the full pullback of $\mathcal A_H$ and the $Y$-history interpretation remain open beside it. *(Revision history: 25.0 stated that the global twin cover is the spinor–vector cover and that a pair's two lifts are its two orthogonal states; 25.1 narrowed the first to an open intertwining of a cover of positions with a cover of frames, and the second to a proposal, and made the slot the whole sphere; 25.2 corrected the blow-up to the fiberwise spatial one, the terminology of spin structure, the parity language, and added the Thomas test; 25.3 demoted "soldering" to an equivariant identification, made the $\mathbb{RP}^5$ statement about local Jacobi data, introduced the bundle over the mass shell, computed the meridian transport; 25.4 typed the mass-shell bundle over $B_{\rm rep}$, recast the meridian computation as a counterfactual test of a candidate the architecture had already declined, weakened its conclusion to the exclusion of the minimal KK transport, separated the Dirac baseline from the observed $g$, and froze the geometric stage; 25.5 synchronized the terminology, wrote the bundle's exact tangent sequence, kept the $Y$-sector identification open, typed the mass shell in the cotangent bundle, narrowed the Kaluza–Klein moral, and constructed the connection on the spin bundle — passing the Bargmann–Michel–Telegdi benchmark for every $g$ with nothing added; 25.6 derived the on-shell Dirac relation as the identity of the lift and the internal discrete-symmetry matrices from $\rho$ and $j$; 25.7 retracted $CPT = -1$ — the internal product is $-\gamma^5$ — inserted the chirality grading into charge conjugation, limited parity to its internal intertwiner, weakened the meridian conclusion to a failed identification, and constructed the antiparticle sector with completeness; 25.9 typed the $v$ family as the negative-frequency partners in the paired mode space rather than further solutions at $p$, wrote parity as the intertwining relation $P_\rho D(g)P_\rho^{-1} = D(\rho(g))$, pinned the $\sigma/\bar\sigma$ conventions, and synchronized the status table; 25.10 made the one editorial change left and froze the part.)* Parts 0–XIX unchanged.

> **What is new in version 24 — centrade triality, Part XIX.** A branch on representation type: the conjecture that the Kerr–Newman simplex is the rendered image of a permutation-symmetric three-channel centrade state, with spacetime selection assigning the channels their roles as spin, charge and nonextremality. Its firewall is that the symmetry must exist before the rendering labels the channels, and Part XVIII dictates that permutations act on the rendering functor, never on the metric, which dissolves the earlier fork by letting the gravity base change. First computations: the two static channels have curvature readouts of one fall-off — the optical trace reads $\delta^2/r^4$, the Reissner–Nordström Ricci tensor reads $Q^2/r^4$, traceless — with budget $(M/r^2)^2$, the Newtonian field squared, so the $\mathbb Z_2$ exchange swaps two readouts and keeps the field equations; the spin channel's readout is axial and linear in $a$ where the others are quadratic scalars of the same fall-off, so no $S_3$ can act on readouts; and the spectral numerology fails at the first hurdle, spherical symmetry giving two optical Ricci eigenvalues, not three. Static first; spin last; three dimensions last of all. Parts 0–XVIII unchanged. *(24.1: the referee's corrections — all three readouts fall as $r^{-4}$ and differ in representation type, not fall-off; the charge readout is a tensor; the $\mathbb Z_2$ exchange is an involution of magnitudes with the charge sign unfixed, its readout exchange a proposal and its field-equation closure derived; the budget operator made abstract with unordered eigenlines; the simplex distinguished from the typed parameter space, with the lift test as the hard test — and the author's flavor inspiration built in: the channel space $\mathbb C^3\supset S^5\to\mathbb{CP}^2$ with the Kerr–Newman weights as phase-blind intensities, $S_3$ as the Weyl group, a mixing-matrix rendering under which relative phase repacks the budget within a fixed sum, the Hopf phase as the common phase with the relative phases inert under it, two imaginary units kept apart, and the minimal typed object for the $\mathbb Z_2$ sector. 24.2: the theory's own $\mathbb C^3$ computed — 24.1's tension withdrawn, since the diagonal Hopf circle acts on the shell's ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2$ as a common phase and the space is twin-even and the complexified 3-space; rendering as a real frame, with the polarization $p$ as the one invariant and $(1+p)/2$ the largest weight any channel can wear; the canonical normal making spin axial and charge–gap a transverse doublet, the $\mathbb Z_2$ an exact quarter turn, the surviving symmetry $\mathbb Z_4$ with the plane reflection; and history transport repacking the budget through the spatial holonomy, given the transport assumption. 24.3, precision and the Kerr instance: weight two, with the common connection $2\mathcal A_H$ and twin-evenness the same fact; descent narrowed to the source–twin identification; the carrier distinguished from a populated state space — single-spinor states are null, $p = 0$, excluded under both natural renderings, so the channel state must be a superposition, which the conjugate-lift pair supplies; the Schur–Horn region exact; the rendering hypothesis separated from the natural $SO(3)$; the selected axis in place of the global normal, with $\mathbb Z_4\times\mathbb Z_2$ computed; the split transport withdrawn as a phase source; and the author's sanity check applied — Kerr–Newman's source is $\zeta = \delta+ia$ along the axis, rod and Newman–Janis displacement, with spacetime's own imaginary unit, so the geometry pairs spin with gap, not charge with gap, and the transverse-doublet proposal is withdrawn. 24.4: the referee's precision — the carrier's symmetry is $SU(2)\times U(1)$ with Weyl group $\mathbb Z_2$, not $U(3)$; $\zeta$ a candidate unified datum carrying the spin–gap subbudget $M^2-Q^2$, not the full budget; the conjugate pair interpolating in polarization with coefficients still to be derived; single-spinor exclusion conditional on the rendering hypothesis; the Kerr principle and rendering covariance stated, with fixed-$g_G$ repacking marked as the version-23 subclass — and the charge question answered by Law I's own Kinnersley symmetry: Harrison charging of Kerr, computed on the axis, gives $M = M_0\cosh b$, $Q = M_0\sinh b$ with $a$ and $\delta$ untouched, so the mass–charge pair is split, the gap–spin pair compact, and the Kerr–Newman budget is the equality of the two invariants, with the Ernst target space the non-compact dual of the flavor $\mathbb{CP}^2$. 24.5: the canonical map from the conjugate pair computed — the conjugate lift is the quaternionic structure $j$ and generates exactly the conjugate null vector, so the pair spans $\{v,\bar v\}$; the Hopf phase acts on the pair-built vector as a rotation about the lift's axis by twice the phase; coefficients $(e^{\eta/2},e^{-\eta/2})$ give $p = {\rm sech}\,\eta$, with the scale character the proposed source; the Kerr–Newman states are the equal-weight pair states; the soldering is the equivariant spinor–vector map; and $\zeta$ is the charging-invariant axial datum where the pole datum $M+ia$ is not.)*

> **What is new in version 23 — conformal repacking over an invariant gravity quotient, Part XVIII.** A branch on identity and budget, begun where the mathematics is exact. The optical geometry of Reissner–Nordström has scalar curvature $-6\delta^2/r^4$: the nonextremality is the trace of the optical Ricci tensor, extremality is optical scalar-flatness, and the optical conformal factor obeys a Poisson equation sourced by the nonextremality — harmonic exactly at extremality, which is why extremal centres superpose into Majumdar–Papapetrou, optically scalar-flat for any number of them. The Couch–Torrence inversion is an exact isometry of the extremal optical geometry, rendering one radial class twice with the photon sphere fixed, and the squared optical Ricci norm is an inversion-invariant identifier of the pair, as the author guessed. On these the branch states its thesis — a spacetime is a rendering of invariant canonical data, centrade fields measure the obstruction to unpacking it, and repackings move between renderings while the gravity quotient stays fixed — corrects the Kerr–Newman budget to a simplex, meets its fork honestly (Law I makes a hole's $M$, $a$, $Q$ gravitationally visible, so the guaranteed budget is between a source and its twin), casts path-dependent identity as a rendering groupoid, and states the Boltzmannian counting with its two requirements. Static first; Kerr through Randers geometry last. Parts 0–XVII unchanged. *(23.1, the next pass of the program: the two-centrade test — the extremal static sector is an exact product and joint extremality is the only static coexistence, so the non-product structure of the static sector is extremality itself; the transition sign derived — the inversion, the throat exchange and the antipodal map are all orientation-reversing, so all three identifications invert the Hopf fiber, unifying the twin parity with the Couch–Torrence pair; the obstruction test's first negative, the conformal factor's Weyl connection being flat; and the stationary test — neither the Randers Riemannian part nor the ZAMO optical metric of Kerr–Newman is scalar-flat at extremality, so the trace dictionary is static. 23.2, the next round: the optical conformal factor named — $W = \sqrt{\Omega/N}$, so the optical trace is the mismatch between the fiber-length locking and the lapse, vanishing exactly where §X.2 found $N\Omega = 1$; the two-body statement made exact through the Chruściel–Tod classification; the kernel-transport criterion derived, gauge does not repack, and the repacking group defined as the branch's first concrete kill test; and the Finsler Ricci of the Randers optical geometry computed with exact Riemannian checks — at extremality with spin it is $-(6/r^4)\,c\,a^2$, so charge resolves optically and spin does not. 23.3, corrections and the decisive question: the Couch–Torrence fiber inversion retracted — its degree on the linking sphere is $+1$, so the Chern class forces nothing, while the throat exchange and the twin keep their inversions; Version 23 pauses conceptually. 23.4, the freeze: intuition 27 synchronized with the Couch–Torrence retraction; the kill tests distinguish the retired conformal mechanism from the open rendering-space one; and the automorphism statement narrowed to the tower's known symmetries, the full classification left to the groupoid direction.)*

On the local model this derives the compact/split diamond of $U(1)$ and $\mathbb R^\times$ over $\{\pm1\}$; one holomorphic connection restricting to pure phase on the compact form and pure scale on the split form, the latter's curvature the area form of a one-sheeted hyperboloid; the dual shell $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ over de Sitter two-space; the identity of the residual sign over the plane with the $-1$ planar holonomy; the reality, from the split side, of the planar dual histories; a split form of signature $(3,3)$ offering a candidate reason for matter to live on the compact one; and, by a reflection theorem, the selection of the surviving redshift sector — it is the polar Galactic normal, and the Hopf data must carry an axial order parameter along it, which a rotating disk supplies. It is honest about what it does not derive: the connection's oddness, which bi-reality assumes under another name. > **What is new in version 21 — visual sheets, Part XVI.** The observer's sky is taken as the projection of an enriched history space of canonical classes, a rendered galaxy as one stationary sheet of it, so that one sky point can lie on several galaxies distinguished by dual-path data the point does not display, and phase, dilation, shape, apparent distance and a possible emergent normal become properties of one sheet — with the Hubble relation as the geometry of how a sheet sits in that space, $d\eta/dD_{\rm app}\simeq H_0/c$, rather than two rules and a coincidence. Two facts open the part: the one-traversal twin image of the Galaxy is a single inverted full-sky image, so multiplicity needs the broken family; and anisotropic magnification cannot make the sky's disks — round renders round through the smooth Galaxy, and order-unity shear needs passage within ten astronomical units of a star — so rendering-made disks survive only as sheet selection beyond shear, the author's off-plane suppression, which steepest descent makes a legitimate mechanism class. No isotropic ball is asserted, because local dynamics forbid it. The substrate is unchanged. *(21.1: the history space stratified; sheets as regular stationary branches conditional on nondegeneracy; the normal placed in sheet selection, not as a second kernel of the $3\to2$ projection, which would render lines; several fiber states made necessary rather than sufficient; off-sheet suppression tied to a Stokes or complex-saddle transition with $\operatorname{Im}\mathcal S>0$, with a defined semiclassical parameter; and the Hubble relation placed on the moduli space of sheets with $\eta$ and $D_{\rm app}$ independent and a no-circularity rule. 21.2: the strong fiber $\Pi_O^{-1}(x,\hat n)$ — same source, same point, different galaxies; the Hopf connection is odd under it, so Part XIII's assumed odd parity is derived and the plane connection is flat with the sign monodromy; the plane is the intersection of two real forms and the lifted reflection equals the real structure on the normal history mode up to charge conjugation; stationarity of the plane is symmetry-protected with the normal as the odd mode; and the same oddness imposes two parity kill tests on Part XV's redshift sector, which the current sign conventions fail unless the Hopf data break the plane symmetry or the twin parities are not like-signed. 21.5, a precision pass: the Chern class derives the fiber-inverting lift, not the connection's parity, which needs equivariance; the lift typed as anti-equivariant on $U(1)$ and $w\mapsto w^{-1}$ on the complexified fiber; the fixed points $w = \pm1$ restricted to the physical fiber; Hopf-phase conjugation, not charge conjugation; the loop orientation tracked, so that the meridian mode is killed and the normal mode may survive; and the kill tests stated on the total systemic $\eta$, with the monopole term the unavoidable model contribution. 21.6, the synchronization pass that freezes the conceptual content of version 21: Part XV's meridian direction relabelled its original candidate, excluded under plane-equivariant Hopf data; the three normals — meridian, Galactic, and per-image rendered — named and kept apart; the topological step from the Galactic-plane reflection to a degree $-1$ map of the linking sphere stated and tagged; the two-real-forms result scoped to the complexified base; one global orientation said to remove only the $Y\to-Y$ ambiguity; the normal-mode extension test added, and passed on the round arena for every class but the great circles through the plane's poles; and the representation problem stated in its cleanest form. 21.7, the final freeze of version 21, the control architecture for version 22: the common-fixed fiber $\{\pm1\}$ distinguished from the $\rho_E$-real fiber, which remains $U(1)$; the meridian verdict qualified by equivariance wherever it is stated; the verified odd transformation of $Y$ separated from the conditional equality of mirror-class $\eta$; the in-plane reflection given its own symbol $\kappa$; and the monopole field called the canonical representative of the forced unit class rather than pointwise forced.)*
>
> **What is new in version 20 — the bi-connection substrate, adopted by decision.** Part XV states the author's Universal Null-History Principle — light and gravitational waves share one characteristic cone, one Jacobi gate and one history class, differing only as representations — and adopts, as a decision, the middle geometry as the symmetric resolution of two exchange-related Weyl-type connections sharing one null geometry. What the pair does is verified: its mean is Levi-Civita, all three connections share every unparametrized null geodesic, and its relative holonomy around a two-history loop is a homothety, $e^{-2\int_\Sigma F_A}$ — abelian, Stokes-expressible, non-exact and additive *(this real-loop mechanism is superseded by 20.2: on the real middle space the scale flux vanishes, and the scale character lives on the conjugate complex lifts of a class)*. It is *not* the Jacobi squeeze of §XIV.9, and cannot be: the two are the $\mathbb R_+$ and $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ factors of one relative transport, scale and shape, which moves the redshift candidate into the homothety and dissolves the reciprocal-branch problem. The enclosed flux is first order in a class's width, so redshift becomes a property of finite classes; its sign rule, magnitude and universality are open, the recurrence-age bound survives for cumulative readings, and the multi-messenger requirement $\eta_{\rm EM} = \eta_{\rm GW}$ is anchored by GW170817. The fiber-product deepening of the middle space is stated as a target, not adopted. Added in the same version: the branch's model-independent foundation — the conjugate-point theorem with its local and global halves (§XV.0) — and §XV.6, the author's larger ambition made precise: the two Born paths and the two connections as one doubled transport, by the closed-time-path dictionary; one complex relative holonomy, phase for interference and scale for dilation, the same $\mathbb C^\times$ as the charge branch's and §IX.1's; and the offloading theorem, exact, with its obstruction the curvature — which sources the candidate dilation. *(20.1: the principle typed through the tower as a commuting diagram; the relative holonomy defined, the factor two coming from comparing the two connections around one loop; the relative transport placed in $CSp(4,\mathbb R)$; the closed-time-path dictionary downgraded to a shared algebraic pattern; the offloading theorem's global loophole examined and closed for the scale part; GW170817's role reduced to the shared cone; and §XV.7 added — the exchange is the compact real structure acting on $\mathbb C^\times$, and $\rho_E$-equivariance of a $\mathbb C^\times$-connection derives the firewall, the scale part vanishing on real directions and living on imaginary displacements. 20.2: that firewall kills the real-ribbon redshift of versions 20–20.1, and §§XV.1–XV.3 are rewritten around what replaces it — the conjugate canonical lifts of a class; the complex connection is *constructed* as the unique analytic continuation of the Hopf connection, the bridge $F_A(v,Jw) = -F_H(v,w)$ verified exactly on the Hopf model, the scale character of a class shown to be a Hopf flux, $\eta_\Xi = 2\int F_H(Y,k)\,ds$, with no new field, and the imaginary displacement shown to be a Jacobi field, so that the conjugate-point gate returns as the existence condition of a complex lift; relative transport and scale character are separated, the ledger's norm factor corrected, and the results marked conditional on the descent. 20.3: the Hopf-flux formula restated as a variation formula, exact on the real locus with an $O(\epsilon^3)$ residual verified; the imaginary displacement as the first-order Jacobi field of a complex variation, conjugacy the necessary infinitesimal gate, the finite family explicit in the round arena; "no new field" narrowed to "no new independent field, $F_A$ fixed by the full $\mathcal A_H$"; real analyticity made explicit; the curvature typed downstairs; and the decisive component computed on the local model — $\bar F_H(\partial_{\rm mer},k) = -k\cdot\nabla a_{\rm mer}$, free there, whose cosine moment along a class vanishes for like twin parities and is one-signed for opposite ones, making the twin parity the sign rule. 20.4: that last claim corrected — an odd component makes the moment symmetry-allowed, not one-signed; the sign observable made the gauge-invariant mixed curvature, with the sign theorem $\bar F_H(N,k)\ge0$; the meridian-invariant gauge stated; the twin-equivariant parity law derived, the mixed curvature even about the midpoint of every class under twin equivariance of the connection; the self-return shown to lie in the kernel with the imaginary direction along the global normal, the convention the recurrence-age bound selects; the comb's washing-out marked a possibility; and Part XV's landing stated — a derived candidate source of dilation, not a derived redshift law. 20.5, the conceptual freeze of Part XV: the acceptance criterion made the weighted moment, pointwise positivity the sufficient stronger target; the parity law restated between paired histories, $f_d(s) = f_{-d}(\pi\ell-s)$, the per-history form shown to need reflection symmetry or a class average; the self-return kernel restricted to the round smooth self-return and exactly twin-paired continuations, with persistence under the perturbed return map an open theorem; scale kernel distinguished from visual kernel; the convention language softened to passing and failing the recurrence-age test; and the summary wording synchronized — scale character, not relative holonomy; curvature as the obstruction that sources the candidate dilation, not the redshift. 20.6: the freeze confirmed; the doubling stated to make the null-history principle representation-independent rather than to derive it; $GL^+(2,\mathbb R)$; the opening and the ledger synchronized with the landing; the twin image a *candidate* carrier; and the finite-width / high-redshift test added to §XIV.8 — with twin-paired returns not accumulating, large $z$ must arise within one class, four to five orders of magnitude above the local calibration, outside the proved small-$\epsilon$ regime.)*
>
> **What is new in version 19 — a deliberately ambitious branch, Part XIV.** From two research briefs: modular return optics, in which other galaxies would be recurrent photon-history classes of the one arena rendered with a unitary multiplicative redshift, and the galactic well as the scale of that redshift. Everything in it is provisional, and its first calculations already prune it. Secondaries do break the round antipodal family, into about two first-order stationary members each — not a galaxy count. The addendum's literal well fails its own first null test: the flux through the hemisphere separating source from twin vanishes by symmetry, and the arena-scale depth is dominated by the galaxy's own central self-potential. The coincidence $M_{\rm gal}\sim cH_0\ell^2/G$ encodes the arena radius, and its heuristic coefficient $1/4$ selects 7 kpc, against 13–16 kpc from Part IV and 25 kpc from the detached wing. Returned starlight would exceed the measured optical background twenty- to two-hundredfold, so the readout must send nearly all of it into its kernel; the branch's sharpest construction test is the invariant that puts the observer's own return there. What survives is stated as proposals with a falsification ledger and a single deciding question (§XIV.10). Parts 0–XIII are unchanged. *(19.1: the ledger corrected after review — the member count is not a galaxy count, the starlight budget overshoots the background, both return times are given with their dependence on $\ell$, and the self-return test is stated as a construction tool. 19.2: the opening synchronized with Part XIV, the flux-suppression factor made conditional on $D_{\rm app}$ acting as a luminosity distance, the Morse count's smoothing stated, the brightness budget sourced in its own box, and the next calculation named. 19.3: the history-dependent functional computed — the boost holonomy vanishes in the static arena and reduces to the tidal integral for the null loop; the tidal integral is sign-indefinite; and the null focusing column gives the Hubble scale to within a factor $1$–$1.6$, independently of $\ell$, for classes centred on the Galaxy — the coincidence becoming $M_{\rm gal}/h^2\approx2cH_0/G$. 19.4: the focusing functional made affine-invariant and explicitly background-subtracted, and renamed; the pivot stated — $\ell$ sets recurrence, $GM/h^2$ the dilation scale; the off-centre observer made the decisive problem; and §XIV.9 added, deriving from the relative Jacobi monodromy a noncompact squeeze with coefficient $1/4$, to which the background contributes nothing, and defining the collective class as a return-map island. 19.5: the focusing functional normalized by the Killing energy; the derived sector named the isotropic $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ block of the full $Sp(4,\mathbb R)$ monodromy, which is then computed, separating a screen-invariant $\eta_{\rm iso}$ from a shear $\gamma_{\rm aniso}$; the $1/4$ restricted to localized lumps; a positive squeeze distinguished from a positive redshift, which is now the bridge to build; the island observer-conditioned with the Liouville measure; and a recurrence-age bound added, $\ell$ cancelling, which caps the cumulative reading of $z$ at about $0.1$.)*
>
> **What is new in version 18 — two additions.** *Conjugate-path optics* (§XIII.10), from the author's brief: the isolated ray replaced by the Jacobi-critical class it belongs to, with conjugacy as the gate. Four calculations are done — on the round arena the release points are $L = n\pi\ell$ and the first is the twin, with the antipodal family a critical manifold; a weak secondary splits it by the point-lens shear; the theory's static metric has the standard Fermat form; and within the solar system the gate stays shut, the solar focus lying at 548 AU. Standard theory already sums stationary rays, so the proposal is placed in the readout, as a Photon History Postulate, with a first acceptance test: on the gravity slice every source refocuses on itself after $\pi\ell/c$ from every direction, and that family must not render as an image — quantified: the Sun's own light would return at the solar constant from every direction, so canonical position cannot be visual position for returning light. A proposed modular-return branch, in which returns render as other galaxies, is opened as a ledger of requirements on that condition, with its pros and cons recorded. And the canonical metric in bundle form, below.
>
> **What is new in version 18 — the canonical metric in bundle form.** Of the recommendations on the Berry-phase analogy, one was worth adopting for its parsimony: the bundle form $\hat g = \pi_H^*g_{\mathcal M}+R_H^2(d\chi+\mathcal A_H)^2$, which packages the frozen $3+1+2$ blocks into a single object with standard curvature formulas, and turns the action target into one question — what condition on $\hat g$ makes the quotient obey Law I? (§IX.1). It gives Berry's phase a size, $\Delta s_H = (L_H/2\pi)\Delta\chi$: the connection says how far around the fiber a loop goes, the locking says how far that is. And the first candidate condition is tested and excluded: Einstein's vacuum equations on $E$ make each source a Kaluza–Klein monopole whose fiber shrinks toward the source while the locking's grows — Obstruction 1 as a sign — and would make the fiber's absolute size observable, a galactic fiber exceeding solar gravity at 1 AU by $5\times10^{26}$. The fork that leaves is recorded. Parts 0–IX remain frozen; §IX.1 is a target, not a law.
>
> **What is new in version 17.6 — the completion's price, and a freeze.** The smooth local completion of §II.2 has a metric consequence: the circle's generator vanishes on the restored meridian, so any smooth metric collapses the fiber there, $L_H = O(\sqrt\rho)$, while Law I's locking grows it toward a source. The Taub–NUT nut is the smooth example — the reciprocal warping already recorded as Obstruction 1 (§XI.3), now forced into the core. So the locking and the principal connection are laws on the *regular stratum* $E^\circ$, and the orbit's behaviour at an exceptional stratum is a separate question; §0.1, the primitive data, Law 0, §III.2, Structure III and the action target are synchronized to this. Canonical optics is typed on $E$, Rule A is labelled an Aethic stipulation rather than a requirement of quantum mechanics, and optical non-closure is phrased as integrated holonomy rather than large curvature. With these, Parts 0–IX are frozen.
>
> **What is new in version 17.5 — the author's latest considerations, audited and made explicit.** The author's entries of 22 September were checked against the document. Two were misrepresented and are repaired: the author's own cut-and-flip of a rosette, which gives two point-symmetric spirals (§XIII.7), and the ordering argument that the normal's special treatment precedes gravity's quotient, so gravity must inherit a correspondent of it (§XIII.9). Several were missing and are added: the Machian motivation for renderings and the rooted form of the complexity criterion (§XIII.0, §XIII.6); visual space as built from pairs, with a blur reading of the circumgalactic boundary (§XIII.8); the author's framing of the gravitational firewall (§XIII.9); and the recurring budget motif. The branch's research program is now stated explicitly, as a numbered list at the end of §XIII.0.
>
> **What is new in version 17.4 — targets, not mechanisms, and an existing budget.** The Reissner–Nordström factorization is recorded as a *target* for an unpacking theory rather than a mechanism: its two weights are internal, and best read as outputs of an unpacking, not objects upstairs; unifying them with Kerr's displacement is posed as an open problem (§X.0). Black-hole physics already has a budget of this kind: the horizon radii multiply to $a^2+Q^2$, and the Christodoulou–Ruffini formula splits mass into an irreducible, area-fixed part and extractable charge and spin energy. The rapidity, though, holds $\delta$ fixed rather than the area — unpacking charge that way shrinks the horizon, so it can be a change of rendering but not a physical process (§XIII.5). The branch's one-sentence thesis, the family of renderings, and a rule against using the dipole to build an axis are added.
>
> **What is new in version 17.3 — the corrections asked for before a conceptual freeze, and one construction.** The shell's circle is identified with the spinor's overall phase through the Hopf bundle's principal *right* action, not the spin group's left action (§I.6). Maps from substrate to rendering are typed branchwise throughout, with the optical connection living on the correspondence itself (§XIII.2). Curvature odd under the plane reflection makes the plane's connection *flat*, which is not yet trivial holonomy on the projective plane; and the descended reflection also fixes an isolated point on the normal axis (§XIII.9). Only *generic* distinct light paths are too far apart in time to interfere (§XIII.8). The charge branch's rapidity is now a *chiral weight rapidity*, acting on gauge-invariant weights rather than on spinor representatives (§XIII.5). The lunar bound is tagged conditional, and the density convention and the Grassmann sentence are corrected. And one construction: **a smooth local model of $E$ near each meridian**, whose circle action is free away from the meridian, whose quotient restores the meridian, and which produces Structure III's unit Hopf class on every linking sphere (§II.2).
>
> **What is new in version 17.2 — two refinements to the branch.** §XIII.9 adds a gravitational firewall and prospect holonomy. The firewall: centrades carry localization, propagation carries relations, and centrade-free sectors would carry no *autonomous* Weyl datum. The stronger rule $C_{abcd} = 0$ fails, since the Bianchi identity sources Weyl curvature from stress gradients, and electromagnetic plane waves are conformally flat only when their profile is isotropic; the weaker rule needs a local split of Weyl curvature that general relativity does not supply. Prospect holonomy moves the normal-direction effect out of optics and into the lift, since gravity along the normal is measured. No flat bundle can close in the plane yet fail along the normal, but curvature odd under the plane reflection makes the plane's connection flat while opening loops along the normal — the geometric shape of a mechanism, with its free choices named.
>
> **What is new in version 17.1 — corrections before a freeze.** A referee's review of version 17 is applied throughout. Coherence is governed by what the detector and environment can distinguish, not by literal identity of endpoints, so phase holonomy is the cleanest carrier of the optical proposal but not the only one (§XIII.3). The incidence correspondence becomes the global primitive of canonical optics, with the old optical map surviving branch by branch (§XIII.2). The "veering normal" is repaired as a canonical line field (§XIII.8). The relative-modulus gauge trap resolves in one direction: in any construction that reproduces Reissner–Nordström, the rapidity cannot be the spinor pair's gauge freedom, and the one rescaling gravity cannot see is the swap $Q\to-Q$ (§X.0). Darkness gains three candidate routes — non-unitary leakage, unitary mode conversion, and *closure obstruction*, in which no rendered endpoint satisfies both legs at once — and none is yet derived (§XIII.8). The involution claims are qualified, the dipole coincidence keeps its geometry but loses its probability, and the complexification of $E$ and the regular locus of the Hopf bundle are stated consistently (§0.4, §II.2).
>
> **What is new in version 17 — a branch, on a frozen architecture.** Version 16's architecture is frozen as Parts 0–XII. Part XIII opens a new branch from the author's newer intuitions: canonical optics, in which an observed event is rigid downstairs while its canonical realizations are flexible upstairs; agreeing structure, in which one underlying point can appear twice; centrades, in which fields are unfinished reductions; and many rendered spacetimes. Its sharpest findings are constraints. Inside standard quantum mechanics coherence is governed by what the detector and environment can distinguish, and a *phase*-type holonomy is the cleanest carrier of the optical proposal (§XIII.3). Rendering one point twice needs a correspondence, not a map (§XIII.4). And neither simple reading of the orbital flip turns a symmetric disk into spirals, although a handedness flip freezes each rosette into a stationary spiral segment (§XIII.7). §0.0 now lists these intuitions beside the older ones, and §X.0's complexified-meridian modulus is refined toward a chiral weight rapidity (§XIII.5). §XIII.8 then tests whether the latest intuitions form one structure: **not yet** — no single literal involution plays all the roles, though time reversal and the plane reflection are genuinely shared; a pure phase or sign cannot dim a single ray, leaving three candidate routes to darkness, none derived; and the in-plane part of our motion relative to the cosmic background lies $3.4^\circ$ from reversed solar motion, a numerical clue whose significance is open.
>
> **What is new in version 16 — a draft branch, not a new architecture.** Parts 0–IX are version 15.7 text with **one architectural exception**, flagged where it occurs: the arena scale $\ell$ is now galactic, and the model deliberately has no cosmological slot (see the note on scale); other galaxies are moved to a detached wing (Part XII). A new Part X tests a proposal: that Reissner–Nordström is the small-charge face of a projective-gravity effect, with extremal holes — observationally poorly sampled — where the effect is fully exposed. **The proposed mechanism fails on the seed's own terms**: displacement along the meridian is exactly what gravity cannot see (§X.4). **But the intuition lands twice** — §X.0 reconstructs it in plain terms. Exactly — in asymptotically flat Einstein–Maxwell, which is the arena's local comparison limit (§X.1) — in this theory's variables Reissner–Nordström satisfies $N\Omega = 1-(M^2-Q^2)/4r^2$, so *extremality is lapse–fiber balance*, and the relation §III.2 retired is precisely the extremal sector (§X.2). And in another form: Reissner–Nordström's own Einstein–Rosen bridge carries a free antipodal twin map whose quotient is an $\mathbb{RP}^3$ geon, and that twin recedes to infinite distance as $Q\to M$ (§X.3). And the proposal's *two halves* are exact after all — as a split in mass rather than position: $\Omega = U_+U_-$ with $m_\pm = (M\pm Q)/2$, so that **Reissner–Nordström subextremality becomes the quadratic positivity condition $m_+m_-\geq0$**, and a naked singularity sits at the zero of a negative-weight factor (§X.7). Parametrized by the angle $m$ with $Q/M = \cos m$, *the subextremal domain is exactly that angle's real range* (§X.9). Made exact, the formula is a single-monopole Reissner–Nordström bridge in standard Einstein–Maxwell, with Kerr–Newman's extremality *sphere* $Q^2+a^2+\delta^2 = M^2$ as its spinning form — but on a conformal three-sphere of radius $\delta/4$, not on this theory's closed base, where the problem is nonlinear and open (§X.10). Read as a rapidity, the imaginary meridian coordinate supplies the angle, its real range covering exactly the subextremal domain, and $(M,Q)$ is represented on a timelike cone whose light-cone components are the factor weights (§X.11). **The branch is graded at §X.12** into exact facts, parameterizations, parallels and hypotheses, and handing back a three-way fork and one sharp question — *does the global projective quotient enlarge $U(1)$ to $O(2)$?* — which §X.13 answers: the quotient does not itself enlarge $U(1)$; only a descended *isolated* charge would require twisted descent and $O(2)$, and neither way of making charge descend survives. **Within the summed-source architecture, lunar laser ranging bounds duplicated electromagnetic energy at the twin above by about $5\times10^{-11}$ — $\eta\lesssim5\times10^{-11}$** (§X.13). Part X is frozen here; the next branch is the optical question at the head of §XI.2.
>
> **What this document is.** A complete, self-contained statement of the theory in Parts 0–XI, which assume no earlier document: every term is defined in Part 0, every law is stated with its equation, and every identity imported from an earlier version is restated in full rather than cited. Part XII is a detached exploratory appendix; it imports the few definitions it needs rather than citing the notes where they began. Part XIII is the version-17 branch, structural and open throughout.
>
> **The architectural change.** Versions 6 through 14 posited a space and then decorated it with a fiber. Version 15 inverts the order. The **canonical space** $E$ is the axiom; the middle space is *derived* from it by quotienting a circle; and the middle space then has **two** four-dimensional readouts, which earlier versions ran together — one where matter and light are localized, one where gravity acts.
>
> **What the fiber now is.** A shell of fixed nonzero radius about a *complex* source worldline is not a real 2-sphere: in the complexified normal space it is the affine quadric $Q^2_{\rm aff}$, a complex surface. And that surface is a homogeneous space of the complex spin group,
> $$\mathbb{C}^\times\ \longrightarrow\ SL(2,\mathbb{C})\ \longrightarrow\ Q^2_{\rm aff}\cong SL(2,\mathbb{C})/\mathbb{C}^\times$$
> whose compact real form is the ordinary Hopf fibration $U(1)\to SU(2)\simeq S^3\to S^2$. **So the Hopf character is no longer decoration. It is the compact real form of the complex spin geometry belonging to an isotropic shell**, and it was the right guess for the wrong reason in every version that assumed it.
>
> **The centrepiece.**
> $$\boxed{\ \text{spin geometry fixes the fiber's topology;}\qquad\text{gravity fixes only its metric length.}\ }$$
> These are different kinds of information. The spin structure says what the fiber **is** — $U(1)\hookrightarrow SU(2)\simeq S^3\to S^2$, the **unit Hopf class** — and Law I says how large that already-existing circle is at each base point, $L_H = \mathcal{F}[g_G]$. Changing $L_H$ does not change the Chern class or make it a different bundle. One fixed spin-derived Hopf bundle, whose fibers stretch and contract with the gravitational geometry.
>
> **What this version retires.** Because the fiber is now *derived*, its Chern class is **fixed at the unit Hopf class** — not a free integer. That contradicts the inherited second-order sector, in which $c_1 = M_-/m_{\rm unit}$ was mass-dependent with a free parameter. **The whole flux branch therefore goes** (§XI.1): the quantum $m_{\rm unit}$, the quantization fork, mass-proportional winding counts, the Reissner–Nordström-like charge $P$ and its Mercury bound, and the candidate source law $dF_H = 2\pi\star J_-$. This is not a loss of content but the removal of an inconsistency — and it takes two free parameters with it.
>
> **The seed, in one line.**
> > Light resolves a representative in $B_{\rm rep}$; gravity resolves only its meridian class in $B_{\rm grav}$.
>
> **Tags.** **[T]** standard or cited · **[V]** verified by computation here · **[D]** derived from the adopted definitions · **[S]** structural proposal · **[O]** open · **[R]** retired, with reason.

---

# PART 0 — VOCABULARY AND THE HIERARCHY

## 0.0 The leading intuitions [D/S/O]

*The theory grew from a handful of intuitions. The formal parts sometimes carry an intuition further than its first statement, sometimes find a different form for it, and sometimes retire it. This section states each one plainly, then says what the mathematics made of it and where.*

**1. Light resolves a representative; gravity resolves a class.** The seed. A point of space can appear twice to matter and light — as itself and as its twin — while gravity sees only the pair. *Made into:* two bases, the representational one where matter and light live and the gravity base that sums over each pair (Law I); and, in its cleanest form, the twin theorem of §IV.1, where one gravitational source on the quotient simply *is* a same-sign pair on the cover. *Status:* the architecture.

**2. The other side of the quotient is dark.** The twin carries its source's gravity but none of its matter, and so none of its light — a stand-in for dark matter. *Made into:* the barren twin, dark because it is barren, not because it is remote (§V.3), with its electromagnetic barrenness bounded by lunar ranging (§X.13). *Status:* the barren-twin realization is available and consistent, but barrenness itself remains unforced (§V.4); its electromagnetic side is bounded under stated assumptions.

**3. Change the shells, not the tangent spaces.** Rather than add structure to spacetime's tangent spaces, let the shells around each source carry an extra circle — the first gesture toward the Hopf fiber — and let a rotating hole be a source tilted against that grain. *Made into:* the complex spin lift of Part I, where the shell's circle acts on normalized spinor representatives exactly as their overall phase (§I.6), and the complex displacement of Part VI, which captures Kerr's complex structure though not, alone, all of Kerr. *Status:* verified for the phase; partial for Kerr; in bundle form the fiber's holonomy acquires a size, $\Delta s_H = (L_H/2\pi)\Delta\chi$ (§IX.1).

**4. Time as what is not quotiented.** Time might be the one direction of the deeper structure that is never quotiented away. *Made into:* three attempted routes, none successful. *Status:* open; time is still an input (§XI.2).

**5. A galaxy fills one hemisphere of a small closed universe.** A galaxy occupies one hemisphere of a closed three-sphere and its dark twin the other, and an exponential falloff of mass from one point makes the rotation curve flatten — flat curves as a closure effect rather than a halo. *Made into:* the galactic arena, one galaxy with no cosmological slot (the note on scale), and the plateau $v^2 = 2.994\,GM_{\rm grav}/\ell$ (§IV.8). *Status:* the plateau is verified; what supplies the arena's closure background, and how light sees past its distance cap, are the two open structural problems (§XI.2).

**6. Charge as a signed imbalance.** Reissner–Nordström might be the small-charge face of a projective effect: two chiral pieces whose balance is Schwarzschild and whose full imbalance is an extremal hole. *Made into:* the factor-weight picture of §X.0 — mass the sum of two weights, charge their signed difference, gravity blind to the sign. *Status:* exact as the algebra of Reissner–Nordström; the readout hypothesis is open. The factorization is a target, not a mechanism, and its rapidity holds $\delta$ fixed rather than the horizon area (§XIII.5).

**7. Other galaxies through apparent scale.** A galaxy's apparent size may differ from its actual size, and the mismatch may be what produces Tully–Fisher's square root. *Made into:* a detached wing, scoped but deliberately not attached (Part XII). *Status:* exploratory.

**8. The observed event is rigid downstairs; its canonical realizations are flexible upstairs.** Two canonical alternatives remain one observed event when their positions project to exactly the same point and their directions agree to within the resolution of whatever physical channel could record a difference — a camera being only an example of such a channel. *Made into:* Part XIII's canonical optics — two agreement rules, a readout map, and a connection whose holonomy separates the canonical endpoints of one observed event. *Status:* definitions and constraints; coherence is governed by what the detector and environment can distinguish, with phase holonomy the cleanest carrier (§XIII.3).

**9. One thing can appear twice.** One underlying point may show up at two places in an output space, each appearance keeping a different part of a common structure — like a logo with different parts quotiented away, read as two logos. Nobody would have noticed, because each appearance keeps something the other lacks. *Made into:* rendering as a correspondence, and agreeing structure as what both appearances retain (§XIII.4); the seed is its first instance. *Status:* definitions.

**10. Fields are what failed to unpack.** A *centrade* can be packed and unpacked: fully unpacked in the Kerr direction it has no angular momentum, in the Reissner–Nordström direction no charge. Unpackings conflict, "whack-a-mole", so there is a budget. *Made into:* admissible unpackings as a non-product set, and fields as residual obstructions, with standard precedents (§XIII.5). *Status:* conjecture, with a verified miniature in the Hopf-sign budget of §V.4.

**11. Our spacetime is one rendering among many.** Each global unpacking scheme outputs its own visual spacetime; ours is privileged by the sheer number of microstates and the relational complexity it supports, not by the laws. A hexagonal sheet of richly linked nodes, each carrying an infinite single-file tower, would hide the towers from its inhabitants. *Made into:* a renderer, multiplicity, and relational complexity as the criterion of visibility (§XIII.6). *Status:* conjecture; what stays invariant across renderings is open. The motivation is Machian — the galaxy's frame dominates because the galaxy holds far more centrades — but the statement concerns renderings, not a preferred frame inside one spacetime (§XIII.0).

**12. The galactic plane is a fixed set, and spirals are rendered.** The stress-energy upstairs may be roughly isotropic, with the galactic plane the fixed set of an involution; and a star at a turning point of its orbit might be rendered on the opposite side, making rosettes look like spirals. *Made into:* the involution fixing a great two-sphere, and the two readings of the orbital flip, computed (§XIII.7). *Status:* the fixed set is standard; neither simple flip makes spirals from a symmetric disk, though a handedness flip freezes each rosette into a stationary spiral segment, and the author's own cut-and-flip gives two point-symmetric spirals, trailing when the outgoing halves are flipped (§XIII.7).

**13. Our motion may be simpler upstairs than it looks.** Stars may share a simpler canonical motion, with its normal part hidden and what we see a readout of the rest — and the in-plane part of our velocity relative to the cosmic background lies almost exactly opposite our orbital motion. *Made into:* the readout package and the dipole clue (§XIII.8). *Status:* a clean formalism and a numerical clue; the test for one unifying structure fails in its strong form, with two shared pieces surviving.

**14. Centres gravitate as centres; relations between them do not.** Between the centrade that emits a photon and the centrade that absorbs it, the propagation may carry no gravitational identity of its own, and a charged hole's metric and Coulomb field may be two readouts of one centre. *Made into:* the gravitational firewall and the two-readout picture (§XIII.9). *Status:* conjecture. Imposing zero Weyl curvature fails, and the weaker rule needs a local split of Weyl curvature that general relativity does not supply.

**15. Far out along the normal, a lift stops closing.** Gravity above the disk is real, so what changes far along the normal is not gravity but how visual representatives of a gravitational class glue together. *Made into:* prospect holonomy (§XIII.9). *Status:* no flat bundle can do it, but curvature odd under the plane reflection makes the plane's connection flat and opens loops along the normal — the shape of a mechanism, not yet derived; exact closure of every planar loop also needs trivial holonomy on the projective plane.

**16. A photon's two legs may separate where the geometry allows.** The isolated ray is only the generic limit; where the geometry makes the endpoints conjugate, a photon's stationary history may be a whole class with the same observed ends — the author's picture of a pair that opens. *Made into:* the Jacobi-kernel gate, with the round arena's release points at $L = n\pi\ell$ and the first at the twin (§XIII.10). *Status:* standard theory already sums stationary rays, so the new content can only lie in the readout; within the solar system the gate stays shut; no observable difference is yet derived.

**17. Other galaxies may be recurrent canonical history classes of the one arena.** What an observer calls an external galaxy might be a returning two-path photon history of the single arena, rendered at an apparent distance with a redshift, rather than a second arena far away. *Made into:* Part XIV — the antipodal family broken by secondaries, a return map, a unitary spectral-dilation readout. *Status:* provisional throughout; the self-return requirement fixes the price, the first-order member count is not a galaxy count, and the branch's sharpest test is an invariant placing the observer's own return in the readout kernel.

**18. The Galaxy's own well may set the Hubble scale.** The dimensionless depth of the Galaxy's well on the arena scale, $GM/c^2\ell$, may be the dimensionless redshift of one arena radius, $H_0\ell/c$. *Made into:* the well-Hubble calibration of §XIV.6, with its coefficient to be derived. *Status:* the literal hemisphere flux vanishes by symmetry and the depth is packing-dominated; the surface through which the well would act is not yet defined.

**19. Light and gravitational waves must not be able to tell which of them is objective.** If a galaxy's image is a two-history object for light, uniqueness of that galaxy under both messengers demands the same structure for gravitational waves, with no mechanism privileging either — a no-privileging principle in the spirit of general covariance. *Made into:* the Universal Null-History Principle (§XV.0), exact at leading eikonal order, with $\eta_{\rm EM} = \eta_{\rm GW}$ as its test. *Status:* adopted; anchored by GW170817; standard footholds real.

**20. Bake it into spacetime: the middle space as a combination of two deeper structures.** Rather than append readouts, let the middle geometry emerge as the symmetric resolution of two deeper transports, designed so that the dual gravitational-wave picture lives there and is wired to the electromagnetic one — a substrate-deepening move that might make the Hubble behaviour emergent. *Made into:* the bi-connection of §XV.1, adopted by decision, whose scale character is a homothety complementary to the Jacobi squeeze (§XV.2), the real-loop form superseded in 20.2 by the conjugate lifts; the fiber-product form of $\mathcal M$ deferred (§XV.5). *Status:* the structure is verified; its sign rule, dynamics and universality are open.

**21. The two Born paths and the two connections are one thing.** The two paths a photon always requires and the two connections of the substrate would be manifestations of one representation-independent structure — the gravitational analogue of the quantum pair — with a gauge-like freedom to offload a system's defining constraints from the quantum pair onto the Weyl decomposition and back. *Made into:* the closed-time-path dictionary, one complex relative holonomy with phase and scale as its parts, and the offloading theorem (§XV.6). *Status:* the dictionary and the theorem are standard mathematics assembled; that the theory's three $\mathbb C^\times$'s are one is the deepest open conjecture, and the exchange of the bi-connection turns out to be the compact real structure acting on $\mathbb C^\times$, which derives the firewall; the Hopf connection then analytically determines its own noncompact partner, and a class's scale character is a Hopf flux (§XV.7, §XV.3).

**22. One sky point, many galaxies.** Index versions of visual space by the dual-path degrees of freedom beyond the arrival direction and the source point; then constraining a rendering by "this exact point is included" can paint several different galaxies through it, the object swept out as the direction tilts depending on the relational data. *Made into:* the Visual-Sheet Principle (§XVI.0) and the fiber experiment (§XVI.3). *Status:* adopted; the phenomenon needs several fiber states lying on distinct continuable branches with distinct images — several states alone are only necessary — and whether that occurs is a computation on the broken family.

**23. The privileged normal as an equilibrium, killed off the plane.** A galaxy's normal may be less a $\cos(\text{angle})$ than a stationary condition met in the plane and lost off it by a phase incompatibility — with real off-plane density falling off exponentially as wavefunctions do in forbidden regions. *Made into:* off-sheet suppression by complex saddles (§XVI.5), the only version of rendering-made disks left after shear fails (§XVI.2). *Status:* a mechanism class, not a derivation; the isotropic-ball reading is excluded by local dynamics (§XVI.7).

**24. The galaxy as a fixed point of a duality.** The off-plane structure may be a duality in its own right, interlocking with the complexified geometry, with the galaxy — or its plane — as its fixed point. *Made into:* the lift of the plane reflection to the Hopf bundle — an anti-equivariant, fiber-inverting lift forced by the Chern class, antilinear in its real-slice expression — commuting with the real structure and agreeing with it on the normal history mode; the plane as the intersection of two real forms; the normal as the odd Hessian mode (§XVI.8). *Status:* derived on the local model; it gives Part XIII's assumed odd parity a canonical enforcer, conditional on the connection's equivariance under the lift, and imposes two parity kill tests on Part XV's redshift sector, of which the meridian mode fails the first and the normal mode may survive.

**25. Two equally intrinsic types of reality.** The complexified canonical geometry may be primary because it supports more than one real form: one compact real structure, where matter lives, and an equally intrinsic split real-form *type*, occurring as an isotropic family whose member is solution-selected, with the scale transport on the selected split form and the two agreeing on the selected plane. *Made into:* Part XVII, with the diamond, the two restrictions of one connection, the dual Lorentzian shell, and the reflection theorem selecting the surviving sector. *Status:* an experiment in architecture; derives more than it assumes locally; does not yet earn modification of the core.

**26. Alignment exposes a projective opening direction.** The author's original form — that nearly aligned paths can reorient the direction between them far more sharply than distant ones, so that the same common-real intersection that fixes the Galactic plane at full scale might select each Kerr black hole's equatorial plane in the path-aligned regime — motivated the branch, and the calculation narrowed it: small separation makes the direction ill-conditioned, actual flexibility needs a small Jacobi gap, and the Kerr near zone, computed, has a large one — the theory correcting its author. *Made into:* the projective opening direction $[Y]$, a family $\sigma_{[N]}$ of second real structures with solution-selected members, and the Kerr bridge — the Newman–Janis displacement is a displacement into the split real form (§XVII.9). *Status:* the family is a conjugacy orbit derived from the shell symmetry and the Kerr reality is verified on the local model; the freedom of the direction is the Jacobi nullity, not smallness; the selection of $[N]$ by the history geometry is derived at the level of symmetry and confirmed numerically for the Galaxy, with the strong-field Kerr near zone now computed: the spin twists kernels by at most about $20°$, and the Kerr axis is reconstructed from the spin-selected axial symmetry, not recovered from the local spin-induced twisting of an otherwise isotropic history family (§XVII.10).

**27. Repacking within a budget, by one conformal transformation.** Switching among selections and redistributing a body's charge, spin and nonextremality could both be operations of one conformal transformation on the pre-quotient space, tuning the agreeing structure in the quotient while preserving the gravity-base metric that controls the fiber lengths. *Made into:* the repacking transformation and its invariance condition (§XVIII.6), the budget corrected to a simplex (§XVIII.1). *Status:* the fork is real — Law I makes a hole's parameters gravitationally visible — and the guaranteed budget is between a source and its twin; between two bodies the static sector's constraint is same-sign joint extremality with no continuous compensation, and degree $-1$ throat and twin identifications invert the Hopf fiber while the Couch–Torrence pairing forces no inversion (§XVIII.3, §XVIII.5).

**28. Extremality as the idealized case; nonextremality as a gained irreducibility.** The extremal black hole, with its traceless optical Ricci tensor, may be profoundly idealized, and tilting out of extremality — losing the neat Laplacian condition — may be the beefing-up of a connection. *Made into:* $R_{\rm opt} = -6\delta^2/r^4$, and the Poisson equation for the optical conformal factor whose source is the nonextremality, harmonic at extremality (§XVIII.2). *Status:* exact; its reading as a connection's curvature open.

**29. Boltzmannian space.** If each repacking is a microstate, the Galactic frame is privileged by conditioning on the vastly larger macrostate of the Galaxy's bulk, and a black hole's fixed parameters are a remnant of that conditioning. *Made into:* the microstate space without gauge copies, the coarse-graining and the entropy (§XVIII.8). *Status:* a construction awaiting a derived measure; the collective normal of §XVII.10 its first shape.

**30. Three complex amplitudes before three physical roles.** A black hole's spin, charge and nonextremality may be three abstract channels of a deeper centrade, with the rendering choosing which appears as which — perhaps three because space has three dimensions. *Made into:* the triality conjecture with its firewall, the abstract budget operator, representation type as what the rendering assigns, and the $\mathbb Z_2$ step (Part XIX). *Status:* all three readouts share a fall-off and differ in representation type, so any symmetry is pre-rendering; the spectral numerology fails for spherical symmetry; an unlabelled object with a real $S_3$ action is the whole question.

**31. Reuse the flavor structure.** Nature reuses its structures, so unification should follow them: as the two connections came from the two Born paths, the channel space should come from lepton flavor — complex, not real — with flavor's interplay with phase imported as this structure's interplay with the theory's phase, and the complex data either kept in the complexified-spacetime slot or quotiented away in the output. *Made into:* the channel space $\mathbb C^3\supset S^5\to\mathbb{CP}^2$, the Kerr–Newman weights as phase-blind intensities, $S_3$ as the Weyl group, the mixing-matrix rendering by which relative phase repacks the budget, the Hopf phase as the common phase, and the two-imaginary-units firewall (§XIX.2). *Status:* the structure is standard and verified, and the theory canonically contains the carrier — the shell's ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2$, weight two under the Hopf phase, twin-even, the complexified 3-space; promoting states in that carrier to channel states, and identifying renderings with real frames, remain the hypotheses. Under them the polarization classifies exactly which budgets a centrade can wear, and single-spinor states, being null, are excluded by observation.

**32. Use Kerr's own construction as the instance.** No internal direction may be attached to the canonical space by hand; the sanity check is that the way Kerr is built must itself instantiate the general principle. And the microstate picture was always this: a new gravity base for each render, constrained so that the base keeps controlling the middle space's curvature and fiber lengths. *Made into:* §XIX.2b — the Kerr–Newman source as the complex axial datum $\zeta = \delta+ia$, rod half-length real and Newman–Janis displacement imaginary, with spacetime's own imaginary unit and $p = 1$; and the locking law as the invariant of repacking (§XIX.1). *Status:* the instance is exact, and Law I's own Kinnersley symmetry explains the budget — charging is a boost of the split pair $(M,Q)$ that leaves the compact pair $\zeta$ untouched, so $M^2-Q^2 = |\zeta|^2$ (§XIX.2c); the soldering that makes the instance canonical is the theorem target.

**33. A vanishing separation may leave a spin-state slot.** The magnitude separating two equipped histories can vanish while a projective direction survives; perhaps for massive matter the resolved degree of freedom is not another spacetime direction but the slot in which a spin state lives. The cosmos does not choose the electron's spin: the canonical geometry supplies the slot, local preparation and dynamics choose the state. If the slot is real it must lift through the theory's Hopf $U(1)$ to $\mathbb{CP}^1$, reproduce the half-solid-angle Berry phase and the $2\pi$ spinor sign, and extend by existing Lorentz and spin indices to the Dirac kinematics without adding coordinates. *Made into:* Part XX. *Status:* the theory already contains exactly the right local Hopf and spin geometry — the spin-group identification and its Hopf–Bloch bundle are Part I's own — and the complete free Dirac plane-wave spinor data, both mode families, with the internal discrete-symmetry intertwiners, follow from the lift and the theory's two spinor structures; and the connection constructed on the spin bundle over the mass shell reproduces Bargmann–Michel–Telegdi for every $g$ with nothing added, while a counterfactual meridian-Kaluza–Klein transport is excluded as spin dynamics, its $g = 1$ half of Dirac's; the candidate slot exists along every massive history; the canonical connection is Berry's, constraining its deformations; the conjugate structure gives the orthogonal partner; the local antipodal sign is the $2\pi$ rotation while its identification with the global twin is open — and proving that this geometry is the state bundle of physical matter is what remains.

**34. From kinematics to matter.** The reconstructed spinor kinematics is not yet an electron; the electron needs a local field on representational spacetime, an action, statistics and a vacuum, and none may be appended by fiat. *Made into:* Part XXI — the two bundles kept apart, the spacetime spinor bundle topologically unambiguous on the cover with its global gluing open, the twin sign as a choice of descent to the gravity base, the action and statistics as the open half. *Status:* topology settled, dynamics open.

**35. What spin becomes when the legs separate.** In the aligned-legs regime a pair's separation has only a direction; when the legs genuinely separate it acquires a magnitude. Perhaps what the coincident regime reads as quantum spin is repurposed in the resolved regime while remaining rooted in the same object — the projective residue of a relational degree of freedom whose radial part becomes physical only when the histories resolve. *Made into:* §XX.12, a question with a theorem target — one state space over $[0,\infty)$ with $\mathbb{CP}^1$ as its $r = 0$ boundary and a connection interpolating Berry transport and the comparative dual-history transport — and its kill tests. *Status:* speculative; the conservative alternative, spin as a local state at all separations, is equally open.

**36. A transformation that cycles the lepton family.** Perhaps a transformation of the whole rendered universe cycles through the charged-lepton family, and the Koide structure of their masses is geometry rather than a numerical relation. *Made into:* §XXI.4 — the mass eigenvalue as a product of two dual factors a quarter turn apart, $m_n = m_*(\cos\theta_n+1/\sqrt2)^2$, so that Koide's $2/3$ follows from the $\mathbb Z_3$ identities; the operator form on the theory's own spin-one carrier with the weight states of the selected axis as sectors; each factor twice a Born overlap; the cycle as rendering covariance, $CM(\theta)C^{-1} = M(\theta+2\pi/3)$. *Status:* exact mathematics, available pieces, nothing forced: the quarter turn's use is a postulate, the cycle is not a shell operation, and only one relation is explained — but the three has a structural candidate: the carrier is the regular representation of a cyclic group only for order three, the three being the dimension of the rest space, with the occupancy premise the open theorem (§XXI.4a); and the native conjugation exchanges the two transverse sectors covariantly with the axial sector, the $\tau$'s candidate, fixed. The descent exists conditional on the reduction (§XXI.4b), and the reduction has a candidate origin in the split real-form selection every rendering makes (§XXI.4d): the generation space is the multiplicity space of the selected axis's stabilizer on the native carrier — three weights, Lorentz-scalar, sector-preserving under the canonical transport with a diagonal holonomy when the axis moves (§XXI.4c) — so the three is $\dim H_u$ without any $\mathbb Z_3$, the third turn entering only as the equal spacing of the Koide phases, which is channel-before-role applied to the sector phases.

**37.
