**Opened by the draft branch of Part X [O].** (a) *Charge without charge* on $S^4\setminus\Gamma$ (§X.5): whether Maxwell extends source-free to the complement of the meridians, with charges as linking-sphere periods — Gauss's law on the closed base would then be automatic, but for a single meridian it reopens the twin charge retracted in version 6. (b) Whether a black hole's **bridge-twin** and its **arena twin** can be identified (§X.3) — topologically inviting, geometrically unsupported. (c) Whether the extremal sector admits any deviation from Reissner–Nordström that neither resurrects the retired charge $P$ nor alters Law I (§X.6). (d) **Whether every hole must carry its bridge-twin** (§X.7) — the principle that would turn the subextremality condition into an exclusion mechanism; whether extremality would then be dynamically unattainable is a further question. (e) **What the two factors are** — the sharpened target of §X.0: ordered chiral data read symmetrically by gravity and antisymmetrically by electromagnetism, giving $M = m_++m_-$, $Q = m_+-m_-$, $\delta^2 = 4m_+m_-$. Can chiral composition, as in Kim's construction, produce exactly that algebra (§X.0, §X.7)? (e′) *Answered* (§X.13): the quotient does not itself enlarge $U(1)$; only a descended isolated charge would need $O(2)$, and neither way of making it descend survives. What remains open is the hole-specific form: whether charged black holes carry a conjugate mouth at the twin, with effective charge $\sqrt2\,Q$ and extremality at $|Q| = M/\sqrt2$. (f) **Whether a chiral weight rapidity exists** — once the two weights are constructed as gauge-invariant data of the chiral solutions — **and a canonical $\mathbb{C}^\times$ modulus parameterizes it**, acting on the weights as $(m_+,m_-)\mapsto(e^\beta m_+,e^{-\beta}m_-)$ — the form in which the complexified meridian's *modulus*, rather than an imaginary displacement, would carry charge. If it does, the rapidity of §X.11 becomes a transformation of the canonical geometry (§X.0, §X.8). Since the reciprocal rescaling changes the mass, it cannot be the projective gauge freedom in any construction reproducing Reissner–Nordström; the target is to show that the chiral constituents' weights are separately invariant, so that the rapidity is a physical deformation between holes. (g) **An exact charged hole in the Einstein static universe** (§X.10): the nonlinear Lichnerowicz constraint on the unit sphere, $-8\tilde\Delta\phi+6\phi = 6\phi^5+2\tilde E^2\phi^{-3}$, where $\tilde E$ is the divergence-free *conformal* electric datum and the physical field is $E^i = \phi^{-6}\tilde E^i$. The conformal weights must be fixed before any numerics: written with the physical $E$, the equation is not closed. With throat boundary conditions and twin symmetry. The harmonic three-sphere cannot reach it; a numerical solution is the natural next computation.

**The constant count.** The theory's explicit constants are now just $G$ and the arena radius $\ell$ — galactic in the intended reading — with $\Lambda = 1/\ell^2$ an internal constant of the arena rather than the observed cosmological constant, plus whatever the eventual Law II dynamics introduces. **The model has no cosmological parameter.** The arena-law constant $A$ of the detached wing (Part XII) is not a constant of the model; were that law ever adopted, $A$ would be a further universal parameter unless derived. **$m_{\rm unit}$ and $g_2$ went with the flux sector**, and the permeability function $\mu(\rho)$ went with the old Law II, whose equation this version no longer contains — it was an orphan in the previous draft and is removed (§XI.1).

---

## XI.3 Chiral composition: two bridges and two obstructions [T/V/O]

*Kim's result (§XI.2) suggests a bridge to the chiral sector. Two parts of it are established, two are blocked, and one is a stated theorem target. None is folded into the architecture.*

### Bridge 1 — the shell group is the diagonal of the chiral pair [T]

Complexified four-dimensional spin geometry factorizes, and the stabilizer of a non-null direction $u$ sits **diagonally** inside it:

$$Spin(4,\mathbb{C})\cong SL(2,\mathbb{C})_L\times SL(2,\mathbb{C})_R\ \supset\ \Delta SL(2,\mathbb{C}) = SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{\rm shell},\qquad g\mapsto(g,g)$$

whose compact form is the familiar $Spin(3)\cong SU(2)\hookrightarrow Spin(4)\cong SU(2)_L\times SU(2)_R$. **This is standard group theory, and it places the shell group of Part I rather than positing it.** The whole chain reads

$$Spin(4,\mathbb{C})\ \xrightarrow{\ \text{stabilize }u\ }\ SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{\rm diag}\ \xrightarrow{\ /\,\mathbb{C}^\times\ }\ Q^2_{\rm aff}\ \xrightarrow{\ \text{real form}\ }\ S^2$$

with dimensions $12\to6\to4\to2$ **[V]**. Given $u$, the rest of §I.2–§I.4 is much less arbitrary than it looked: **one shell spin group is the diagonal residue of two chiral spin groups.**

### Bridge 2 — self-dual Taub–NUT carries the same shell type [T]

In Gibbons–Hawking form $ds^2 = V^{-1}(d\tau+\omega)^2+V\,d\mathbf{x}^2$ with $\nabla\times\omega = \nabla V$, the potential $\omega$ is a Dirac monopole and each constant-radius three-surface is a circle bundle over the angular $S^2$ — for unit charge, the Hopf fibration $S^1\to S^3\to S^2$. **So the elementary chiral constituents of Kim's construction carry the same shell *type* as the canonical shells here.** More than a coincidence of names.

### Obstruction 1 — the same bundle, the reciprocal warp [V]

With $V = 1+2m/r$ and a dimensionless Hopf angle $\psi$, Taub–NUT's constant-$r$ slice is

$$g_r = Vr^2\,d\Omega_2^2 + 4m^2V^{-1}\big(d\psi+\cos\theta\,d\phi\big)^2$$

so with $b$ the horizontal scale and $c$ the Hopf-fiber scale, $b^2 = Vr^2$ and $c^2 = 4m^2V^{-1}$.

**(a) The warp factors are reciprocal.** The horizontal coefficient carries $V$; the vertical carries $V^{-1}$. Law I does the opposite of reciprocal — it warps base metric and fiber *alike*, both by $\Omega^2$. **That is the obstruction**, and it is stated in the warp factors deliberately: the physical radius of a fixed-coordinate shell also carries the factor $r$, and in Taub–NUT both physical scales in fact shrink toward the nut. *(An earlier table here showed the warp $V^{1/2}$ under a heading suggesting a physical length; withdrawn.)*

**(b) The shell is squashed everywhere except the nut.**

$$\frac cb = \frac{2m}{r+2m}$$

| $r/m$ | $V$ | $c/b$ |
|--:|--:|--:|
| 10 | 1.200 | 0.167 |
| 1 | 3.000 | 0.667 |
| 0.3 | 7.667 | 0.870 |
| 0.01 | 201.0 | 0.995 |

A **Berger sphere** for every $r>0$ — most squashed far away — and round only in the limit $r\to0$ **[V]**. *An earlier formula, $1/V^2r^2$, compared an unnormalized fiber coordinate with dimensionless angles, and its "round at $r = 1-2m$" added a pure number to a length. Retired (§XI.1).*

> **The shells agree as bundles and disagree as geometries.** So "derive the fiber-length locking inside the chiral Taub–NUT sector" is not available: done naively it gives the wrong sign *and* the wrong metric. The sharp question is instead **why the same Hopf topology should carry a reciprocal metric law** — and no answer should be adopted merely because it repairs the sign.

### Obstruction 2 — this branch cannot derive time [D]

The timelike direction $u$ is presupposed **three times over**: by $N_\gamma = \{\xi:g_\mathbb{C}(\xi,\dot\gamma) = 0\}$, which needs $\dot\gamma$; by Kim's stationary composition, which uses $u^\mu$; and by Bridge 1 itself, since *which* diagonal depends on which direction is stabilized.

> **A composition law defined on this shell architecture cannot derive $u$, because $u$ already defines the architecture on which it acts.** This closes the chiral route to time in the present version. Rebuilding $E$ *prior* to choosing a real form or a non-null direction, and showing a diagonal reduction emerges dynamically, would be a different and more foundational version — not a result available here.

### The theorem target — an equivariance test, stated without prejudging [O]

Kim's composition contains a bilinear $\ell^{\dot\alpha\alpha}\propto\tilde o_+^{\dot\alpha}o_-^{\alpha}$. Let the principal $U(1)_H$ act on the two principal-spinor lines through its induced, associated action — not as a subgroup of the spin group acting from the left (§I.6) — with weights $q_\pm$. Then the **necessary and sufficient local phase-invariance condition for that bilinear** is

$$\boxed{\ q_+ + q_- = 0\ }$$

verified: with $h_\theta = \operatorname{diag}(e^{i\theta},e^{-i\theta})$ acting diagonally, opposite-weight lines give an invariant bilinear and same-weight lines do not **[V]**. *Local, not global:* full descent of the composed field through $E\to\mathcal{M}$ needs more — compatible normalizations, patches, connections and gluing. The weight condition is the algebraic core of that theorem, not the whole of it.

*An earlier formulation of this test — "identify $U(1)_H$ with the anti-diagonal of $U(1)_L\times U(1)_R$" — is retired* **[R]**. It conflated two different objects: the **diagonal $SL(2,\mathbb{C})$**, which is a spacetime spin-group embedding, with an **anti-diagonal $\mathbb{C}^\times$**, which is the projective rescaling redundancy of a bilinear's two representatives. $U(1)_H$ lies in the diagonal, and the cancellation must come from *representation content*, not from relocating the subgroup by fiat. Stated as a weight condition, it is something to prove rather than to assume.

### What the branch is actually for [S]

Not time, and not the fiber-length locking. The natural job is

$$\star:\ \mathcal{D}_{\rm SD}\times\mathcal{D}_{\rm ASD}\ \longrightarrow\ \mathcal{D}_{\rm real}$$

— **how non-chiral gravitational geometry is assembled from chiral constituents** — and whether $\star$ lifts to $E$ and commutes with both reductions:

$$\pi_H(D_+\star_E D_-) \overset{?}{=} \pi_H(D_+)\star_\mathcal{M}\pi_H(D_-),\qquad q(D_+\star_\mathcal{M}D_-)\overset{?}{=} q(D_+)\star_G q(D_-)$$

If that diagram commutes, chiral composition would be intrinsic enough to survive **both** of this theory's information-losing maps. That is a better prize than another account of the fiber.

# PART XII — DETACHED WING: OTHER GALAXIES

> **Status: detached by design.** The model is a single galactic arena — ours — with no cosmological slot. This part is scoped but deliberately **not attached**: the aspiration to derive the Tully–Fisher relation for *other* galaxies. Its intended route is a later abstraction or reframing — an optical rule relating canonical histories to the sizes and fluxes an observer assigns — and **not** the construction of an arena metric for each galaxy, which is taken in advance to be a dead end. Nothing here is a claim of the model. The computations below were run once, as exploratory compatibility checks: they map what any future rule must deliver, and they are not evidence for one.
>
> **What any attachment must satisfy [D].** In the reading that survives (reading (i) below), the rule enlarges a galaxy's apparent size by up to about five times *without* enlarging its inferred mass. **Ordinary metric lensing cannot do that.** Geometric-optics lensing conserves surface brightness — Liouville's theorem: magnification scales flux and solid angle together, so apparent size grows as the square root of the magnification while inferred mass grows as the magnification itself, leaving inferred surface density unchanged. That is precisely the literal reading (ii), which the checks below exclude. And Etherington reciprocity, $D_L = (1+z)^2D_A$, holds whenever spacetime is metric, photons follow null geodesics and photon number is conserved. So an attached rule must say which of these premises it generalizes — the single optical metric, null-geodesic propagation, photon-number and phase-space conservation, or the map from canonical histories to detector observables. What this shows is that the rule cannot be ordinary magnification — not yet that it must break surface-brightness conservation. A readout that changes the inferred angular-diameter distance, or the assignment of physical radius, rather than magnifying angular area, might preserve measured specific intensity while changing inferred size. The observable map of §XI.2's item 0 will decide which premise, if any, has to fail. Either way it is a new optical law, not a lens, and it must still reproduce local GR, lensing, surface brightness as actually measured, and distance duality. It is the same construction §XI.2's item 0 asks for.
>
> **The acceptance test, if the wing is ever attached [D].** One optical map, one arena rule, at most one or two universal parameters, and **no per-galaxy freedom** — then predict all 175 SPARC rotation curves and their photometry. Until a rule of that kind exists, the wing stays detached.

## XII.1 The lemma and its two readings [V/S]

**The lemma.** The author's lemma has three parts: an *arena law*, $GM_{\rm gal} = A\,\ell^2$ with $A$ an acceleration scale (originally written $A = cH_0$); an *invariant*, $M/\ell^2$, compared against the peak surface density; and the **apparent-scale parameter**. Its formalized version is adopted here: *the actual $\ell$ tracks $\sqrt M$ universally, and the observed scatter in surface density — Freeman scatter, low versus high surface brightness — is an apparent factor from the reference-point setup*, so that "a physically $2\times$ galaxy at $0.5\times$ apparent scale registers the same." The square root comes from the arena law; the apparent scale's job is to make surface-density differences optical.

| part | result |
|:--|:--|
| **the arena law** | with the plateau $v^2 = 2C_{\rm plat}\,GM_{\rm gal}/\ell$ — writing $C_{\rm plat}$ for the plateau coefficient, to keep $c$ for the speed of light — it gives $v^4 = 4C_{\rm plat}^2A\,GM_{\rm gal}$ — **the baryonic Tully–Fisher law, slope exactly 4**, with no artifact needed **[V]** |
| **its normalization** | in the compact limit $C_{\rm plat} = 1.499$, matching $v^4 = GMa_0$ needs $A = a_0/(2\times1.499)^2$, so $cH_0/A\approx49$ and the arena radius is $\ell\approx7a$, with $a = \sqrt{GM/cH_0}$ the radius the arena law gives when $A = cH_0$. *An earlier "$\ell = \pi a$, within 10%" rested on its plateau coefficient $4/\pi$, which kept only the $4\pi G\bar\rho$ term of $v^2_{\rm eq} = 4\pi G\rho_{\rm comp}+3\Phi(\pi/2)$ and missed the larger second one — low by $2.35\times$ in $v^2$. With the verified $2.994$ the $\pi$ does not survive* **[V/R]**  Stated explicitly: **a galaxy's arena radius would have to be $\ell = 2\times1.499\sqrt{GM_{\rm gal}/a_0}\approx3.0\,r_M$**; setting $\ell$ equal to the MOND radius $r_M$ itself gives the right slope but $v^4\approx9\,GMa_0$ |
| **the invariant** | the arena law makes $M/\ell^2 = A/G$ the same for every galaxy — a universal surface density, $\approx96\,M_\odot$ pc$^{-2}$, on the scale that MOND's $\Sigma^\dagger = a_0/2\pi G\approx137\,M_\odot$ pc$^{-2}$ and Freeman's law both circle. For a thin exponential disk, $M = 2\pi\Sigma_0\ell^2k(h/\ell)$ with $k(x) = x^2(1+e^{-\pi/x})/(1+x^2)\approx x^2$, so **the ratio of the invariant to the central surface density is the rotation curve's shape parameter**: $(M/\ell^2)/\Sigma_0 = 2\pi k(h/\ell)$ **[V]** |
| **the apparent scale** | two readings, both traceable to the author's words. **(i) The formalized reading**, adopted: apparent scale inflates observed *size* while mass is measured without it, so observed surface density varies optically and actual surface density can be universal. **(ii) A literal reading of the original statement** — perceived mass growing as the square of the scale factor — inflates mass and area together, leaves surface density invariant, and would put the square root in the inflation rather than in the arena law. Reading (ii) competes with the arena law; reading (i) complements it. An earlier revision of this section used only (ii) **[R]** |

## XII.2 The surface-brightness test, with a thin disk [V]

**The test.** The plateau coefficient depends on $h/\ell$, and under the arena law $h/\ell$ is fixed by surface density — so the lemma predicts a surface-density dependence of the Tully–Fisher normalization, which the observed relation lacks: it has about $0.1$ dex of scatter and **no residual trend with surface brightness**. The size of the predicted trend depends on the galaxy's geometry. A spherical exponential profile gave offsets exceeding a decade. The realistic case is a **thin exponential disk on a great two-sphere of $S^3$**, solved here in Legendre modes and validated against Freeman's exact flat-space disk to $0.1\%$ near the center and against the monopole plateau in the compact limit:

| $h/\ell$ | thin disk $C_{\rm plat}$ | spherical profile $C_{\rm plat}$ |
|--:|--:|--:|
| 0.05 | 1.495 | 1.478 |
| 0.10 | 1.482 | 1.417 |
| 0.20 | 1.460 | 1.205 |
| 0.30 | 1.345 | 0.923 |
| 0.50 | 0.917 | 0.504 |
| 1.00 | 0.337 | 0.158 |

The flattened geometry holds the plateau far better. Under the arena law, normalized in the compact limit:

| $\Sigma_0$ ($M_\odot$ pc$^{-2}$) | $h/\ell$ | $C_{\rm plat}$ | offset in $\log M$ |
|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 10000 | 0.04 | 1.496 | 0.00 dex |
| 1000 | 0.12 | 1.477 | 0.01 dex |
| 300 | 0.23 | 1.424 | 0.04 dex |
| 100 | 0.42 | 1.081 | 0.28 dex |
| 50 | 0.66 | 0.662 | 0.71 dex |
| 30 | 0.98 | 0.357 | 1.25 dex |

> **High-surface-brightness galaxies pass; low-surface-brightness ones do not — and no arena law can fix it.** For a galaxy to sit on $v^4 = GMa_0$ at all, its arena radius must satisfy $C_{\rm plat}(x)^2k(x) = \Sigma^\dagger/4\Sigma_0$ with $x = h/\ell$. The left side has a **maximum**, $0.178$ at $h/\ell\approx0.43$: a disk spread too widely over the sphere loses its plateau. So a solution exists only for
> $$\Sigma_0\ \geq\ 1.41\,\Sigma^\dagger\ \approx\ 190\,M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-2}$$
> Below that, **no arena radius of any kind** puts a thin exponential disk on the observed relation **[V]**. That is not a defect of the $\sqrt M$ law but a ceiling on what the plateau can deliver — and it falls in exactly the wrong place. Low-surface-brightness galaxies, with $\Sigma_0\sim10$–$100\,M_\odot$ pc$^{-2}$, are the most dark-matter-dominated galaxies known: the plateau mechanism works where the discrepancy is smallest and fails where it is largest. The threshold sits at MOND's transition surface density, because it is set by the same constant; the physical difference is that MOND's asymptotics depend only on enclosed mass, while the plateau depends on how the mass is spread relative to $\ell$.

## XII.3 Exploratory checks [V/S]

**Reading (i) is compatible with the test — within a window [V/S].** Under reading (i), a low-surface-brightness galaxy is *actually* compact and only looks extended, so its true $h/\ell$ is small and it sits on the relation. But a disk's rotation curve has a shape, and apparent scale stretches radii without changing it:

| true $h/\ell$ | peak $v$ / plateau $v$ | shape |
|--:|--:|:--|
| 0.03 | 2.08 | peaked, then falling |
| 0.05 | 1.63 | peaked, then falling |
| 0.10 | 1.20 | peaked, then falling |
| 0.20 | 1.00 | rising to the plateau |
| 0.40 | 1.00 | rising to the plateau |

A disk that is actually *very* compact would show a peaked curve falling by up to a factor of two before flattening, which is not what low-surface-brightness galaxies show. But there is a window: at a true $h/\ell\approx0.2$ the curve rises to its plateau *and* the Tully–Fisher offset is only $0.02$ dex. So the formalization works **if every disk has actual $h/\ell$ near $0.2$ and observed surface-density differences are entirely optical**. Taken strictly, that would leave bulges as the only source of rotation-curve diversity, and every bulgeless disk would share one curve shape in units of $R/h_{\rm obs}$. The check below tests exactly that: it rejects the strict form — bulgeless disks still differ, and bulges can only raise inner curves — but finds the relaxed form compatible, with actual $h/\ell$ in a narrow band.

**An exploratory compatibility check on real rotation curves [V].** *Run once; each galaxy's true $h/\ell$ is fitted freely, so this checks compatibility, not the rescaling rule, and is not evidence for it.* Six bulgeless SPARC disks (NGC 2403, NGC 3198, NGC 0100, UGC 128, F563-1, DDO 154), observed central surface densities $408$ down to $12\,M_\odot$ pc$^{-2}$, compared as $V/V_{\rm flat}$ against $R/h$ so that distance and inclination errors cancel. **A strictly universal shape is rejected** — curves differ at fixed $R/h$ by about ten times their errors. **But a narrow band of true $h/\ell$, $0.20$–$0.32$, is compatible with every spiral** (rms misfits $0.02$–$0.09$), costs Tully–Fisher at most $0.11$ dex, and **does not track observed surface density**: the two faintest disks fit at $0.22$–$0.23$, like NGC 3198. The literal reading fits worse for every galaxy and places the faintest disks beyond the twin. Bulges cannot supply the variety — all six are bulgeless, and a bulge only raises the inner curve, while most departures are slower rises. Under reading (i) the actual central surface densities cluster at $\sim160$–$400\,M_\odot$ pc$^{-2}$, and the needed apparent-to-actual size factors run from $0.7$ to $5$. The gas-dominated DDO 154 needs its stars-plus-gas distribution modelled. The six-galaxy table follows.

| galaxy | observed $\Sigma_0$ | reading (ii): $h/\ell$ · misfit · TF offset | reading (i): best $h/\ell$ · misfit · TF offset |
|:--|--:|:--|:--|
| NGC 2403 | 408 | 0.20 · 0.132 · 0.02 dex | 0.30 · 0.085 · 0.10 dex |
| NGC 3198 | 268 | 0.25 · 0.058 · 0.05 dex | 0.20 · 0.041 · 0.02 dex |
| NGC 0100 | 94 | 0.44 · 0.110 · 0.31 dex | 0.32 · 0.087 · 0.11 dex |
| UGC 128 | 25 | 1.14 — **disk extends past the twin** · >1.2 dex | 0.22 · 0.023 · 0.03 dex |
| F563-1 | 12 | 3.17 — **disk extends past the twin** · >1.2 dex | 0.23 · 0.065 · 0.04 dex |
| DDO 154 | 54 | 0.62 · 0.451 · 0.66 dex | 0.30 · 0.446 · 0.10 dex |

*Misfit is the rms of $V/V_{\rm flat}$ over $0.5$–$5$ scale lengths; measurement errors are $0.01$–$0.04$; all six lie on the observed Tully–Fisher relation. Each galaxy's $h/\ell$ is fitted freely, so this is a compatibility check, not a test of the rule.* Two consequences of reading (i) are recorded rather than pursued: Tully–Fisher residuals should correlate with rotation-curve shape, slow risers sitting about $0.08$ dex below fast ones; and shape in units of $R/h_{\rm obs}$ should not correlate with observed surface density.

## XII.4 Open [O]

**What remains open.** The optical rule itself (see this part's preamble); the arena constant $A$, which would be a further universal parameter unless derived; the optical mechanism that inflates observed size without inflating mass, which reading (i) requires and nothing yet supplies; and whether real disk curves share one shape in scale-length units. The surface-brightness independence of Tully–Fisher remains the sharpest single test — a strong discriminator between MOND-like and halo-based accounts, though not a clean binary separator, since some halo models also produce weak residual trends with size.

# PART XIII — RENDERING, AGREEING STRUCTURE AND CANONICAL OPTICS: THE VERSION-17 BRANCH [T/D/V/S/O]

*This part collects the author's newer intuitions and gives each the most precise form currently available. Parts 0–XI are unchanged by it, and nothing here is yet a law. Its purpose is to find the right abstraction — and to discard quickly whatever the abstraction cannot support.*

> **The branch's centerpiece, as of 17.1 [S].** Substrate identity is encoded by an incidence correspondence; renderings are compatible reductions of that correspondence; fields measure obstructions to completing those reductions; and observable coherence is controlled by overlap as seen by the detector and environment, not by literal identity of endpoints. This names one mathematical direction for agreeing structure, centrades, rendering and canonical optics together, without claiming that they already arise from one involution. In one sentence: **the canonical space contains the invariant relational content, and a visual spacetime is a globally compatible pattern of partial resolution of it** — with the associated conjecture that what appears as a field or geometric parameter measures what a rendering fails to unpack, rather than an additional substance upstairs.

## XIII.0 The intuitions

**Canonical optics — the camera principle.** An observed event need not be a unique point of canonical space. Several distinct canonical endpoints may constitute *the same* observable event, provided their readout agrees: their positions must project to exactly the same point (**rule A, exact**), while their directions need only agree to within the resolving power of whatever physical channel could record the difference (**rule B, resolution-limited**). A camera is only the example; what matters is whether a physically instantiated record could tell the alternatives apart. The slogan: *the observed event is rigid downstairs; the space of its canonical realizations may be flexible upstairs.*

**Agreeing structure.** One point of the underlying space might appear *twice* in an output space, each appearance keeping different parts of a common structure — as if one "painted a logo on an object in the underlying space and quotiented different parts of it away so as to turn it into two apparently different logos." Would anyone have noticed? No: each appearance keeps something the other lacks, so the two read as two things.

**Centrades.** A centrade is a canonical object that can be packed and unpacked. Fully unpacked in the Kerr direction it carries no angular momentum; in the Reissner–Nordström direction, no charge. A field is the irreducibility that remains. Unpackings conflict — unpacking one can limit another, "whack-a-mole" — so there is a budget.

**Many rendered spacetimes.** Each global unpacking scheme outputs its own visual spacetime. Ours is singled out not by the laws but by the vast number of microstates and the relational complexity it supports; other renderings — every Kerr hole tuned to $a = 0$, say — are equally outputs of the same substrate, inaccessible because the patterns that make up an observer do not survive in them. The picture: a hexagonal sheet of richly linked nodes, each carrying an infinite single-file tower above and below. Its inhabitants would never notice the towers, though they move through them. The motivation was Machian: the whole galaxy holds far more centrades than any local field, so it supports vastly more unpacking microstates, which is why the rendered spacetime privileges the galaxy's frame over nearby inertial frames — while the laws stay invariant, and one could as well have unpacked an electromagnetic field right next to oneself. The author's correction makes this a statement about *renderings*, not a preferred frame inside one spacetime: each global unpacking outputs its own visual spacetime, and whether the gravity base stays invariant across them is open.

**The galactic plane and the spiral.** The stress-energy upstairs may be roughly isotropic, with the galactic plane the fixed set of an involution. And a star passing either of the two points per radial period where its velocity is perpendicular to its position might be rendered on the opposite side, so that the metric's precessing rosettes look like spiral arms.

**One motif runs through all of these: a limitation, or budget.** Locally, centrades whose unpackings conflict; relationally, appearances that keep different parts of one structure; globally, only some assignments of unpackings admissible. The author has flagged this recurrence repeatedly, and it is the thread the formal sections follow.


> **The research program, stated explicitly [D].** What the branch is trying to build, in order of dependence:
>
> 1. **Define an unpacking** — a compatible reduction of the incidence correspondence that outputs a rendered spacetime. This is the bottleneck; everything below depends on it (§XIII.4–XIII.6).
> 2. **Define a centrade minimally** — an agreement object that no permitted readout decomposes — with its resolution states and a compatibility structure that is not a product: a hypergraph, a cocycle, or a joint constraint (§XIII.5). A verified miniature exists in the Hopf-sign budget (§XIII.1).
> 3. **Show fields as residual obstructions**: charge and spin as what a rendering fails to unpack, and decide whether unpacking holds $\delta$ fixed, as a change of rendering, or the horizon area, as a physical process (§XIII.5, §X.0).
> 4. **Construct the chiral weights** $m_\pm$ as gauge-invariant data of chiral solutions — Kim's constituents are the place to look — and only then the rapidity, and any canonical $\mathbb{C}^\times$ behind it (§XIII.5).

> 6. **Tie gravity to rendering**: gravity as a quotient of each rendering, the compatibility $r_u\circ U_u = \bar U_u\circ q$, and a local split of Weyl curvature for the firewall (§XIII.9).
> 7. **Build the optical readout**: a connection on the correspondence, coherence through detector overlap, closure obstruction, and prospect holonomy with curvature odd under the plane reflection — the route to the optical cap problem (§XIII.2–XIII.3, §XIII.8–XIII.9, and §XI.2's item 0).
> 8. **Account for the galactic plane and the spirals**: the fixed-set involution, and the flip as readout monodromy, reconciled with coherent rotation (§XIII.7).
> 9. **Do the constructions available now**: glue the local models of $E$ into a global one, starting at the poles (§II.2); write an explicit prospect connection; construct gauge-invariant chiral weights.
> 10. **Decide the conjugate gate**: the higher-order completion at a Jacobi zero mode, the passage of a critical family through the correspondence, and one observable that differs from standard theory — with the twin-located antipodal family, which must not render as a self-image, as the first test (§XIII.10).
> 11. **Decide the modular-return branch** (Part XIV): a return map with discrete stable classes, a unitary flux redistribution, a non-exact dilation cocycle whose scale is derived rather than inserted, and an invariant that puts the self-return class in the readout kernel — or fail it cleanly on its ledger.
> 12. **Give the bi-connection its dynamics** (Part XV): a sign rule from the substrate, an action for $A$ with the exchange symmetry, the flux on return-map islands and the constancy of $\eta/D_{\rm app}$, and the frequency readout of a length homothety — then the fiber-product form of $\mathcal M$ and both field sectors from one null structure.
> 13. **Decide whether the three $\mathbb C^\times$'s are one** (§XV.6): the charge branch's $w$, Berry's phase with a size, and the two-leg holonomy — and whether the pair's exchange is the twin.
> 14. **Build the sheets** (Part XVI): the compatibility functional on the broken family, the fiber experiment, the Hessian normal derived before it is compared with the sky, and the Hubble derivative $d\eta/dD_{\rm app}$ on a family of sheets — after Part XV's calculations, because a sheet's $\eta$ is Part XV's.
> 15. **Test the equal-footing branch** (Part XVII): whether the Hopf data carry the disk's rotation sense, the twin parity as angular momentum, the global split quotient — and retire it if it merely doubles the arbitrary choices.
> 16. **Run the static program of Part XVIII**: the repacking groupoid and the transport of agreeing structure, the two-centrade Majumdar–Papapetrou test, the obstruction test for $R_{\rm opt}$, the transition sign — and only then Kerr–Newman through Randers geometry.
> 17. **Derive matter** (Part XXI): the $\mathbb{RP}^3$ spectrum and the twin-sign selection, the local action from the laws or its failure, and only then statistics — stopping where the branch would have to append.
> 18. **Test the reduction's origin** (§§XXI.4d–4e): whether a matter field's generation structure is referred to the ambient split real form, and whether the theory's anisotropic split transport supplies two independent, non-collinear split directions — its second-order, pair-of-steps data, since its net symmetric part is single-direction — and what restricted $H_\nu$ that transport generates before the data are consulted; a subfamily with fewer than four mixing parameters and $J_{\rm CP}\neq0$ would be a flavor prediction.
> 19. **Classify the neutrino operator** (§XXI.5): the Hermitian $H_\nu$ on $G_{\rm gen}$ under the surviving conjugation, third-turn, quarter-turn and rendering symmetries, with $[H_\nu,\hat m]\neq0$ required and the tilted-axis family excluded; then what mixing matrices survive.
> 20. **Test the dual-factor lepton clue** (§§XXI.4–4b): whether the three sectors are equivalent channels before the rendering assigns their roles, which would force the third-turn spacing; whether the quarter-turn pairing can be selected by anything but its value; whether $M_F\in{\rm End}(G_{\rm gen})$ can be the flavor mass matrix of a matter action; then the $\tau$ criterion and the phase — retiring the mass law if the quarter turn must be chosen only to fit Koide.
> 21. **Test the spin slot** (Part XX): the identification of a doubled massive history with an electron's data, the bundle it lives over, the local dynamics test, then the massive Weyl coupling and the discrete symmetries — stopping at the first failed arrow.
