# PART XVI — VISUAL SHEETS: THE VERSION-21 BRANCH [S/T/D/V/O]

*Part XV ended with a candidate source of dilation and five missing links, two of which — $\eta\leftrightarrow D_{\rm app}$ and the observable map — are not about the substrate at all but about how a rendered sky is read off canonical histories. This part takes the author's proposal for that reading: the observer's sky is not a single map from histories to directions but the base of a richer history space, and a visible galaxy is one sheet of it. Nothing here adds a mechanism to the substrate; it adds structure to the readout, and it opens with two facts computed in the round arena that any such structure must respect.*

## XVI.0 The intuition and the principle

**The intuition, in the author's terms.** Visual space has already shown signs of differing even from the middle-space representational leaf: the thought that the arms of a spiral might be projections of different legs of one middle-space orbit, and the unresolved fact that a rendered galaxy carries a privileged normal while the middle space is more isotropic — a ball rather than a disk. So index versions of visual space by the dual-path degrees of freedom beyond the arrival direction and the source point. Then a single exact point on a galaxy in the sky need not fix the galaxy: constraining a model by "this point is included" could paint several different galaxies through it, the total object swept out as the arrival direction tilts depending on the relational dual-path data and on where the destination lands relative to the privileged normal. And the normal itself may be less a $\cos(\text{angle})$ than an equilibrium — stationary-action-from-stationary-phase in the plane, killed off it by a phase incompatibility — with the eerie echo that off-plane density in real galaxies falls off exponentially, as wavefunctions do in classically forbidden regions. Having landed the two-paths analogue in Part XV, push the ambition to a phase analogue.

**The Visual-Sheet Principle [S].** For a fixed observer $O$, the admissible canonical history classes arriving at $O$ form an enriched *stratified* space $\mathscr H_O$ — stratified: its discrete data, the Maslov index and the island label, label *sectors*, while the *strata* are produced by degeneracy loci — changes in the Jacobi-kernel dimension, Morse–Bott degeneracies, bifurcations, caustics and island boundaries — so that smooth structure is expected only inside regular strata — each class carrying, beyond its source point and arrival direction, the data the theory already possesses — the imaginary displacement $Y$ and $\nabla_kY$, the phase $\Phi_\Xi$, the scale character $\eta_\Xi$, the relative transport $\mathsf R_\Xi\in CSp(4,\mathbb R)$, the Maslov index, the island — with a projection

$$\pi_{\rm sky}:\mathscr H_O\to S^2_O$$

that asks only in which direction a history arrives. The rendered configurations are the stationary, compatible submanifolds $\mathscr L_\alpha\subset\mathscr H_O$ — *visual sheets* — and a visual object is a projected sheet, $G_\alpha = \pi_{\rm sky}(\mathscr L_\alpha)$. Distinct sheets may project through one sky point, so a visible point does not determine the rendered object. Two disciplines attach. *No new galaxy label*: the sheet index must be an invariant of canonical data the theory already has, or the principle has only renamed "galaxy ID." And *derive the axis first, compare with the sky second*: any emergent normal is computed from the sheet's dynamics before it is set beside the Galactic pole, the dipole, or the prospect-holonomy clue of §XIII.5.

## XVI.1 What the theory already has, and the precedent [T/D]

The sheets are not new objects. The broken antipodal family of §XIV.3 — the full-sky Jacobi-critical family from a source to its twin, carved by secondaries into countable stable stationary members — already supplies their candidates: each *nondegenerate* stationary member is a candidate local sheet branch over nearby source and direction data, by the implicit function theorem — which needs exactly the nondegeneracy that Part XIV has kept open, so degenerate members may form Morse–Bott strata, bifurcate or terminate, and the branch structure is to be constructed, not assumed; the fiber $\pi_{\rm sky}^{-1}(\hat n)$ is the set of members arriving at $O$ along $\hat n$. The standard precedent is exact **[T]**: in wave and geometrical optics the rays form a Lagrangian, or the wavefronts a Legendrian, submanifold of phase space whose projection to configuration space is multi-valued — several images, folds, cusps, caustics, with the Maslov index counting the passages — while the object upstairs stays smooth. Projective Gravity's difference is only that its history space retains coordinates ordinary optics has discarded: the dual-history data of Parts XIII–XV. So the principle is not "invent a second visual universe" but *the sky is the projection of a richer stationary-history manifold*.

## XVI.2 Two facts the sheets must respect [V]

**The one-traversal twin image is one inverted sky, not a field of galaxies.** In the round arena the twin of a source at arc distance $d$ from $O$ appears diametrically opposite its direct image, at arc distance $\pi\ell-d$ — the great circle through $O$ and the source continues past $O$ to the twin — checked on two thousand random sources to $10^{-16}$ **[V]**. So the candidate one-traversal carrier of §XV.3 renders the Galaxy as one global inverted image of the source distribution, its centre toward the anticentre, at distances $\pi\ell-d$ that run from about $25$ to $50$ kpc across the Galaxy's extent at $\ell = 16$ kpc. Multiplicity — many rendered objects at many positions — is not in the round twin image; it needs the broken family: lumps, and source patches near conjugacy, which is exactly where the sheets live.

**The computed shear does not make disks.** The simplest reading of "the middle space is a ball, the rendering makes a disk" is anisotropic magnification, and the theory computes it: an intrinsically round patch renders with axis ratio $q = e^{-2\gamma_{\rm aniso} }$ from the anisotropic squeeze of §XIV.9. For classes from the Sun through the smooth Galaxy $\gamma_{\rm aniso}\sim10^{-7}$, so $q = 0.99999986$: round renders round. A lump gives $\gamma\simeq2GM\ell/c^2b^2$, and $q = 0.3$ at a solar mass needs passage within $10$ AU, which about $4\times10^{-5}$ of arena-length classes do **[V]**. The shear mechanism of §XIV.9 cannot make the sky's disks — other anisotropic magnifications are not thereby excluded, but this is the one the theory computes. Disk-ness must be intrinsic to the rendered patch — the Galaxy's own matter is a disk, and an image of a disk patch is a disk — or come from sheet selection before projection, which is the author's off-plane mechanism of §XVI.5 and the version of the idea the computed shear leaves standing.

**The magnification of a sheet's source patch [D, local].** How large a patch a sheet renders is set by the Jacobi field of the refocused family at the twin. Let $\epsilon_c := |\delta L|/\ell$ be the *longitudinal* displacement of the conjugate point that §XIII.10 computed at parts per million for the Galaxy — a shift of the zero of $D_0(s) = \ell\sin(s/\ell)$ from $\pi\ell$ to $\pi\ell+\delta L$, not an angular shift — so that at the old twin location $|D(\pi\ell)|\sim|\delta L|$ and an angular separation $\Delta\vartheta$ on the sky corresponds locally to a transverse source separation $\Delta x_\perp\sim\ell\,\epsilon_c\,\Delta\vartheta$. At $\ell = 16$ kpc that is $0.016$ pc per radian: **locally, an arcminute of sky corresponds to about one astronomical unit of source.** The statement is local — $D$ is a derivative around one ray, and the shift varies with direction, impact parameter and secondary structure — and it does not extend to a global source region for the whole sky without a global endpoint map, which the theory does not yet have.

Three consequences follow, each conditional. *Coherence:* across an AU patch, $|\Delta\eta|\lesssim\sup|\nabla_s\eta|\,\Delta x$, and Part XV does not supply $|\nabla\eta|\sim1/\ell$ — the mixed Hopf profile is open — so the coherence condition of §XVI.6 holds automatically for near-conjugate sheets *provided the mixed Hopf data have no AU-scale structure*: an acceptance condition, $L_\eta\gg{\rm AU}$, on the smoothness scale of $\eta$. *Internal structure:* if one regular sheet maps an entire image through this near-conjugate regime, then the galaxy's arms, apparent size, velocity field and stellar populations cannot be spatially copied from an AU patch, which has none of them; they must be sheet structure, the caustic organization of the family — the author's proposal in §XVI.5 in its most demanding form, and the place where the detached wing's apparent scale would live. This creates the branch's most brutal acceptance test: real galaxies have resolved spectroscopy — spatially varying line-of-sight velocities, stellar populations, gas lines, metallicities, supernovae, variability — and every one of those macroscopic correlations would have to live in the sheet and history data. *The visual kernel, benchmarked:* on the standard surface-brightness mapping, and assuming the first-conjugate splitting also describes the full $2\pi\ell$ self-return, the fraction of the Sun's own light returning within a solar radius of the Sun is $\sim2\times10^{-13}$ at $\epsilon_c = 10^{-6}$, a point source of apparent magnitude about $-8$, still about $+2$ at $\epsilon_c = 10^{-4}$. Both assumptions are exactly what this part says may fail — the observable map may be nonstandard, and the full-return transfer map is not the first-conjugate one — so this is a *conditional flux benchmark*, not a prediction. It is not seen; suppressing a magnitude $-8$ source below a limiting magnitude $m_{\rm lim}$ needs a factor $S_{\min} = 10^{0.4(m_{\rm lim}+8)}$ — about $10^6$ for naked-eye invisibility, $10^{11}$ to $10^{14}$ for survey thresholds of magnitude $20$ to $27$, depending on the angular morphology the model predicts. The scale kernel of §XV.3 is not enough, and this parameterized constraint is what "visual kernel" must deliver.

## XVI.3 One point, many galaxies [D]

Let two sheets have images meeting at $\hat n_0\in G_\alpha\cap G_\beta$. Then the fiber over $\hat n_0$ holds at least two admissible states, $\Xi_0\neq\Xi_0'$, invisible to the sky at that point. The converse is not automatic: two states in one fiber may lie on the same connected sheet, or on sheets with the same global image. The phenomenon needs the states to lie on *distinct continuable stationary branches* with *distinct projections*, $\pi_{\rm sky}(\mathscr L_\alpha)\neq\pi_{\rm sky}(\mathscr L_\beta)$ — several fiber states are necessary, not sufficient. Continuing each as the direction moves, $\hat n_0\to\hat n_0+\delta\hat n$, traces $\Xi^{(\alpha)}(\hat n)$ and $\Xi^{(\beta)}(\hat n)$ whose projections are the two galaxies. Ordinary optics has a trivial version — two sources along one line of sight overlap on the sky, differing in depth — and the principle's content is that here the states in a fiber differ in *dual-path data*, not only in depth: which galaxy the point belongs to is decided by canonical data the point does not display. The author's intuition is stronger still, and it is what makes the claim specific to this theory: hold fixed not only the visible point but the *source point* behind it. With the source map $s:\mathscr H_O\to B_{\rm rep}$, set $\Pi_O = (s,\pi_{\rm sky}):\mathscr H_O\to B_{\rm rep}\times S^2_O$; the genuinely novel fiber is $\Pi_O^{-1}(x_0,\hat n_0)$, and the phenomenon is: *same source point, same observed point, different hidden history data, different possible galaxies*. Ordinary line-of-sight overlap cannot produce that. The experiment is exact: fix $(x_0,\hat n_0)$, compute the fiber, find every stationary admissible state in it, continue each, project, and compare the global images; if two states lie on distinct continuable branches with distinct images, the phenomenon is real. In the broken-family model this is a computation the theory can already set up, with the members of §XIV.3 as the states.

## XVI.4 Selection and projection: where the normal lives [D/O]

Two operations are involved in rendering, and they must be kept apart. The *projection* is the local rendering map of a sheet on the three-dimensional spatial source patch of the representational base, $d\mathcal R_\alpha:T_q\Sigma_{\rm rep}\to T_{\hat n}S^2$, a $3\to2$ map — not on the five-dimensional middle space, where rank–nullity would force a three-dimensional kernel — whose one kernel direction, *on a regular noncaustic branch*, is the line of sight of the class's ray bundle — the direction ordinary optics also forgets — and whose transverse part is there locally the inverse Jacobi map $D^{-1}$, so that a round patch renders with $\cos i_{\rm app} = e^{-2\gamma_{\rm aniso} }$, which §XVI.2 shows is one for almost every class. On the caustic and branch loci that motivate the whole part, $\det D = 0$ and $D^{-1}$ does not exist; there the description is by generating function or uniform catastrophe asymptotics, which is exactly what the stratification of §XVI.0 is for. A galactic normal cannot be a second forgotten direction of this map: a $3\to2$ map with a two-dimensional kernel has rank one and renders something locally *line-like*, not disk-like. So the normal is not a null singular vector of $d\mathcal R_\alpha$.

It belongs to the other operation, *selection*. The picture that survives §XVI.2 is

$$\text{3D source family}\ \xrightarrow{\ \text{stationary sheet selection}\ }\ \text{2D effective visible support}\ \xrightarrow{\ \pi_{\rm sky}\ }\ \text{2D sky image}$$

— selection reduces the admissible *history family*, not the physical matter distribution, to an effective two-dimensional sheet, and an ordinary rank-two projection then sends that sheet to the sky. The physical disk already exists on $B_{\rm rep}$ (§XVI.7); what selection decides is which histories render, how they continue, and what morphology they carry. The normal $N_\alpha$ — $N_\alpha^{\rm vis}$ in the notation of §XVI.8, distinct a priori from the Galactic normal and from the meridian direction — is the suppressed or unstable eigenvector of the Hessian of the sheet's compatibility functional, $H_{AB} = \partial^2\mathcal S_{\rm eff}/\partial\xi^A\partial\xi^B$: the stable two-dimensional subspace is the visible support, the distinguished direction is the normal, and it is a property of the selection problem rather than of the sources or of the camera. This is the precise form of the author's idea — the plane as where a compatibility equilibrium survives — and it is open; what is closed is that it is not shear and not a kernel of the projection. Three tests attach. Derive $N_\alpha$ from the sheet Hessian without feeding in the disk orientation, and ask whether it aligns with the independently measured dynamical normal, $N_\alpha\parallel N_{\rm dyn}$ — if not, the normal branch fails, and this is stronger than any comparison with the dipole. If selection produced one normal common to all rendered images, the isotropy of galaxy orientations on the sky would falsify it; a per-image normal set by the class's own data is what survives. And whatever the calculation gives is compared with the Galactic pole, the dipole and the prospect-holonomy clue only afterwards.

## XVI.5 The phase analogue: sheets as stationary branches, and off-sheet suppression [S/T]

Let a sheet be selected by stationarity in an internal history variable $\zeta$, $\partial\mathcal S/\partial\zeta = 0$, with a real stable stationary solution $\zeta_*(q)$ on the rendered plane. Off the plane, one of four things can happen to that saddle: it disappears; it collides with another; it moves into the complexified history space; or its steepest-descent contour ceases to meet the physical integration cycle. These are not one mechanism. For $e^{i\mathcal S/\varepsilon_{\rm WKB} }$ with $\mathcal S = \mathcal S_R+i\mathcal S_I$ and $\varepsilon_{\rm WKB}$ the semiclassical parameter of the history integral, a *contributing* complex saddle with $\mathcal S_I>0$ gives the factor $e^{-\mathcal S_I/\varepsilon_{\rm WKB} }$ **[T]**; but a collision of real saddles is a caustic, with Airy-type uniform asymptotics and possibly *enhancement* rather than darkness, and a complex saddle contributes or not according to the steepest-descent thimble and Stokes geometry. So the theorem target is specific: *off-plane continuation must cross a Stokes or complex-saddle transition with $\operatorname{Im}\mathcal S>0$*; then, and only then, exponential suppression follows. That is the legitimate cousin of the author's Schrödinger analogy: complex stationary-action geometry *can* have exponentially suppressed off-sheet branches, when the transition is of that kind. It is *not* the claim that a galaxy's vertical profile is a tunnelling wavefunction, which would be far too quick; it is a mechanism class, and the only one §XVI.2 leaves for rendering-made disks. Part XV makes it far less arbitrary than it would have been: the histories already carry a complex structure — conjugate lifts $\widetilde\gamma_\pm = \gamma\pm iY$, a complex relative factor $e^{-\eta_\Xi+i\Phi_\Xi}$ — so a branch functional $\mathscr W_\alpha = \exp(-\Sigma_\alpha+i\Phi_\alpha)$ on a sheet — $\Sigma_\alpha$ and $\Phi_\alpha$ dimensionless, the imaginary and real parts of the relevant action divided by $\varepsilon_{\rm WKB}$ — with $\Phi$ for compatibility and interference, $\Sigma$ for weight and visibility, $\eta$ for the scale readout and the monodromy for morphology, has its complex plane supplied rather than invented. $\Sigma_\alpha$ is not identified with $\eta_\Xi$: they may descend from the same complex-history geometry, and that is a derivation to do, not an assumption to make. The spiral arms return in this form and no other: not one orbit chopped and mirrored, which §XIII.7 showed does not make spirals from a symmetric disk, but one middle-space orbit family met by several sheets, whose projected pieces separate on the sky because the map is multi-valued and carries monodromy and caustics — still speculative, and far harder to kill than the flip.

## XVI.6 The Hubble relation on the moduli space of sheets [S/D]

Part XV has $\eta_\Xi$ and no $D_{\rm app}$. If one galaxy is one sheet, Hubble's relation compares *different* sheets, so its home is the moduli space of rendered objects,

$$\mathfrak S_O := \{\text{admissible visual sheets for }O\}/\!\sim,\qquad \eta:\mathfrak S_O\to\mathbb R,\qquad D_{\rm app}:\mathfrak S_O\to\mathbb R_+$$

with the two observables defined *independently* on it — not as two functions on one sheet, which would mean a redshift gradient across one galaxy.

**The Sheet Coherence Condition [S/D].** Part XV gives one scale character per history class, $\eta_\Xi$; a point of $\mathfrak S_O$ is a whole sheet. The missing theorem between the two levels is a coherence condition: for $\Xi\in\mathscr L_\alpha$, $\eta_\Xi = \eta_\alpha+\delta\eta_{\rm int}(\Xi)$ with $\eta_\alpha$ the sheet's systemic dilation and $\delta\eta_{\rm int}$ controlled, either by deriving $\eta_\alpha = \langle\eta_\Xi\rangle_{\mathscr L_\alpha}$ from a canonical measure or by proving $\eta_\Xi$ essentially constant across the coherent class. Without it, one galaxy could contain many unrelated canonical redshifts. The stronger form is the target: observationally $\delta\eta_{\rm int}$ is not noise but the resolved velocity field and the internal gravitational and local shifts, so the condition should read $\eta_\Xi = \eta_{\rm sys}(\alpha)+\eta_{\rm kin}(\Xi)+\eta_{\rm local}(\Xi)$, with the sheet dynamics generating the latter two. For near-conjugate sheets §XVI.2 makes the systemic part automatic provided $\eta$ has no AU-scale structure, $L_\eta\gg{\rm AU}$ — and then $\eta_{\rm kin}$ cannot be the source's motion, since an AU patch has none; a rendered rotation curve, if this branch survives, is sheet structure. For sheets away from conjugacy the condition is open.

Along a one-parameter family $\alpha(u)$ the low-redshift target is a single derivative,

$$\frac{d\eta}{dD_{\rm app} }\simeq\frac{H_0}{c}$$

in place of three separate rules — an apparent-distance rule, a redshift rule, and an unexplained proportionality between them. One criterion is ruthless and non-negotiable: **$D_{\rm app}$ must be constructed without using $\eta$ or $H_0$.** Otherwise $D_{\rm app} := c\eta/H_0$ derives Hubble by definition, and the branch forbids that. But one curve through a high-dimensional moduli space is too weak a target: many curves exist. The prize is a *population* statement — on the physically populated low-redshift sheets, $\eta_\alpha\simeq f(D_{ {\rm app},\alpha})$ with weak dependence on the other sheet coordinates, $f'(0) = H_0/c$, and a predicted scatter — so that locally $d\eta\simeq(H_0/c)\,dD_{\rm app}$ through the populated directions of the moduli, not along one convenient trajectory. So the program is to derive two independent observables on the population of stationary sheets and ask whether their image in the $(D_{\rm app},\eta)$ plane is a line of slope $H_0/c$ with the predicted scatter — a falsifiable statement, which inherits the finite-width test of §XIV.8 unchanged: the family must span $\eta$ from $10^{-6}$ to order one. The same logic could later return the detached wing's apparent scale as a sheet property, $R_{\rm app} = R_{\rm app}(\Xi)$ with mass attached to the deeper source class — the optical rescaling Part XII has waited for — but that is downstream and not an axiom here.

## XVI.7 The constraint from local dynamics [T/D]

If the physical Milky Way were an isotropic ball upstairs rendered as a disk, photons would not be the only witness. Parallaxes, stellar distances, proper motions, radial velocities, vertical oscillations and gas dynamics measure the disk directly: it is dynamical, with stars oscillating through a plane of scale height a few hundred parsecs **[T]**. A rendering that altered only appearance would leave the ball visible in stellar motions, so an optics-only isotropic ball is excluded **[D]**. So version 21 asserts no isotropic ball. What it tests is narrower and legitimate: whether a preferred *visual* normal can emerge from sheet selection under a more symmetric geometry than the image displays — and how far that can be pushed is for the data to say, with the representational base $B_{\rm rep}$, where matter and detectors live, and not the optical image, as the place where any such rendering would have to act.

## XVI.8 The plane as a fixed locus: the duality test [V/D]

*The author asked whether the off-plane structure is a duality in its own right — "almost treating the galaxy as a fixed point to it" — and whether it interlocks with the complexified geometry. The theory already had every ingredient: the plane involution of Part XIII, whose fixed set is the Galactic plane; that part's unexplained input that the prospect curvature is odd under it; the real structure $\rho_E$ of Part XV, which exchanges conjugate histories and flips the noncompact scale; and this part's plane as the locus where selection survives. The referee's rule for testing them together was to reduce structure and add none, and to construct the lift rather than assume it. Here is the construction.*

**The lift is forced to invert the fiber [T/D], and it is explicit [V].** At a meridian crossing whose intersection with $B_{\rm rep}$ lies in the Galactic plane, extend the reflection $\jmath:x_4\mapsto-x_4$ to the transverse three-space; its restriction to the small linking $S^2$ has degree $-1$, so $\jmath^*c_1 = -c_1$, and a lift $\widetilde\jmath$ to the Hopf bundle can preserve $c_1 = +1$ only by inverting the fiber **[T/D]**. The typing is this: on the physical Hopf bundle the lift is *anti-equivariant*, $\widetilde\jmath(p\cdot e^{i\chi}) = \widetilde\jmath(p)\cdot e^{-i\chi}$; on the complexified fiber $\mathbb C^\times$ its holomorphic extension acts as $w\mapsto w^{-1}$; and in the $S^3\subset\mathbb C^2$ realization its real-slice expression is $\widetilde\jmath(z_1,z_2) = (\bar z_2,\bar z_1)$, which covers $x_4\mapsto-x_4$, squares to the identity and inverts the phase **[V]**. Being the complexification of a real map, it commutes with $\rho_E$ **[T]**. Two real structures then meet: with $\rho_E:w\mapsto\bar w^{-1}$ antiholomorphic, $\sigma := \widetilde\jmath\circ\rho_E$ is antiholomorphic too, $\sigma(w) = \bar w$; on the base its fixed locus is the purely imaginary normal, and $\operatorname{Fix}(\rho_E)\cap\operatorname{Fix}(\sigma)$ is the plane, $n = y = 0$ — on the complexified fiber $\operatorname{Fix}(\sigma) = \mathbb R^\times$, and on the physical $U(1)$ fiber over the plane the $\sigma$-fixed points are $w = \pm1$, a double cover **[D, local]**. **On the complexified base, the Galactic plane is the intersection of two real forms.** Upstairs the $\rho_E$-real physical fiber over the plane remains $U(1)$; only the locus fixed by *both* real structures has fiber $\{\pm1\}$. That is the precise sense of the author's fixed point: the plane is fixed pointwise; a whole sheet can be fixed as a class, $[\mathfrak D\mathscr L_\alpha] = [\mathscr L_\alpha]$, which is stated as a theorem target and not a postulate.

**Part XIII's parity, given a canonical enforcer [V/C].** The Chern class derives the fiber-inverting lift; it does not by itself derive the parity of the connection. The *standard* Hopf connection is odd under the lift, $\widetilde\jmath^*\mathcal A_H = -\mathcal A_H$ — checked on the model — so its curvature is odd, $\jmath^*F_H = -F_H$, its pull-back to the fixed plane vanishes, $i^*F_H = 0$, the plane connection is flat, and a loop in the plane around a crossing bounds a hemisphere of flux $\pi$, holonomy $-1$ **[V]**. For the full connection, oddness follows if and only if $\mathcal A_H$ is equivariant under $\widetilde\jmath$, and the deformations $\delta\mathcal A_H$ that the class leaves free — the mixed component among them — need not be. So the structure §XIII.9 needed and had to assume — curvature odd under the plane reflection, a flat plane connection with the sign monodromy — now has a canonical symmetry that would enforce it, with the field equation still to select the symmetric connection. One free input of Part XIII acquires a canonical origin, conditional on that equivariance.

**The normal history sector [D].** For a planar history, $\widetilde\jmath\gamma = \gamma$, with imaginary displacement $Y$ along the Galactic normal, $\widetilde\jmath_*Y = -Y$, the lifted reflection and the real structure act identically on the histories, $\widetilde\jmath(\gamma+iY) = \gamma-iY = \rho_E(\gamma+iY)$, and differ on the fiber only by the conjugation the Chern class forces: $\rho_E$ sends $(\mu,\chi)\mapsto(-\mu,\chi)$, $\widetilde\jmath$ sends $(\mu,\chi)\mapsto(-\mu,-\chi)$, so $\widetilde\jmath = \rho_E\circ(\text{Hopf-phase conjugation})$ on this sector — fiber inversion on $U(1)_H$, not to be confused with the electromagnetic charge conjugation of Part III until the three $\mathbb C^\times$'s are shown to be one — and the relative scale is odd under both. Above-and-below-plane reflection *is* complex-history conjugation on the normal mode, up to Hopf-phase conjugation. The orientation of the two-leg loop is what decides everything that follows, so it is tracked explicitly. For the *meridian* displacement $\jmath$ fixes $Y$, each complex leg is preserved individually, and the oriented loop $C$ is mapped to itself with orientation preserved; if $\widetilde\jmath^*\mathcal A = -\mathcal A$ then ${\rm Hol}(C) = {\rm Hol}(\widetilde\jmath C) = {\rm Hol}(C)^{-1}$, so ${\rm Hol}(C)^2 = 1$: $\eta = 0$ and $\Phi\in\{0,\pi\}$. For the *normal* displacement $\widetilde\jmath_*Y = -Y$ exchanges the legs, the oriented loop goes to $C^{-1}$, the orientation reversal and the sign reversal compensate, and no constraint follows: scale is allowed. **For $\widetilde\jmath$-equivariant Hopf data, the meridian mode is killed; the Galactic-normal mode may survive.** If the sheet functional is $\widetilde\jmath$-invariant, $\mathcal S_{\rm eff}(q_\parallel,n) = \mathcal S_{\rm eff}(q_\parallel,-n)$, the plane is stationary by symmetry — no odd powers of $n$ — and the Hessian splits into even and odd sectors, with the normal $N_\alpha$ the anti-invariant mode: the "one eigenvector that happens to be special" of §XVI.4 becomes the odd representation **[T]**. Symmetry fixes stationarity and nothing more: the sign of the quadratic mode and any Stokes transition remain the dynamics' to decide (§XVI.5).

**Three normals, kept apart [D].** The word "normal" now does three jobs, and they are not one vector. $N_{\rm mer}$ is the normal to $B_{\rm rep}$ inside $\mathcal M$ — the meridian direction of Part XV's original candidate. $N_{\rm gal}$ is the normal to the Galactic plane inside $B_{\rm rep}$ — the odd mode of this section. $N_\alpha^{\rm vis}$ is the sheet-specific suppressed direction of §XVI.4, the rendered normal of one image. A priori $N_{\rm gal}\neq N_\alpha^{\rm vis}$: §XVI.4 requires a per-image normal that survives the observed diversity of orientations, while this section uses one fixed plane reflection; if there is a map $N_{\rm gal}\mapsto N_\alpha^{\rm vis}$, the sheet dynamics must derive it, and the duality must not be allowed to smuggle in a universal visible orientation.

**The normal-mode extension test [V/O].** The identity above is computed for a planar history. The carrier must work for the whole rendered population, so the surviving odd displacement must extend as an admissible conjugate-lift Jacobi mode across every relevant $\jmath$-paired class — symmetry can say how a putative $Y$ transforms, but it cannot manufacture a Jacobi zero mode where conjugacy does not supply one. On the round arena it does extend: along the great circle through $p$ in direction $d$, the normal component of the Galactic normal is the constant vector $A = e_4-p_4p-d_4d$, parallel along the circle, so $Y = y_0\sin(s/\ell)A$ is a fixed-endpoint Jacobi field for every antipodal class except the great circles through the plane's poles, where $e_4\in{\rm span}(p,d)$ and $A = 0$; and $\jmath_*Y_\Xi = -Y_{\jmath\Xi}$ exactly, so the mode transforms in the odd representation across mirror pairs **[V]**; that mirror classes then carry the same $\eta$ for every class, $\eta_{\jmath\Xi} = \eta_\Xi$, needs in addition $\jmath^*F_H = -F_H$ and is **[D/C]**, conditional on $\widetilde\jmath$-equivariant Hopf data. With lumps, where the gate itself is open, the extension is a theorem to prove; failure would mean the replacement carrier exists only on the fixed plane, and Part XV's redshift mechanism would have no universal carrier.

**The pressure it puts on Part XV [D, conditional on $\widetilde\jmath$-equivariant Hopf data].** The same oddness acts on the scale character. The kill tests are stated on the observable itself, gauge-invariantly: for the systemic, Hubble-like part of the dilation, $\eta_{\jmath\Xi} = \eta_\Xi$ under the reflection $\jmath$ through the Galactic plane, and $\eta_{\kappa\Xi} = \eta_\Xi$ under the in-plane reflection $\kappa$ through the centre direction — a different involution, given its own symbol, since a candidate sector can be even under one and odd under the other; if symmetry forces a minus sign in either, that sector is dead. What is excluded observationally is a hemisphere-wide or left–right *systematic* sign reversal of the Hubble-like component — individual blueshifts from peculiar velocities are not the issue. Against these: with the *meridian* displacement of §XV.3, the loop argument above gives planar classes no scale character and mirror classes above and below the plane opposite $\eta$ — a systematic hemispheric reversal, so that sector fails. With the *normal* displacement, mirror classes carry the same $\eta$, planar classes are phase-free with the scale allowed, and one global orientation of the normal removes the $Y\to-Y$ ambiguity for all classes — positivity of $\eta_\Xi$ remaining a separate condition on the total mixed curvature; the in-plane test is then a statement about the *total* mixed curvature $\bar F_H = F_{\rm monopole}+\delta F_H$, of which the monopole term is the canonical rotationally symmetric representative of the unavoidable unit flux class — the class is forced, the pointwise profile is not, and $F_H = F_{\rm monopole}+\delta F_H$ is a chosen decomposition — with $F_{\rm monopole}(N,k) = x\cdot(N\times k)/2r^3$ and moment $b\cdot(N\times k)/b^2$ along a ray, an odd pseudoscalar under $\kappa$ if the disk's crossings carry like parities, while $\delta F_H$ is exactly what the class does not fix. So the normal sector survives the $\jmath$ test and faces the $\kappa$ test with its outcome depending on the parity assignments of item 10 in §XI.2 and on $\delta F_H$. That puts item 10 at the front of the program, ahead of the field equation's positivity.

**Status.** Reduced: the Part XIII parity has a canonical enforcer, the plane is a fixed locus, the normal is the odd mode, stationarity is symmetry-protected. Added: nothing. Gained: two parity kill tests, the transparent verdict that the meridian mode is killed and the normal mode may survive, and the strong test $N_\alpha\parallel N_{\rm dyn}$ of §XVI.4 unchanged. Open: the self-dual sheet, the global existence of the lift, the $\widetilde\jmath$-equivariance of the actual Hopf data. And the next question is now exactly this: *find all fixed-endpoint Jacobi displacement sectors $Y$ satisfying twin, plane and in-plane parity, with $\eta_{\rm self} = 0$, $\eta_{\rm systemic}$ reflection-even and $\eta_{\rm systemic}>0$ on the admissible rendered population — and extending from the planar fixed locus across the full sheet population.* If none, the redshift branch of Part XV dies for a clear mathematical reason; if exactly one survives, this branch will have selected the previously free complex-history direction before the dynamics are solved, and the mixed-curvature field equation has a sharply defined target.

## XVI.9 Status and program [D]

| grade | contents |
|:--|:--|
| **standard mathematics and physics** | Lagrangian and Legendrian projections, multi-valued images, caustics and the Maslov index; steepest descent and complex saddles; the local dynamical evidence for the Galactic disk |
| **adopted by decision** | the Visual-Sheet Principle: the sky as the projection of an enriched observer-conditioned history space, rendered objects as projected stationary sheets, with no new galaxy label |
| **verified here** | the one-traversal twin image is a single inverted full-sky image, every twin diametrically opposite its direct image at distance $\pi\ell-d$; anisotropic magnification cannot make the sky's disks — round renders round to $10^{-7}$ through the smooth Galaxy, and order-unity shear needs passage within $10$ AU of a star, which $4\times10^{-5}$ of classes achieve |
| **verified here (21.4)** | the lift of the plane reflection to the Hopf model, anti-equivariant on $U(1)$ and $w\mapsto w^{-1}$ on the complexified fiber, forced by the Chern class; the standard Hopf connection odd under it, its curvature vanishing on the plane, the plane connection flat with holonomy $-1$ — Part XIII's parity given a canonical enforcer, conditional on the equivariance of the full connection; on the round arena the Galactic-normal Jacobi mode extends to every antipodal class but the great circles through the plane's poles, transforming in the odd representation |
| **derived here** | one point lies on several rendered galaxies when fiber states of $\pi_{\rm sky}$ lie on distinct continuable branches with distinct images — several states being necessary, not sufficient — and here they differ in dual-path data, not only depth; the $3\to2$ rendering differential on the spatial source patch has the line of sight as its one kernel and the inverse Jacobi map as its transverse part; an emergent normal cannot be a second kernel of the projection, which would render lines, and must be the suppressed direction of sheet selection; a common normal for all images would be falsified by the isotropy of orientations; an optics-only isotropic ball is excluded by local dynamics; at the ppm longitudinal conjugate displacement $\epsilon_c$, an arcminute of sky corresponds locally to an AU of source, so sheet coherence holds automatically for near-conjugate sheets provided $L_\eta\gg{\rm AU}$, and if one regular sheet maps an entire image through that regime its internal structure must be sheet structure; on the standard surface-brightness mapping and the first-conjugate transfer map, the returned Sun would be a point of magnitude about $-8$ — a conditional benchmark, with the required suppression $10^{0.4(m_{\rm lim}+8)}$; the plane is the intersection of two real forms, the lifted reflection equals the real structure on the normal history mode up to Hopf-phase conjugation, the plane is symmetry-protected stationary with the normal as the odd mode, and the duality imposes two parity kill tests on the systemic dilation, $\eta_{\jmath\Xi} = \eta_\Xi$ under the plane reflection and under in-plane reflection through the centre — conditional on equivariant Hopf data — with the meridian mode failing the first by loop orientation and the normal mode surviving it |
| **open** | the branch structure of $\mathscr H_O$ within its regular strata, conditional on the nondegeneracy Part XIV left open; the sheet index as an invariant of existing data; the compatibility functional and its Hessian; off-sheet suppression as a Stokes or complex-saddle transition with $\operatorname{Im}\mathcal S>0$, derived rather than assumed; whether $\Sigma_\alpha$ and $\eta_\Xi$ share a source; the Sheet Coherence Condition for sheets away from conjugacy; $\eta$ and $D_{\rm app}$ as independent observables on the moduli space $\mathfrak S_O$, with $D_{\rm app}$ built without $\eta$ or $H_0$, and the population factorization $\eta\simeq f(D_{\rm app})$; the smoothness scale $L_\eta$ of the mixed Hopf data; a global endpoint map beyond the local Jacobi regime; the full-return transfer map, distinct from the first-conjugate one; the visual kernel at the parameterized level $10^{0.4(m_{\rm lim}+8)}$; the global existence of the lifted reflection, the $\widetilde\jmath$-equivariance of the actual Hopf data, and the self-dual sheet; the normal-mode extension test with lumps; the map, if any, from the Galactic normal to the per-image rendered normal; which displacement carries the dilation, and the parity assignments of item 10; the experiments below |

**The experiments.** *Same source, same point, many galaxies*: fix a source point and a direction, compute the fiber of $\Pi_O = (s,\pi_{\rm sky})$ in the broken-family model, continue each state, compare the images. *The dynamical normal*: derive $N_\alpha$ from the Hessian blind to the disk, and test $N_\alpha\parallel N_{\rm dyn}$. *Resolved spectroscopy*: if the near-conjugate regime maps a whole image, the sheet data must reproduce spatially resolved velocity fields, populations and metallicities — the decisive internal-structure test. *The emergent normal*: compute the Hessian of the sheet's compatibility functional at a selected member, find its suppressed direction, and only then compare with the Galactic pole, the dipole and the prospect-holonomy clue. *The orientation test*: a universal rendered normal fails against the sky. *The Hubble derivative*: on a family in the moduli space of sheets, construct $D_{\rm app}$ without $\eta$ or $H_0$, and test whether the population traces $\eta\simeq f(D_{\rm app})$ with $f'(0) = H_0/c$ and the predicted scatter, and against the finite-width test.

**The program.** Part XVI is frozen conceptually at this version: no further mechanism is to be added, and what follows is calculation. Part XV's calculations come first — now beginning with the representation problem of §XVI.8 in its cleanest form — all fixed-endpoint Jacobi displacement sectors satisfying twin, plane and in-plane parity, with the self-return in the kernel, the systemic $\eta$ reflection-even and positive on the rendered population, and extending from the planar fixed locus across the sheet population — then the mixed-curvature field equation and the finite-width test — because a sheet's $\eta$ is Part XV's $\eta$. Then the sheets: the compatibility functional on the broken family, the fiber experiment, the Hessian normal, and the derivative. The prize — a prize, not a result — is one statement: *galaxy identity is one stationary history sheet*, carrying phase, dilation, shape, apparent distance and normal together, so that the Hubble relation is the geometry of the sheet moduli rather than something slipped in.
