| **verified here** | $R_{\rm opt} = -6\delta^2/r^4$; extremality as optical scalar-flatness with $\vert{\rm Ric}\vert^2 = 24M^2(r-M)^6/r^{12}$; the decomposition $\vert{\rm Ric}\vert^2 = R^2/3+\vert\mathring{\rm Ric}\vert^2$; the Poisson equation $\Delta W = \tfrac34\delta^2W^5/r^4$ for the optical conformal factor, harmonic at extremality; Majumdar–Papapetrou optically scalar-flat, with the two-centre relational curvature; the inversion an exact optical isometry fixing the photon sphere; the identifier $24x^6/(1+x)^{12}$ inversion-invariant and monotone on each side; the leading-order two-body balance condition and its simplex form; the degrees on the linking sphere — $+1$ for the inversion, $-1$ for the throat exchange and the antipodal map; the conformal invariance of the static optical metric and of the stationary Randers data; the failure of scalar-flatness at extremality for both the Randers Riemannian part and the ZAMO optical metric of Kerr–Newman; $W = \sqrt{\Omega/N}$ with $N\Omega = 1$ at extremality; the Finsler Ricci of the Randers optical geometry, Riemannian checks exact, and its fit $-(6/r^4)(\delta^2+c\,a^2)$ |

**The program.** Of the eight theorem targets, six are now done or decided: the optical defect theorem, the multi-centre theorem, the inversion model, the identifier, the two-centrade test — joint extremality is the intrinsic constraint of the static sector — and the transition sign, fiber inversion. The obstruction test has its first negative result, the abelian Weyl connection of the conformal factor being flat. The exact two-body statement is now the classification theorem, the kernel-transport criterion is derived, and the stationary case is measured: the Finsler trace at extremality is $-(6/r^4)c\,a^2$, so the spin channel does not resolve optically. The decisive question is answered within the frozen tower: no conformal transformation of the pre-quotient metric and none of the tower's known symmetries at fixed $g_G$ changes agreeing structure; only a change of rendering does. **Version 23 pauses conceptually here.** Its exact results — the optical defect theorem, the harmonic conformal factor at extremality, the multi-centre scalar-flatness, the inversion isometry and its identifier — stand on their own; its mechanism, conformal repacking, is dead in the optical layer, and its budget survives only as Part XVI's moduli of sheets. Fixed-$g_G$ repacking was the version-23 test subclass, and it is killed; rendering covariance — different gravity bases per render, the locking law invariant (§XIX.1) — is the author's broader principle and was always the intention. What remains is calculation on the one door left open: whether a repacking acting on the meridian scale or on the rendering functional can change agreeing structure at fixed $g_G$, and, separately, the inversion pair's transition from the history connection and a non-abelian realization of $R_{\rm opt}$, or its exclusion. **What the branch has established is one exact picture: extremality is the sector in which the optical conformal factor is harmonic and centres superpose, nonextremality is its source, and the extremal geometry renders one radial class twice with a curvature identifier that tracks the pair.** What it proposes on top of that is graded as such.

# PART XIX — CENTRADE TRIALITY: THE VERSION-24 BRANCH [T/V/D/S/O]

*The author's next idea is that the three channels of a centrade — the spin, charge and nonextremality of a black hole — might be three abstract channels of a deeper object, with the rendering selecting which of them appears as which; that another rendering might have the optical trace measure $a^2$ or $Q^2$ where ours has it measure $\delta^2$; and that the number three might be the number of spatial dimensions. Part XVIII dictates the form this must take: a conformal transformation of the same geometry changes nothing optical, so whatever permutes the channels must act on the rendering, not on the metric. This part states the conjecture, its firewall, a disciplined order — the $\mathbb Z_2$ exchange of charge and nonextremality before any permutation involving spin — and the first computations, two of which are negative in instructive ways. Parts 0–XVIII are unchanged.*

## XIX.0 The conjecture, and the firewall [S]

**The conjecture.** The Kerr–Newman simplex is the rendered image of a permutation-symmetric three-channel centrade state, and spacetime selection breaks that triality by assigning its channels the roles of spin, charge and nonextremality.

**The firewall.** Permutation symmetry must exist *before* the rendering labels the channels. Noticing that $a^2+Q^2+\delta^2 = M^2$ is symmetric and then swapping the names afterward discovers only an algebraic symmetry of an equation. The conjecture has content only if there is an unlabelled canonical object with an actual $S_3$ action, and spacetime itself chooses which slot means charge, which spin, which gap.

## XIX.1 The simplex, the typed parameter space, and representation type [T/D/S]

The weights $w_a+w_Q+w_\delta = 1$ of §XVIII.1 live on a 2-simplex, a triangle, with the exact permutation symmetry $S_3$ — the natural home of the intuition, and it needs no appeal yet to the dimension of space. Separate *channel* from *role*: let the deeper centrade carry channel amplitudes $u = (u_1,u_2,u_3)$ with $u_1^2+u_2^2+u_3^2 = M^2$ and no label saying which is which, and let a rendering supply a role assignment $R:u\mapsto(a,Q,\delta)$; another rendering is $R_\pi = R\circ\pi^{-1}$ for $\pi\in S_3$. The clean carrier is not a diagonal matrix in an already chosen basis — that quietly installs the channels one is trying not to label — but an abstract positive self-adjoint operator $B$ on an internal channel space $\mathcal C_x$ with ${\rm tr}\,B = 1$, whose *unordered* eigenvalues are $\{w_1,w_2,w_3\}$ and whose eigenlines carry no role names; a rendering is then partly a bijection from the eigenlines to $\{\text{spin},\text{charge},\text{gap}\}$, and $S_3$ is the permutation ambiguity of that labelling rather than something bolted on. $B = \tfrac13I$ is maximally degenerate; distinct eigenvalues distinguish three slots before any of them is called $a$, $Q$ or $\delta$.

**The simplex is not the physical parameter space.** The triangle remembers only $a^2$, $Q^2$, $\delta^2$. Charge has a sign, $Q = \pm|Q|$; spin has an axis, $\mathbf J = Ma\,\hat n$; $\delta$ has neither. So the physical Kerr–Newman data form a bundle $\mathcal P_{\rm KN}\to\Delta^2$ whose fiber carries the sign and the axis, and the hard test is whether the $S_3$ on $\Delta^2$ *lifts* to $\mathcal P_{\rm KN}$. §XIX.2 answers it: the action lifts to the pre-rendering channel space and fails to lift to the physical data because the spin axis is spacetime data no channel carries — the failure is the rendering's, which is what the conjecture requires.

**Representation type.** What the readouts of §XIX.5 show is that a role map does not assign names; it assigns *representations*. If triality exists it cannot mean one rendered tensor exchanged for an identical one; it must mean one abstract channel sent by the selection to a Lorentz scalar, another to a gauge charge, a third to an axial spacetime representation, $R:\mathcal C\to{\rm Rep}(G_{\rm rendered})$, with isomorphic channels landing in inequivalent representation spaces. The actual question of this part is therefore: **can three isomorphic pre-rendering channels acquire inequivalent spacetime representation types through the selection functor?** That is stronger and cleaner than asking whether three numbers can be permuted. It also dissolves Part XVIII's fork rather than fighting it: different role assignments may produce *different* gravity bases, and what stays invariant is deeper than $g_G$ — the canonical centrade data, the budget, and the incidence structure. That is what the author intended from the start of Part XVIII: a new middle space, or at least a new gravity base, for every rendered microstate, with the constraint that only those transformations between microstates are allowed under which the gravity base *continues to control the curvature and the Hopf fiber lengths of the middle space* — the locking law of Part IV must hold for the new base as it held for the old, since otherwise the laws would apply only some of the time. The invariant of repacking is the law, not the metric. Stated as principles: **the Kerr principle** — a new rendered degree of freedom must arise by reorganizing, complexifying or selecting existing canonical structure before any enlargement of the canonical space is allowed, and when the shell's own states fall short one searches the dual-history data, the holonomies, the real-form choices and the Jacobi data before introducing a state variable; and **rendering covariance** — the laws are rendering-covariant, the rendered spacetime need not be. Each admissible microstate $U$ is a rendering $\mathscr R_U(\mathfrak X) = (E_U,\mathcal M_U,B_{ {\rm rep},U},B_{ {\rm grav},U},g_{G,U},\mathcal A_{H,U},L_{H,U},\ldots)$ of one canonical substrate $\mathfrak X$, and an allowed transformation $T:U\to U'$ sends an admissible rendering to another — so $L_{H,U'} = \mathcal F[g_{G,U'}]$ must follow from $L_{H,U} = \mathcal F[g_{G,U}]$, and the selected axis, its real form and the history sheets transform as parts of the package — exactly as a symmetry sends solutions to solutions rather than fixing every field. A microstate is then a rendering prescription with its whole output package, and the macrostate counting of §XVIII.8 is over renderings of one set of laws.

## XIX.2 The channel space as $\mathbb C^3$: the flavor inspiration [T/V/D/S]

*The author's principle is that nature reuses its structures, so that is the road to unification: the two connections of Part XV were inspired by the two Born paths, and the channel space should be inspired by lepton flavor rather than by a bare $\mathbb R^3$ — flavors being, as the author recalled, a complex three-dimensional structure — with however flavor interplays with phase imported as the way this structure interplays with the theory's phase, and the complex data either left in the complexified-spacetime slot or quotiented away in the output mapping.*

**The structure [T/V].** Three flavor amplitudes live in $\mathbb C^3$, and the flavor and propagation bases are two bases of that one space related by a $3\times3$ unitary — the mixing matrix — so that phases accumulated in one basis change the weights read in the other. Take the deeper centrade state to be $z\in\mathbb C^3$ with $z^\dagger z = M^2$, so $z\in S^5$, and quotient the common phase: $S^5/U(1) = \mathbb{CP}^2$, the sibling of the Hopf construction $S^3/U(1) = \mathbb{CP}^1$ that already sits at the heart of this theory. The moment map $[z]\mapsto(|z_1|^2,|z_2|^2,|z_3|^2)/\|z\|^2$ has image exactly the 2-simplex — checked on two thousand random states — with fiber over an interior point the torus $T^2$ of the two relative phases **[V]**. So a generic state carries two budget weights *and two relative phases*, and the triangle remembers only the first half: **the Kerr–Newman weights become the phase-blind intensities of three complex amplitudes.** The symmetry group of an *abstract* three-channel space is $U(3)\supset U(1)^3$; the common phase is factored out, the relative phases remain, and the Weyl group of $SU(3)$ is $S_3$ **[T]**, lifting to $\mathbb{CP}^2$ by permutation matrices **[V]**. But the theory's own carrier (§XIX.2a) does not supply $U(3)$: ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2$ inherits $SU(2)\times U(1)_H$, whose Weyl group is $\mathbb Z_2$, and $S_3$ exists only for the complexification $SL(3,\mathbb C)$, which neither real form supplies. The permutation triality of §XIX.0 is therefore not native to the shell; what is native is computed in §§XIX.2a–2c. Three different threes are kept apart: $\mathbb C^3$ the space, $S_3$ the Weyl permutations, and $\mathbb Z_3$ the centre of $SU(3)$, which is not yet in play.

**Rendering as a mixing matrix [V/D].** With a channel basis $|c_i\rangle$ and a role basis $|r_\alpha\rangle$ supplied by the rendering, $|r_\alpha\rangle = \sum_iU^{(R)}_{\alpha i}|c_i\rangle$ with $U^{(R)}\in U(3)$, and a pure permutation is the special case $U^{(R)} = P_\pi$, for which phases do nothing but reorder. For a general rendering basis the rendered weights are $P_\alpha = |\langle r_\alpha|\psi\rangle|^2$, and relative phase repacks them: with channel intensities fixed at $(0.5,0.3,0.2)$ and an illustrative mixing of lepton-like angles, the rendered weights range over $(0.03$–$0.97,\ 0$–$0.94,\ 0$–$0.65)$ as the two relative phases vary, with $P_1+P_2+P_3 = 1$ always **[V]**. The budget is invariant; the split moves with phase; and "repacking" ceases to mean moving real weight between boxes — it means changing relative channel phase and thereby the projection into rendered roles. This is the mechanism Part XVIII's conformal route lacked, and it lives where §XVIII.6 said any repacking must: in the rendering.

**What the existing Hopf phase can and cannot do [D].** The natural home is not a new spacetime dimension but an internal bundle: the associated rank-three bundle $\mathcal F = E\times_{U(1)}\mathbb C^3$ with $U(1)_H$ acting diagonally, so that the theory's Hopf phase is the *common* phase of the three channels and the quotient $E\to\mathcal M$ that forgets the spin phase forgets exactly it, leaving the relational phase geometry $[z_1:z_2:z_3]$ behind. But the projectivization $\mathbb P(\mathcal F)$ is a $\mathbb{CP}^2$-bundle whose structure group is the image of the diagonal $U(1)$ in $PU(3)$, which is trivial: $\mathbb P(\mathcal F) = \mathcal M\times\mathbb{CP}^2$. The Hopf connection transports the common phase and leaves the two relative phases inert; twisting them would need an $\mathfrak{su}(3)$-valued connection, $\mathbb A = \mathcal A_{\rm common}I_3+\mathcal A_{\rm rel}$ with $\mathcal A_{\rm common}\overset{?}{=}\mathcal A_H$, and the theory has none. The conservative route is the flavor route: no new gauge field, only a mismatch of bases and eigenvalue-dependent history phases, $\psi(s) = e^{-isf(B)}\psi(0)$, converted by $U^{(R)}$ into rendered weights; a non-abelian connection is promoted only if the geometry forces it **[S]**. Version 24.1 recorded a tension here — that the theory's own $\mathbb C^3$ carries three different Hopf charges — and it is withdrawn in §XIX.2a: that is true of the maximal torus of $SU(2)$, not of the Hopf circle the theory actually uses.

**XIX.2a The theory's own $\mathbb C^3$: the carrier, computed [V/D].** The semi-free local model of §II is $\mathbb R_t\times S^1\times\mathbb C^2$ with the *diagonal* circle $(z_1,z_2)\mapsto e^{i\chi}(z_1,z_2)$, and the shell's spin-one representation is ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2 = \mathbb C^3$. On it the diagonal circle acts as $e^{2i\chi}$ on every component — a common phase of *weight two* — and the twin involution $-1$ acts trivially, which is the same fact **[V]**. So the associated channel bundle is $\mathcal F_2 = E\times_{\rho_2}\mathbb C^3$ with $\rho_2(e^{i\chi}) = e^{2i\chi}I_3$, its induced common-phase connection is $2\mathcal A_H$, not $\mathcal A_H$, and its projectivization is still trivial, $\mathbb P(\mathcal F_2) = \mathcal M\times\mathbb{CP}^2$, with the relative phases untouched. Two actions coexist on it and are kept distinct, exactly as Part I keeps them: $SU(2)_{\rm left}$, which rotates ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2$ as the complexified vector representation $\mathbb C\otimes\mathbb R^3$ and supplies the real-frame $SO(3)$, and $U(1)_{H,{\rm right} }$, the principal action, which supplies the common phase. The shell's base vector, invariant under the fiber, and the weight-two channel state, which transforms under it, are representation-theoretically related and not the same object. Twin-evenness makes the representation compatible with descent through the source–twin identification; full descent through $U(1)_{\rm mer}$ to the gravity base remains to be established. What the theory owns, then, is the correct **carrier representation** — three because space has three dimensions, through the representation the shell already carries — not yet a physically populated $\mathbb{CP}^2$.

**The coherent-state fork [V].** A single shell spinor $\psi = (\alpha,\beta)$ generates only $\psi^{\odot2} = (\alpha^2,\sqrt2\alpha\beta,\beta^2)$: projectively the Veronese $\mathbb{CP}^1$, the $SU(2)$ coherent-state orbit inside $\mathbb{CP}^2$, and in Cartesian terms exactly the *null* vectors $z\cdot z = 0$ — every single-spinor state has polarization $p = 0$ to machine precision **[V]**. Under the rendering hypothesis, such states are incompatible with the Kerr–Newman weight identification in both natural bases. In any real frame their weights are all $\leq\tfrac12$, so a single-spinor centrade could never render as Schwarzschild, extremal Reissner–Nordström or extremal Kerr. In the weight basis their weights are $(x^2,2x(1-x),(1-x)^2)$, a curve $\sqrt{w_+}+\sqrt{w_-} = 1$ through the simplex, which with the ends as spin and gap reads $a+\delta = M$, $Q^2 = 2a\delta$ — and an uncharged Kerr hole with $0<a<M$ is off the curve. So **the shell spinor alone does not supply the channel state**; it must be a superposition, and the theory has a candidate at hand. For a null $v$, the states $z = Av+B\bar v$ have polarization $p = 2|AB|/(|A|^2+|B|^2)$ exactly **[V]** — a coherent state plus its conjugate interpolates continuously from $p = 0$ to $p = 1$, and with rotations reaches the general normal form — so the conjugate-lift pair of Part XV is the natural route to populating the carrier. But Part XV's conjugate histories do not yet supply the coefficients $A$, $B$ in a proved channel-state map; the theorem target is a canonical map from the conjugate-history pair into ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2$ with its allowed coefficients. That the physical channel state is this pair's superposition is the branch's state hypothesis **[S]**. Note also that ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2$ has *two* canonical bases — the real Cartesian frame and the weight basis of a chosen axis — related by a fixed unitary: the "two preferred bases" of the flavor picture exist here canonically **[D]**, with the Cartesian basis reading rendered intensities and the weight basis diagonalizing rotation about the axis.

**Rendering as a frame: the natural subgroup, the hypothesis, and the exact region [T/V/S].** $\mathbb C^3 = \mathbb C\otimes\mathbb R^3$ inside the shell gives a canonical real form and hence a natural $SO(3)$ **[D/T]**; that physical role renderings are exactly real frames rather than some larger unitary basis is the branch's rendering hypothesis **[S]**. Under it the polarization theorem is exact. A common phase can always be chosen so that $z = x+iy$ has $x\cdot y = 0$, and a frame then puts $z = M(\cos\alpha,\ i\sin\alpha,\ 0)$ with $0\leq\alpha\leq\pi/4$, so $p = |z\cdot z|/\|z\|^2 = \cos2\alpha$ and $|x|^2 = M^2(1+p)/2$, $|y|^2 = M^2(1-p)/2$. The rendered weights are the diagonal of $xx^{\rm T}+yy^{\rm T}$ in an orthonormal frame, whose eigenvalues are $M^2((1+p)/2,\ (1-p)/2,\ 0)$, so by Schur–Horn the attainable budget region is *exactly* $\{w\in\Delta^2:\ \max_iw_i\leq(1+p)/2\}$ **[T]**, confirmed by frame optimization at $p = 0.36$: targets with $\max w_i$ up to $0.681$ reached to $10^{-16}$, a target at $0.731$ not **[V]**. $p$ does not merely bound the budget; it classifies exactly which part of the Kerr–Newman simplex a state can reach by changing real frames — all of it at $p = 1$, none of the vertices at $p<1$. The Hopf phase, being common, cannot change $p$; nor can any frame.

**XIX.2b The Kerr construction as the instance [T/V/D].** *The author's sanity check against attaching internal directions to the canonical space by hand — which, given the theory's record, would ruin it — is to use how Kerr itself is constructed as an instance of the general principle.* It is. Kerr–Newman has $\Delta = (r-M)^2-\delta^2$ with $\delta^2 = M^2-a^2-Q^2$, and in Weyl coordinates $\rho = \sqrt\Delta\sin\theta$, $z = (r-M)\cos\theta$ the horizon is the rod $z\in[-\delta,\delta]$ on the axis: **the real half-length of the source along the axis is the nonextremality** **[V]**. And by Newman–Janis, Kerr's Weyl scalar is Schwarzschild's with $r\to r-ia\cos\theta$, the distance from a source displaced by $ia$ along the axis: **the imaginary displacement of the source along the axis is the spin** **[T]**. So the two axial data of Kerr–Newman package as one complex number along the selected axis,

$$\zeta = \delta+ia,\qquad |\zeta|^2 = M^2-Q^2,$$

a *candidate unified axial datum* — that it is literally one canonical complex source variable of the underlying geometry is the theorem target, not yet the result — and the charge lies outside it. As a channel vector $d = \zeta\,\hat n$: real and imaginary parts collinear, $p = 1$ exactly, so every split of *its own norm* $M^2-Q^2$ between gap and spin is reachable by a real frame; this is the spin–gap subbudget, not the full $M^2$. What Kerr has produced is one complex axial doublet $(\delta,a)$ and one still-separate charge datum $Q$ — not three equivalent channels — and §XIX.2c shows why they nevertheless share one quadratic budget. This decides three things. First, the complex structure that pairs the channels is $i_{\rm spacetime}$, the complexification of Part XV in which imaginary displacements are Jacobi fields: the spin channel is an imaginary displacement in $E_{\mathbb C}$, which the theory already has, and no new internal direction is attached; the two-imaginary-units firewall below is resolved for this pair, and the flavor $i$ is not needed for it. Second, the geometry's complex pairing is $(\delta,a)$ — one polar, one axial, the real and imaginary parts of one complex axial datum rendered in two representation types, which is the thesis of §XIX.1 realized on the theory's own data — and *not* the transverse pair $(Q,\delta)$ of version 24.2, which is withdrawn as the pre-rendering pairing; the $(Q,\delta)$ exchange of §XIX.4 stands as what it was, an observation about rendered readouts. Third, the discrete structure: multiplication by $i$ sends $(\delta,a)\to(-a,\delta)$, Schwarzschild to extremal Kerr and back, with the sign of $a$ undetermined — the same sign as before — and conjugation $\zeta\to\bar\zeta$ reverses the spin sense, which is the antilinear $\rho_E$ of Part XV that reverses the rotation sense of the conjugate lift **[D]**. The soldering the construction asks for is now explicit: the channel bundle is the complexified tangent bundle of the rest space, ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2\simeq\mathbb C\otimes T\Sigma_{\rm rest}$, with Newman–Janis as its instance; establishing that identification canonically, so that the rest-space spin connection acts on $\mathcal F_2$, is the theorem target that would make history transport rotate the channel state with no new connection **[S]**.

**XIX.2c Why the doublet and the charge share one budget: Law I's own symmetry [T/V/D].** The reconciliation the Kerr instance demands — the theory owns a $\mathbb C^3$ carrier with compact symmetry, yet Kerr pairs spin with gap and leaves charge outside — is supplied by Law I. Its stationary sector, the Ernst equations of Einstein–Maxwell, carries the Kinnersley symmetry $SU(2,1)$, whose target space $SU(2,1)/S(U(2)\times U(1))$ is the *non-compact dual* of $\mathbb{CP}^2 = SU(3)/S(U(2)\times U(1))$ **[T]**: the flavor picture's $\mathbb{CP}^2$ and Law I's Ernst space are the compact and split real forms of one complex symmetric space — the motif of Part XVII, met again. The Harrison subgroup charges a vacuum solution, and applied to Kerr it can be computed on the axis, where the Kerr Ernst potential is Schwarzschild's with an imaginary shift of the axis coordinate, $\mathcal E(z) = (z-M_0+ia_0)/(z+M_0+ia_0)$ — the Newman–Janis displacement at the level of the Ernst data **[V]**. Charging with parameter $c = \tanh(b/2)$, restoring $f\to1$ at infinity and removing the constant potential there, the asymptotics give exactly

$$M = M_0\cosh b,\qquad Q = M_0\sinh b,\qquad a = a_0,\qquad \delta = \delta_0$$

**[V]**. So the mass–charge pair is a *split* pair, boosted with $M^2-Q^2 = M_0^2$ invariant, and the gap–spin pair $\zeta = \delta+ia$ is a *compact* pair, rotated by $i$ with $\delta^2+a^2$ invariant and left exactly untouched by the boost. **The Kerr–Newman budget $M^2 = a^2+Q^2+\delta^2$ is the equality of the two invariants,** $M^2-Q^2 = |\zeta|^2$: the split invariant of the mass–charge pair equals the compact norm of the gap–spin pair. This is the answer to the question the Kerr instance raised: the three real components of the budget are one genuinely complex degree of freedom, $\zeta$, whose imaginary unit is spacetime's own, and one hyperbolic degree of freedom, the Harrison boost that trades mass for charge — not three equivalent channels of one $\mathbb C^3$. And both structures are the theory's: the compact real form $\rho$ of Part XVII acts on $\zeta$ by conjugation, reversing the spin sense, and the split real form $\sigma$ is where the boost lives. (The other generators complete the picture in the standard way: the Ehlers rotation pairs mass with NUT charge and electric–magnetic duality pairs $Q$ with $P$, so that in general $\delta^2 = |M+iN|^2-|Q+iP|^2-a^2$ — compact pairs inside, the one split relation between them **[T]**.) Whether the flavor $\mathbb{CP}^2$ has any independent role now that its non-compact dual is found in Law I is open; the conservative reading is that it has none.

**XIX.2d The canonical map from the conjugate pair, and the soldering, computed [T/V/D/S].** The soldering is the standard spinor–vector map $\psi\otimes\phi\mapsto\psi^{\rm T}\epsilon\,\boldsymbol\sigma\,\phi$, ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2\to\mathbb C\otimes\mathbb R^3$, equivariant under $SU(2)\to SO(3)$ — checked on random elements **[V]** — so the spin connection of the space the shell is built on (Part I constructs the shell on the normal space of the worldline) acts on $\mathcal F_2$ through it. A single lift's vector ${\rm vec}(\psi,\psi)$ is null, and the axis $n_1\times n_2$ of its plane is the Bloch vector of $\psi$ — the lift's base point on $S^2$ **[V]**: the axis of a coherent channel vector is a spatial direction the shell already assigns. The conjugate lift is the quaternionic structure $j:(\alpha,\beta)\mapsto(-\bar\beta,\bar\alpha)$, antilinear and $SU(2)$-equivariant, with $j(e^{i\chi}\psi) = e^{-i\chi}j(\psi)$, and ${\rm vec}(j\psi,j\psi) = -\overline{ {\rm vec}(\psi,\psi)}$ **[V]**: **the conjugate lift of Part XV generates exactly the conjugate null vector**, so the pair $(\psi,j\psi)$ spans $\{v,\bar v\}$ and the map from the pair into the carrier is

$$\Phi(\psi,j\psi;A,B) = A\,{\rm vec}(\psi,\psi)+B\,{\rm vec}(j\psi,j\psi) = Av-B\bar v,$$

canonical up to the coefficients. Two things follow. Under the Hopf phase $\psi\to e^{i\chi}\psi$ the two terms turn oppositely, and the pair-built vector transforms by a *real rotation about the lift's axis by $2\chi$* **[V]**: on a single lift's carrier the Hopf phase is a common phase, but on the pair-built vector it is a rotation of the real channel vector about the axis — which is how the existing Hopf transport acts on the channel state once the state is the pair's, answering the strongest question above in the affirmative for pair states **[D]**. And with coefficients $(A,B) = (e^{\eta/2},e^{-\eta/2})$ the polarization is exactly $p = {\rm sech}\,\eta$ **[V]**; the proposal that $\eta$ is the pair's scale character of Part XV is the natural one and is marked as such **[S]** — equal weights, $\eta = 0$, give a real vector in the plane of $v$, and the Kerr–Newman states are the equal-weight pair states with $\zeta = \sqrt2A$ the common complex coefficient **[D]**. Finally, which complex axial number is the canonical one: the Kerr–Newman axis data are $\mathcal E(z) = (z-M+ia)/(z+M+ia)$ and $\Phi(z) = Q/(z+M+ia)$ **[V]**, whose pole sits at the complex point $z = -M-ia$ independently of $Q$, while the horizon rod is $[-\delta,\delta]$; under Harrison charging $M\to M_0\cosh b$ moves the pole and leaves $\delta$ and $a$ fixed. So $\zeta = \delta+ia$ is the charging-invariant axial datum and the pole datum $M+ia$ is the rendered, charge-dependent one **[D]** — the invariance argument for $\zeta$, short of the theorem that it is one canonical source variable.

**The axis, and the discrete group [D/V].** The rendering axis is not one global normal but the solution-selected local axis $[N]_{\rm sel}$ of §XVII.9 — $[N_{\rm gal}]$ at Galaxy scale, $[J]$ for a Kerr hole — and the polar–axial firewall of Part XVII stays: $[N]_{\rm sel}$ chooses the distinguished channel *line*, and an additional axial datum determines whether that channel renders as spin and with which orientation. Choosing the line breaks $SO(3)\to SO(2)$; the quarter turn $R_{\pi/2}$ generates $\mathbb Z_4\subset SO(2)$ and exchanges the two transverse intensities exactly, and the plane reflection $j$ commutes with rotations about the axis, $jR_{\pi/2}j^{-1} = R_{\pi/2}$ **[V]**, so the discrete group is $\mathbb Z_4\times\mathbb Z_2$. Part XVIII's lesson stays explicit: rotating the *state relative to the role frame* repacks; rotating state and frame together is gauge and cannot.

**The strongest question, answered as far as it can be [D/S].** Can the existing Hopf phase and history transport act on the channel state so that relative phase decides the roles? On a single lift's carrier the Hopf phase cannot: it is the common phase, and the relative phases are inert under it; on the pair-built vector of §XIX.2d it rotates the channel vector about the pair's axis by twice the holonomy, so the existing transport does act once the state is the pair's. The split real form's scale transport cannot either, and version 24.2's suggestion is withdrawn: it is $\mathbb R^\times$, real and noncompact, and modifies scales, not a $U(1)$ phase. The conservative source of relative phase remains the flavor one of version 24.1, $\psi(s) = e^{-isf(B)}\psi(0)$, different eigenchannels acquiring different history phases, to be related to the complex connection by a later theorem if at all. What the existing geometry *can* do is rotate: once the soldering above is canonical, the rest-space connection acts on the vector representation and transport along a history rotates the channel state relative to the rendering frame, and the interference formula repacks the budget — with no new connection, given that identification **[S]**.

**Two complex structures, kept apart [D].** $E_{\mathbb C}$ of Part XV is the analytic continuation of canonical spacetime geometry; $\mathcal F_x\simeq\mathbb C^3$ is an internal channel space. Their imaginary units are different mathematical roles, and they are not identified until a theorem does it — the firewall that answers the author's question about leaving the complex data in the complexified-spacetime slot or quotienting it away: the flavor $i$ is quotiented by the moment map in the output, and the spacetime $i$ is not.

**The sharpened thesis.** Not three channels before three roles, but **three complex amplitudes before three physical roles**: a centrade state $[\psi]\in\mathbb{CP}^2$, its phase-blind moment $(w_1,w_2,w_3)\in\Delta^2$, and a rendering basis $U^{(R)}$ that assigns the roles — with $U(1)$ common phase of weight two and — under the rendering hypothesis — $SO(3)$ on frames broken to $SO(2)$ by the selected axis, in place of $SU(3)$-type mixing and $S_3$; and, from the Kerr construction, the spin–gap pair as one complex axial datum $\zeta = \delta+ia$ whose imaginary unit is spacetime's own.

## XIX.3 What Part XVIII dictates [D]

The permutation acts on the projection functor, not on $g$ by $g\to\Omega^2g$: same deeper centrade state, different selection map, different effective spacetime physics. So the branch never demands $g_G' = g_G$ while $a$, $Q$, $\delta$ change, and its invariant is not a metric.

## XIX.4 The $\mathbb Z_2$ step, tested: charge against nonextremality [V/D/S]

The two static channels each have a curvature readout in our rendering, and the readouts have the same fall-off. The nonextremality channel reads the optical trace, $R_{\rm opt} = -6\delta^2/r^4$ (§XVIII.2). The charge channel reads the four-dimensional Ricci tensor of Reissner–Nordström, $R^a{}_b = (Q^2/r^4)\,{\rm diag}(-1,-1,+1,+1)$, traceless, computed here **[V]**. Their budget is

$$\frac{\delta^2}{r^4}+\frac{Q^2}{r^4} = \frac{M^2}{r^4} = \Big(\frac{M}{r^2}\Big)^2$$

— the square of the Newtonian field. With $E_g = M/r^2$ and $E_Q = Q/r^2$, the optical trace is $-6(E_g^2-E_Q^2)$, the matter Ricci is $E_Q^2$, and extremality is $E_g = E_Q$, the balance of gravity and Coulomb repulsion that Majumdar–Papapetrou superposes. The swap $Q\leftrightarrow\delta$ is, so far, an involution of *magnitudes*: $Q' = \sqrt{M^2-Q^2}$, $\delta' = |Q|$, so extremal Reissner–Nordström maps to Schwarzschild and back; but the full solution carries $F\propto Q$, and turning $\delta$ into a charge requires choosing $Q' = \pm\delta$, which nothing in the squared simplex decides — the same sign the multi-hole sector discovered. What is established is a parameter-space involution, matched $r^{-4}$ curvature amplitudes, and closure under the field equations **[V/D]** — the permuted hole is still Reissner–Nordström, which item seven of the program requires, though this is not yet restrictive, since any $|Q'|\leq M$ is another solution. That the optical trace and the electromagnetic Ricci are *two readouts of one thing* remains a proposal **[S]**. The minimal typed object that would make it more: in the language of §XIX.2, take $\mathbb K = [z]$ with $z_\delta$, $z_Q$ the two static amplitudes, the involution $S$ the Weyl transposition of the two channels, and the projections $P_\delta:|z_\delta|^2\mapsto-6|z_\delta|^2/r^4$, a three-dimensional scalar, and $P_Q:|z_Q|^2\mapsto(|z_Q|^2/r^4)\,{\rm diag}(-1,-1,1,1)$, a four-dimensional tensor. Then $S$ acts on an unlabelled object and $P_\delta\circ S$, $P_Q\circ S$ exchange which amplitude each representation type reads — the amplitudes exchange, the types are attached by the projections **[D]**. What would make this a result rather than a construction is that the projections be canonical, which is open; if even the $\mathbb Z_2$ case cannot be lifted to such an object canonically, the full $S_3$ is dead. (§XIX.2b finds that the geometry's own complex pairing is $(\delta,a)$ along the axis, not $(Q,\delta)$; the present exchange is a rendered-level observation and is kept as such.)

## XIX.5 The spin channel, and why $S_3$ cannot act on readouts [D]

The spin channel's readout is of a different kind — though not, as version 24 first said, of a different fall-off. All three readouts go as $r^{-4}$: the optical trace $-6\delta^2/r^4$, a three-dimensional scalar; the Reissner–Nordström Ricci $(Q^2/r^4)\,{\rm diag}(-1,-1,1,1)$, a four-dimensional parity-even symmetric tensor, not a scalar; and the gravitomagnetic tidal field $\sim J/r^4 = aM/r^4$, an axial tensor — their squared invariants all $\sim r^{-8}$. What differs is representation type, parity and orientation content, and parameter dependence: the spin readout is linear in the axial $J$ where the others are quadratic in scalars. The Finsler residual of §XVIII.9 — charge cancelling the optical trace at extremality, spin not — is this asymmetry seen optically. So there is no obvious $S_3$ acting on the rendered curvature objects; if the conjecture is true the symmetry is pre-rendering only, exactly as the firewall requires — and this is the stronger form of the intuition, since triality could then only mean one abstract channel sent to different representation types (§XIX.1). The permutations involving $a$ cannot be tested by exchanging readouts; they need the pre-rendering object first.

## XIX.6 The spectral test [V]

The author's dimensional intuition has a precise form to test: in three dimensions the Riemann tensor is the Ricci tensor, a symmetric operator with three eigenvalues, so an $S_3$ on eigenslots is natural — does the budget operator map canonically into the spectral data of the optical Ricci? The bare numerology fails at the first hurdle: by spherical symmetry the optical Ricci of Reissner–Nordström has two distinct eigenvalues, radial and angular, not three **[V]**. At the photon sphere $r_{\rm ph} = (3M+\sqrt{9M^2-8Q^2})/2$ they are $(-2/27,0,0)/M^2$ for Schwarzschild and $(-1/16,1/32,1/32)/M^2$ at extremality — the angular eigenvalue vanishing exactly on the Schwarzschild photon sphere, a curiosity worth recording — and they vary with position where $a$, $Q$, $\delta$ do not, so any bridge needs a canonical evaluation surface or a global spectral invariant. This is a kill test, not an axiom, and the "three because space is three-dimensional" reading is the last item of the program, not the first.

## XIX.7 Selection of the role assignment [S]

If all six permutations define valid renderings of the same deeper centrade network, they need not support the same relational complexity. With the microstate measure of §XVIII.8, our role assignment could dominate, $\mu(R_{\rm ours})\gg\mu(R_\pi)$, because the observer-supporting centrade subgraph fails to be invariant under channel triality. Then the familiar assignment is not written into the laws; it is the overwhelmingly populated macrostate — intuition 29 applied to representation type rather than to orientation.

## XIX.8 Program and kill tests [D]

**The program, in order.** Construct the abstract channel space $\mathcal C$ and an $S_3$ action without using the labelled $a$, $Q$, $\delta$; recover the invariant budget $u_1^2+u_2^2+u_3^2 = M^2$; show that one selection map reproduces Kerr–Newman; apply $Q\leftrightarrow\delta$ and decide whether the exchanged readout is a rendering change — begun in §XIX.4; only then permutations involving $a$; search for the common pre-rendering operator; require every permuted rendering to satisfy consistent field equations, which macrostate rarity cannot rescue — passed for the $\mathbb Z_2$ step; and last, whether the three-channel structure is canonically related to three-dimensional spatial curvature — begun negatively in §XIX.6. The next calculation is structural: the carrier is found in existing data (§XIX.2a) and the Kerr construction fixes its instance (§XIX.2b); the charge question is answered by Law I's own symmetry (§XIX.2c) — the budget is a split invariant meeting a compact norm — and the canonical map from the pair and the soldering are computed (§XIX.2d), and what remains, in order, is the derivation of the pair's coefficients from Part XV's histories — the scale character being the candidate — the descent of the carrier through the meridian circle, and the theorem that $\zeta$ is one canonical source variable, of which its charging invariance is the first half. **Kill tests:** if no unlabelled object with a genuine $S_3$ action exists, the conjecture is an algebraic symmetry of one equation; if the $\mathbb Z_2$ exchange cannot be realized as a rendering change, the same; if a permuted rendering violates the field equations, it is out; if the spectral bridge needs a hand-chosen surface, it is numerology.
