# PART XXI — FROM THE ONE-PARTICLE BUNDLE TO A LOCAL MATTER FIELD: THE VERSION-26 BRANCH [T/D/S/O]

*Part XX ends with a candidate classical one-particle spinor kinematics and no derived quantum electron field. The problem that remains is sufficiently different in kind — an action, locality, statistics, a vacuum — that it opens a version rather than a patch. Nothing in Parts 0–XX is touched.*

## XXI.0 Two bundles, kept apart [T/D]

Part XX constructed a one-particle spin-state structure over the mass shell, $\mathcal S_{\rm spin}\to\mathcal P_m$, where $p$, the Wigner rotations, $u_s(p)$ and $v_s(p)$ naturally live. A Dirac *field* is not a section over phase space. The field-theoretic object is a rank-four complex bundle over representational spacetime, $S_D\to B_{\rm rep}$, with $\Psi\in\Gamma(S_D)$, and the mass shell reappears only when its free solutions are mode-decomposed,

$$\Psi(x)\sim\sum_s\int_{\mathcal P_m}\big[a_s(p)\,u_s(p)\,e^{-ip\cdot x}+b_s^\dagger(p)\,v_s(p)\,e^{+ip\cdot x}\big]\cdots$$

after quantization. The transition the branch must make is $\mathcal S_{\rm spin}\to\mathcal P_m$ (one-particle kinematics) $\longrightarrow$ $S_D\to B_{\rm rep}$ (local spacetime field), and it is not made by quantizing the $\mathbb{CP}^1$ bundle over the mass shell directly.

## XXI.1 The first question, and its topological half [T/D/O]

*Does the theory's shell construction canonically produce a rank-four Dirac bundle $S_D\to B_{\rm rep}$ and a local action on its sections?* The existence half is answered by topology. The representational section is $\Sigma_{\rm rep} = S^3(\ell)$, parallelizable and simply connected, so the spin structure on $\mathbb R\times S^3$ is unique up to equivalence and its complex Dirac bundle is trivializable **[T]**: there is no topological ambiguity in the spacetime spinor bundle. "Trivializable" does not mean a preferred trivialization, and uniqueness of the spin structure does not by itself show that the shell construction has globally produced *that* bundle rather than a canonically isomorphic representation structure along worldlines; the complex shell's $SL(2,\mathbb C)$, attached along each worldline by Part I, is the Lorentz spin group of the structure, and identifying the shell construction globally with the spacetime bundle is the remaining equivariant gluing step **[D/O]**. On the gravity base the situation is richer and is the theory's own: $\Sigma_{\rm grav} = \mathbb{RP}^3(\ell) = SO(3)$ is parallelizable and spin, but $H^1(\mathbb{RP}^3,\mathbb Z_2) = \mathbb Z_2$, so it carries exactly *two* spin structures, distinguished by the sign a spinor acquires under the deck transformation — under the twin. Since matter lives upstairs on $B_{\rm rep}$, this is a descent question, not a question of which structure fermions on $B_{\rm grav}$ select: **the two $\mathbb{RP}^3$ spin structures classify the possible spinorial descents of matter on the cover, and for spinors the twin theorem's "same-sign antipodal pair" becomes a choice of descent**, twin-even or twin-odd **[T/D]**. The choice is spectrally visible: the Dirac spectrum on the round $S^3$ is $\pm(k+\tfrac32)/\ell$ with multiplicity $(k+1)(k+2)$, symmetric under $\lambda\to-\lambda$, while each of the two $\mathbb{RP}^3$ structures keeps half the modes with mirror-image *asymmetric* spectra **[T]** (Bär); which levels each keeps, and which descent, if any, the theory's laws select, is the branch's first computation, not an assertion. The action half is not derived: the standard local action $S[\Psi,\bar\Psi] = \int_{B_{\rm rep} }\sqrt{-g_{\rm rep} }\;\bar\Psi(i\gamma^\mu D_\mu-m)\Psi$, with $D_\mu$ the spin connection of $g_{\rm rep}$ and the Maxwell coupling of Law IV, is *available* on the bundle **[T]**; whether the theory's laws produce it, rather than the branch appending it, is the question **[O]**.

## XXI.2 The statistics firewall [S/O]

A classical Dirac equation does not by itself say to quantize with Fermi statistics; that is the spin–statistics structure of a full quantum field theory. The anti-ad-hoc rule that carried Parts XIX and XX applies with full force: **Grassmann variables are not appended merely because Dirac fields normally use them.** The branch first asks whether the theory's doubled, history and projective architecture contains any mathematical reason for antisymmetry — the twin sign of §XXI.1 is a candidate to examine and is not one to assume, since the sign under the deck transformation is the $2\pi$ rotation sign of §XX.4, not an exchange statistic. If no such reason is found, the branch imports spin–statistics as standard field theory and says so **[S/O]**.

## XXI.3 Program and kill tests [D]

| grade | contents |
|:--|:--|
| **standard** | spin structures and $w_2$; the two spin structures of $\mathbb{RP}^3$; the Dirac spectrum on $S^3$ and its quotients; the Dirac action and mode expansion; spin–statistics as a theorem of quantum field theory |
| **derived here** | the spacetime spinor bundle on the cover topologically unambiguous — unique spin structure, trivializable Dirac bundle — with the global identification of the shell construction with it the remaining gluing step; the twin sign as the choice of spinorial descent to the gravity base, spectrally visible |
| **proposed [S]** | that matter on $B_{\rm rep}$ occupies $S_D$; that the theory's laws produce the local action; that the architecture contains a reason for antisymmetry |
| **open** | the action from the laws; the global gluing of the shell construction to the spacetime spinor bundle; the twin-sign descent and the $\mathbb{RP}^3$ spectrum; occupation; statistics; the vacuum and the particle–antiparticle reading; the full spacetime discrete symmetries on the field |

**Program, in order:** compute the Dirac spectrum on $\mathbb{RP}^3(\ell)$ for both spin structures and state which descent the twin sign selects, if the theory decides it; derive, or fail to derive, the local action on $S_D$ from Laws I and IV; only then ask why the field is fermionic. **Kill tests:** if the action can only be appended, with no relation to the theory's laws, the branch is imported field theory on a spin manifold the theory happens to supply; if the twin sign is physically undetermined, the theory has an unresolved $\mathbb Z_2$ in its matter sector; if statistics must be appended, the branch says so and stops claiming to derive matter.

## XXI.4 Dual-factor lepton mass clue [T/V/S/O]

*A prospective mention, tested. The question is whether an empirical Koide-type charged-lepton mass structure can arise from geometry the theory already has, in the order geometry $\to$ two forced dual factors $\to$ three-state cyclic spectrum $\to$ Koide $2/3$, and not the reverse. Part XX is not touched, and Part XIX's firewall stands: the shell's native symmetry is $SU(2)\times U(1)$ with Weyl group $\mathbb Z_2$, and no $U(3)$, $S_3$ or $SU(3)$ centre is revived.*

**The algebra is exact [T/V].** Do not take $\sqrt m$ as primitive. With $\theta_n = \theta+2\pi n/3$ and two positive angular factors $A_\pm^{(n)} = 1+\cos(\theta_n\pm\pi/4)$, evaluated at directions a quarter turn apart with $\theta_n$ their bisector, the identity $(1+\cos(\theta-a))(1+\cos(\theta+a)) = (\cos\theta+\cos a)^2$ gives at $a = \pi/4$

$$m_n = m_*A_-^{(n)}A_+^{(n)} = m_*\Big(\cos\theta_n+\tfrac1{\sqrt2}\Big)^2,$$

so the square-root form $\sqrt{m_n} = \sqrt{m_*/2}\,(1+\sqrt2\cos\theta_n)$ *emerges* on the branch where all three roots are positive. On that branch the $\mathbb Z_3$ identities $\sum_n\cos\theta_n = 0$, $\sum_n\cos^2\theta_n = \tfrac32$ give $\sum_nq_n = 3/\sqrt2$, $\sum_nq_n^2 = 3$ and hence $K = \sum m_n/(\sum\sqrt{m_n})^2 = 2/3$ **[V]**: the $2/3$ is downstream of the three-member orbit, the quarter-turn pair and the positive branch, and the $\sqrt2$ is $\cos(\pi/4)$. The branch matters: outside it the principal root introduces absolute values and $K\neq2/3$ — at $\theta = 0.35$ one root is negative and $K = 0.598$ **[V]**; the allowed $\theta$ set is a quarter of the circle **[V]**.

**Operator form, on the theory's own carrier [V].** Let $R$ be a rotation by $2\pi/3$ about any axis in the spin-one carrier ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2$ of §XIX.2a. Then $R^3 = 1$ with eigenvalues $\{1,\omega,\omega^2\}$ — the vector representation restricted to a $120^\circ$ rotation is the regular representation of $\mathbb Z_3$ **[V]** — and with $U_\theta = e^{i\theta}R$, $H = {\rm Re}\,U_\theta$, the factors $A_\pm = I+{\rm Re}(e^{\pm i\pi/4}U_\theta)$ commute and satisfy exactly $A_-A_+ = (H+I/\sqrt2)^2$, whose eigenvalues are the $m_n/m_*$ above **[V]**. The candidate flavor mass operator is $M_F = m_*A_-A_+$, positive semidefinite, and its eigenvectors are the axis itself and the two circular transverse combinations $e_1\pm ie_2$ — the weight basis $m = 0,\pm1$ of the selected axis **[V]**: the three sectors would be the axial channel and the two circular transverse channels of Part XIX. Both the quarter turn and the third turn are elements of the stabilizer circle $SO(2)$ of the selected axis, and they commute; Part XIX's $\mathbb Z_4$ is there for its own reason (§XIX.2), while the $\mathbb Z_3$ is *available as a conjugacy class of the native $SO(3)$ and not forced by anything* — choosing an order-three element is where "three" enters, and it is typed as such: a subgroup of the native action, chosen, not an independent flavor postulate and not a symmetry of the physical leptons **[S]**. The pair-built vector of §XIX.2d turns by $2\chi$ under a Hopf phase $\chi$, so a Hopf phase of $\pi/4$ is a quarter turn and $\pi/3$ a third turn — an observation, not a mechanism.

**The Born-overlap reading, and where the ${\rm Sym}^2$ bridge fails [V].** Each factor is twice a canonical $\mathbb{CP}^1$ Born weight of §XX.7: $1+\cos\phi = 2|\langle\psi|\chi\rangle|^2$. With two reference Bloch directions $\mathbf r_\pm$ a quarter turn apart in a plane and the three lepton directions at $120^\circ$ in the same plane, $m_n = 4m_*P_{n-}P_{n+}$ exactly **[V]** — the mass as the product of two projective overlaps against two dual references; the references are not orthogonal rays, since Bloch separation $\pi/2$ is overlap $\tfrac12$. On the symmetric square the expectation identity $\langle\psi^{\otimes2}|{\rm Sym}(P_-\otimes P_+)|\psi^{\otimes2}\rangle = \langle\psi|P_-|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|P_+|\psi\rangle$ holds exactly **[V]**, so the mass law is the expectation of one positive operator on the coherent Veronese states; but that operator is *not* $M_F$ — its spectrum on ${\rm Sym}^2$ is $(0,0,\tfrac34)$, $\theta$-independent, against $M_F$'s three masses **[V]**. So the operator form, whose eigenvectors are the weight states, and the coherent-state form, whose values sit on three directions of the plane, are two distinct constructions that agree on the values and are not yet one object. The coherent-state form uses exactly the single-spinor states Part XIX found insufficient as Kerr–Newman channel states: a different use of the same carrier, not a conflict, and it is an open question whether the same carrier can serve both. The stereographic identity $1+\cos\phi = 2/(1+r^2)$, $r = \tan(\phi/2)$, is recorded as a clue only; the local Bloch factor is not the global $S^3(\ell)$ factor, by the firewall of §XX.4.

**Against the physical leptons [V].** The measured masses give $K = 0.666661$. The fit $\sqrt{m_n} = \sqrt{m_*/2}(1+\sqrt2\cos\theta_n)$ gives $\theta = 0.22227$ rad $= 12.735^\circ$ (Brannen's $2/9 = 0.2222$) and $m_* = 627.68$ MeV, inside the positive branch, with roots $2.379$, $0.580$, $0.040$ for $\tau$, $\mu$, $e$: the electron sits near the branch boundary, and its two dual factors are $1.040$ and $0.00078$ — the hierarchy is near-orthogonality of the electron's direction to one reference, in the Born sense.

**The rendering-covariant cycle [V/S].** Because the masses are unequal, no transformation leaves a nondegenerate $M_F$ invariant while permuting them. The cleaner statement exists exactly: a unitary $C$, the cyclic permutation of $R$'s eigenvectors, satisfies $CRC^{-1} = \omega R$ and $C\,M(\theta)\,C^{-1} = M(\theta+2\pi/3)$ **[V]** — the cycle maps one assignment of the three masses to the next, rendering to rendering, rather than being a symmetry of one rendered world. But $C\notin SO(3)$: it permutes the axis with the two circular states, which no rotation does **[V]**. If rendering covariance is the mechanism, it needs an operation the native geometry does not supply — consistent with Part XIX's firewall, and marked as the proposal it is.

**Audit and status.** No inserted $2/3$, no inserted $\sqrt2$, no $SU(3)$; the $\mathbb Z_3$ typed as an available-but-chosen subgroup of the native action; the positive branch identified and the physical point inside it; the same overlap geometry reused in a different associated representation, with the flavor degree of freedom *not* identified with the particle's spin state; the global and local spheres kept apart. Not met: the quarter turn is native but its use as the reference separation is a postulate; the mass operator's location in a matter action is open, since Part XXI has no action yet; and the predictive residue is exactly one relation — $K = 2/3$ — with $\theta$ and $m_*$ parameterized, no individual mass predicted. **Verdict:** an intriguing structural clue with exact mathematics on the theory's own carrier, short of a derivation. **Theorem target:** derive the order-three orbit and the quarter-turn pair from existing geometry — the stabilizer circle of the selected axis is where both live — and obtain $M_F = m_*[{\rm Re}(e^{i\theta}R)+I/\sqrt2]^2$ as the flavor mass matrix of the matter action of §XXI.1, at which point Koide's relation would be downstream geometry.

## XXI.4a Why three is potentially structural [T/V/S/O]

*A sharpening of §XXI.4's "available but chosen" $\mathbb Z_3$. The observation is not that an order-three rotation exists — any angle exists — but that the native carrier has a geometrically distinguished $1+2$ decomposition, and that order three is the only order matched to the carrier as a complete regular cyclic representation.*

**The weight spectrum, and the uniqueness of three [T/V].** Under a rotation by $\alpha$ about the selected axis the spin-one carrier $F = {\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2\simeq\mathbb C\otimes H_u$ has weight basis $|0\rangle,|+\rangle,|-\rangle$ with eigenvalues $1,\ e^{+i\alpha},\ e^{-i\alpha}$: the axial $m = 0$ state is fixed by every rotation about the axis, and the two circular transverse states carry conjugate phases — the carrier is $1\oplus2$ under the axis stabilizer before any label is attached. At $\alpha = 2\pi/3$ the spectrum is $\{1,\omega,\omega^2\}$, every irreducible character of $\mathbb Z_3$ exactly once: $F|_{\mathbb Z_3}\simeq{\rm Reg}(\mathbb Z_3)$. Nothing analogous happens for other orders: at $\alpha = \pi$ the spectrum $\{1,-1,-1\}$ duplicates a character of $\mathbb Z_2$; at $\alpha = \pi/2$ the spectrum $\{1,i,-i\}$ lacks the character $-1$ of $\mathbb Z_4$; and for $N\geq4$ exactly $N-3$ characters are missing **[V]**, because $\dim{\rm Reg}(\mathbb Z_N) = N$ and the carrier is three-dimensional. So a three-dimensional carrier is a complete regular cyclic representation only for $N = 3$ **[T]**. This is the disciplined form of "why no fourth generation": a $\mathbb Z_4$ can act on the carrier but cannot be fully represented there — a fourth generation would need another native state.

**Where the three comes from [D].** Not from $\dim_{\mathbb R}E = 6$, which no derivation here uses, and not from $a$, $Q$, $\delta$ as such. The chain is $\dim H_u = 3\to{\rm Spin}(H_u) = SU(2)\to{\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2\simeq\mathbb C\otimes H_u\simeq\mathbb C^3\to{\rm Reg}(\mathbb Z_3)$: the three is the dimension of the local rest space, carried through the representation rather than invoked numerologically. Part XIX's three channels live on this same carrier, so the two threes rhyme because one native carrier admits more than one reading — channel before role — and not because $e,\mu,\tau$ are $a,Q,\delta$ componentwise, which Part XIX's firewall forbids; if the readings share a source, the theorem sits before either set of labels.

**Full cyclic occupancy principle [candidate, S/O].** If the generation structure is a faithful cyclic action on the native carrier with every cyclic character represented exactly once and no unused or duplicated sector, then the order of the cyclic group equals the complex dimension of the carrier, hence $N = 3$ and $\theta_n = \theta+2\pi n/3$: the denominator of §XXI.4 would then follow from saturating the carrier by one regular representation rather than being inserted to reproduce Koide. The mathematics is immediate; the premise is not derived. The real question is why physical flavor should exhaust the native carrier as one regular cyclic representation — possible sources being locality or nondegeneracy of the future matter action, rendering covariance, a no-unused-sector minimality, the twin or conjugate structure, the selected-axis decomposition, or consistency of the mass operator with the Hopf structure — and no new principle is adopted because it yields three.

**The distinguished sector, and the conjugation that exchanges the other two [V/S].** The fitted form of §XXI.4 puts the largest root, the $\tau$, in the unshifted $n = 0$ sector. If $n = 0$ is the geometrically distinguished axial state, then $\tau\leftrightarrow|0\rangle$ carries the base phase $\theta$ alone and the transverse pair carries $\theta\pm2\pi/3$; at $\theta = 0.22227$, $m_* = 627.68$ MeV the sector masses are $1776.86$ MeV on $|0\rangle$ and $105.68$, $0.51$ MeV on the circular pair **[V]**. The pair has a native exchange: complex conjugation of the carrier — the real structure of $\mathbb C\otimes H_u$, the reversal of the rotation sense about the axis — fixes $|0\rangle$ and sends $|+\rangle\leftrightarrow|-\rangle$, and it acts on the mass operator *covariantly*, $K\,M(\theta)\,K = M(-\theta)$, exchanging the $e$ and $\mu$ sectors with the $\tau$ fixed **[V]**; the plane reflection through $N^\perp$ of Part XIII does not exchange them, acting as ${\rm diag}(1,1,-1)$. So the native pattern "$\tau$ fixed, $e\leftrightarrow\mu$ as conjugate sectors" exists, as a rendering covariance $\theta\to-\theta$ and not as a symmetry of one $M(\theta)$ — the unequal masses forbid that — and which of the pair is $e$ and which $\mu$ is the orientation convention of the axis. Whether the theory forces the heaviest lepton into the axial sector is the **distinguished-$\tau$ theorem target**: without a native criterion, "the $\tau$ gets $\theta$" is a relabelling.

**Two separate questions.** The $\mathbb Z_3$ explains the shifts $\pm2\pi/3$; the base phase does not follow from it. Empirically $\theta = 0.22227$ rad against $2/9 = 0.22222$, a difference of $5\times10^{-5}$ rad at pole masses — recorded as numerical, not derived, and not to be treated as exact until an independent equation fixes $\theta$ or the accuracy is established at the appropriate running scale. If the $\tau$ is the axial sector, the near-$2/9$ phase attaches specifically to the unique invariant sector rather than to an arbitrary member of a symmetric triplet, which is what would make it interesting; a strong future result would fix that phase relative to the quarter-turn references. And the literal cycle remains what §XXI.4 found: the native rotation supplies three spectral sectors, the operator $C$ that reassigns the masses lies outside the shell, and if the whole-universe cycle survives its home is the rendering groupoid.

**Kill tests.** The branch downgrades if the $\mathbb Z_3$ remains chosen only because three leptons are observed; if the occupancy premise has no source in the theory; if the $\tau$ privilege is only labelling; if a fourth native sector appears when the matter bundle is built; if flavor needs a carrier other than ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2$; if the transverse pair is treated as an exact symmetry; if $2/9$ is treated as derived; or if three spatial dimensions are invoked without the representation chain. **Exact result:** ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2|_{R_{2\pi/3} } = 1\oplus\omega\oplus\omega^2 = {\rm Reg}(\mathbb Z_3)$, with the generation interpretation **[S/O]**. **The two questions for the next pass:** why flavor should exhaust the carrier as the regular representation of a cyclic group, and why the invariant $m = 0$ sector should be the $\tau$ and whether its phase can be fixed.

## XXI.4b Flavor descent, the tensor-product audit, and the premise reclassified [D/V/S/O]

**Provenance, stated exactly.** The prospective three-slot carrier is not appended: it is the shell's native weight-two representation $\mathcal F_2 = E\times_{\rho_2}{\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2$, inherited from the canonical space's complex Hopf shell, with the Hopf circle acting by the already-derived weight two and the twin trivially (§XIX.2a). So the theory supplies the object on which the regular-$\mathbb Z_3$ fact occurs, and that is **[D/V]**; that matter interprets its sectors as generations is **[S/O]** — *carrier derived from the theory $\neq$ physical flavor occupation derived from the theory.*

**The obstruction, and the descent that removes it [D/V].** The carrier is the complexified spatial vector representation, $F\simeq\mathbb C\otimes H_u$, whereas a generation must be an internal Lorentz-scalar label: a literal $(e,\mu,\tau)\equiv(|+\rangle,|-\rangle,|0\rangle)$ would let a spatial rotation of a particle's state mix species — checked: the weights of $D(W)v$ relative to a *fixed* axis differ from those of $v$ **[V]**. The descent is the multiplicity-space construction. Once an axis $[N]$ is selected, the stabilizer $SO(2)_N$ acts on $F$ with the three weight lines $m = 0,\pm1$ as its isotypic components — each residual rotation acts on each line by a phase only **[V]** — and the generation space is

$$G_{\rm gen} := \bigoplus_{m\in\{0,\pm1\} }{\rm Hom}_{SO(2)_N}(\chi_m,F),$$

three canonical lines labelled by weight, on which the residual symmetry acts trivially by Schur. $G_{\rm gen}$ is isomorphic to $F$ as a vector space, given the axis, but carries the *trivial* action: the same carrier read as its own multiplicity space. The six questions of the descent are answered as follows. The unordered spectrum is not enough — it gives three numbers; the weight construction gives three lines. The lines are labelled canonically by weight, not by an ordering, and with an *unoriented* axis the labels are the axial sector and the transverse *pair*, which orientation of the axis splits into $m = \pm1$ — matching §XXI.4a exactly: $\tau$ canonical, $(e,\mu)$ separated only by the axial datum. Quotienting the conjugacy orbit by $SO(3)$ leaves the spectrum together with this local carrier, because rotating state and axis together preserves every weight **[V]** — the labels are covariant under rotations of the rendering. Transport over $B_{\rm rep}$ by the canonical connection of §XX.8a, which carries state and axis by the same rotations, preserves the sector exactly (weight content $(0,1,0)$ after fifty random transport steps **[V]**): **the canonical transport preserves the generation sectors and induces no off-diagonal flavor mixing**, so no new flavor connection is needed. That is weaker than flatness — it leaves open a diagonal holonomy of the sectors when the axis itself moves around a loop, which §XXI.4c computes. Boosts change $H_u$ by the Wigner rotation of §XX.8, which acts on state and axis alike, so the same holds; and the construction is Lorentz-covariant given the transported axis, with the residual action on $G_{\rm gen}$ trivial. **The flavor descent exists, conditional on a selected axis reduction**: once $SO(3)\to SO(2)_N$ has happened, the isotypic decomposition of the native carrier under the stabilizer converts the spatial $\mathbb C^3$ into three internal multiplicity labels with no independent flavor fiber and without letting ordinary rotations mix $e$, $\mu$, $\tau$ **[D]**. The reduction itself is the missing physics; §XXI.4d proposes where it comes from **[S/O]**.

**The tensor-product audit [D].** $S_D\otimes F$ fails: Lorentz acts on both factors and a rotation about a non-axial direction mixes the three components — a spinor times a vector, not three generations. $S_D\otimes G_{\rm gen}$ is the right object: three Dirac copies labelled by weight, Lorentz acting on $S_D$ and trivially on the label. The flavor mass operator is then typed as a function of the inherited weight-label operator $\hat m = {\rm diag}(-1,0,+1)$ on the multiplicity decomposition — not the generator of the residual $SO(2)$, whose action on $G_{\rm gen}$ is trivial, but the label it leaves behind —

$$M_F = m_*\Big(\cos\big(\theta+\tfrac{2\pi}{3}\hat m\big)+\tfrac1{\sqrt2}\Big)^2\in{\rm End}(G_{\rm gen}),$$

diagonal on the sectors with the eigenvalues of §XXI.4, a Lorentz scalar because the labels are invariant; its place is a local term $-\bar\Psi M_F\Psi$ in the matter action of §XXI.1, once that action exists **[S/O]**.

**The occupancy premise, reclassified [V/D/S].** With $G_{\rm gen}$ the weight decomposition, *three sectors need no $\mathbb Z_3$ at all*: the number of weights of $SO(2)$ on the vector representation is three because $\dim H_u = 3$. The order-three element enters only in the Koide phase structure — the sector phases $\theta+m\alpha$ for the rotation angle $\alpha$ of the mass unitary $U = e^{i\theta}R_\alpha$, and Koide's identities need them equally spaced, $\alpha = 2\pi/3$. Where that could come from is now exact: the spectrum of $M(\theta)$ is invariant under the one-step reassignment $\theta\to\theta+\alpha$ *if and only if* $3\alpha\equiv0$ — checked at $\alpha = \pi/2$, $1.0$ and $2\pi/3$ **[V]** — so "flavor exhausts the carrier as one regular cyclic representation" is equivalent to "the base phase is defined only modulo reassignment of the three sectors," which is Part XIX's *channel before role* applied to the sector phases **[D]**. That reformulation is a real gain: the premise is an existing principle's applicability, not a new one — whether the three sectors are equivalent channels before the rendering assigns their roles remains **[S/O]**, and no principle is adopted because it yields three.

**The quarter turn against the surviving $\mathbb Z_4\times\mathbb Z_2$ [V].** Among the dual pairings the axis's surviving discrete group generates from one reference direction — the same direction, its quarter-turn image, its half-turn image — exactly two give a $\theta$-independent Koide ratio within the positive branch: the trivial pairing, $K = 1/2$, and the quarter turn, $K = 2/3$; the half turn is $\theta$-dependent **[V]**. So the quarter turn is native to the axis and is the unique *nontrivial* pairing with an invariant ratio — but nothing yet selects it except its value, and the classification does not derive it; the mass relation stays a structural clue on this point.

**The $\tau$ criterion, and universality [V/O].** With the sectors typed, the axial sector is the heaviest exactly when $\theta\in(-\pi/3,\pi/3)$ **[V]**; since the base phase is defined modulo $2\pi/3$, "the $\tau$ on the axial sector" is the choice of the principal representative of $\theta$ — a convention unless an independent criterion fixes the representative, which is the distinguished-$\tau$ target of §XXI.4a and is not attempted before the occupation. $G_{\rm gen}$ itself is species-independent — three weights for any field valued in the carrier — so the same three slots are available to $(e,\mu,\tau)$, $(\nu_1,\nu_2,\nu_3)$, $(u,c,t)$ and $(d,s,b)$, while the dual-factor operator is charged-lepton-specific and is not imposed elsewhere. The near-$2/9$ phase is left last, as §XXI.4a says. **Verdict of the pass:** the flavor descent exists and the tensor-product problem is resolved, so "three generations descend from three-dimensional space through the shell representation" is a claim with a construction behind it — the three sectors being the three weights of the selected axis on the complexified rest space — with the occupation, the applicability of channel-before-role to the phases, and the quarter turn's selection still open.

## XXI.4c The axis bundle: holonomy of the sectors, and the remaining bridge [T/V/D/O]

**The family of stabilizers over the axis space [V].** Put $SO(2)_N$ over the whole sphere of oriented axes. The weight lines become three line bundles over $S^2$: $L_0(N) = \mathbb CN$, the complexified normal line, which is trivial; and $L_{\pm1}(N) = \mathbb C(e_1\pm ie_2)$, the $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$ tangent lines of the axis sphere at $N$, with $c_1 = \pm2$. Their holonomies, computed as discretized Pancharatnam phases around latitude loops of axes, are $0$ for $m = 0$ and $\mp\Omega$ for $m = \pm1$, $\Omega$ the enclosed solid angle — at $\Omega = 1.097$ and $4.006$, to four figures modulo $2\pi$ **[V]**. So the sectors are preserved along every path — no mixing ever — while the two transverse sectors carry *opposite geometric phases* under a loop of the axis and the axial sector carries none: the induced connection on $G_{\rm gen}$ is diagonal and not flat. Over the *unoriented* axis space the picture descends consistently: continuing the axial state along a path from $N$ to $-N$ returns it with the sign $-1$ — the complexified Möbius line, a $\mathbb Z_2$ holonomy on the $\tau$ line over $\mathbb{RP}^2$ — while the transverse states are exchanged, $|+(-N)\rangle\propto|-(N)\rangle$ **[V]**, so the transverse pair is one rank-two bundle over $\mathbb{RP}^2$ and the $e\leftrightarrow\mu$ exchange with the $\tau$ sign is the orientation-reversal covariance $\theta\to-\theta$ of §XXI.4a, read as holonomy.

**What is and is not observable [D].** The holonomies depend only on the loop's solid angle, or on its homotopy class over $\mathbb{RP}^2$ — conjugacy-invariant data, which is what the descent requires. They are diagonal, so charged-lepton mass eigenstates, superselected by mass in the free theory, never see them. A generation-*coherent* superposition would, and that is the neutrino sector: if the axis moved along a neutrino's path, the $\pm1$ sectors would acquire opposite geometric phases $\mp\Omega$ on top of the oscillation phases; with a fixed axis nothing is added. Two things must be kept apart here: the *absolute orientation* of $N$, which may be rendering-dependent like the local potential of a Berry connection, and the *holonomy of a changing* $N$, which is an invariant loop observable like the Berry phase. The first being unphysical is fine; the second is a killer empirical question — does whatever determines $N$ vary along real neutrino trajectories? If $dN = 0$ there is no extra phase; if not, the theory predicts a geometric contribution $\Delta\phi\sim\mp\Omega$ between the transverse sectors on top of the propagation phase — a constraint rather than an aesthetic issue, and a kill test rather than a fit.

**The remaining bridge: the axis itself [O].** For spin the theory supplies the whole sphere of directions as the slot and needs no preferred axis before spin exists. For flavor the decomposition $F = \chi_{-1}\oplus\chi_0\oplus\chi_{+1}$ requires the reduction $SO(3)\to SO(2)_N$, and the branch has not said what $N$ is for every matter field. It cannot simply be the Galactic axis; it cannot be a laboratory direction; and it should not be the particle's own spin direction, which would entangle flavor with spin. The ideal result is that $N$ is an auxiliary canonical order parameter — Part XVII's $[N]_{\rm sel}$ is the candidate — whose orientation is unobservable while its decomposition type $1\oplus1\oplus1$ survives as the internal generation structure: $N$ may choose a representation frame, but observable flavor must depend only on conjugacy-invariant data, which §XXI.4c shows the spectrum and the holonomies are. **Why every matter field possesses the required reduction, and what $N$ is physically, is now the central open problem of the flavor branch**, and it is where flavor still trails spin.

**Maturity, stated.** Spin: canonical representation geometry — shell, $SU(2)$, $\mathbb{CP}^1$, Berry, the $2\pi$ sign, Wigner and Thomas, Lorentz spinors, on-shell Dirac modes — with dynamics and occupation still open. Flavor: conditional representation geometry — shell, ${\rm Sym}^2\mathbb C^2$, three weights, $G_{\rm gen}$, three Lorentz-scalar slots, $M_F$, stationary rays — whose required reduction now has a native candidate source in the split real-form selection (§XXI.4d), still lacking a derived occupation, a derived action, a derived rather than fitted mass operator, a derived quarter turn and $\theta$, and a weak-interaction realization tying the labels to the actual $e,\mu,\tau$. The mysterious axis has been replaced by a pre-existing order-parameter candidate; the gap to spin has narrowed and is not closed. Neither is finished matter.

## XXI.4d The origin of the reduction: the split real-form selection [D/S/O]

*The referee's arrow — canonical geometry $\Rightarrow$ a universal but non-preferred $SO(3)\to SO(2)$ reduction with unphysical absolute orientation — has a candidate in the architecture that is not a spatial choice.* Part XVII established that the complex canonical geometry carries one compact real structure, unique, and a *family* of split real structures, $\mathcal R_{\rm split}\simeq SO(3)/O(2)\simeq\mathbb{RP}^2$, of which any real rendering must select a member, $\sigma_{[N]}$ — solution-selected, per history sheet, as a collective order parameter. §XX.4a then showed that the family is canonically and uniquely equivariantly identified with the space of unoriented lines of the rest space. Put together, and stated with Part XVII's own caution — that part remained an experimental branch, its kill criterion requiring the member to be *solution-selected* and not installed as a preferred plane: **the theory already contains an admissible split-real-form family, its rendering and solution branch provides a mechanism by which a member may be selected, and selecting a member is selecting an unoriented axis.** Where the selection occurs it is not preferred (the family is one homogeneous orbit, and the rendered physics is covariant under the choice — Part XVIII's rendering covariance) and orientation-free, since the selection is of a line; the orientation is the conjugate-lift sign of §XX.4, which is unphysical **[D, at the architectural level, for the structure; the selection mechanism is Part XVII's proposal]**. The new hypothesis, narrower than before, is then that **matter generation refers to that ambient solution-selected member** **[S]**.

**What the selection does to the carrier [V].** The stabilizer of the selected form is the $O(2)$ of the line. Its identity component fixes the axial state and multiplies the two circular states by conjugate phases; its other component sends the axial state to its negative and exchanges the circular states **[V]**. So under the full stabilizer of the *unoriented* selection the carrier is $1\oplus2$ — the axial sector canonical, the transverse pair an irreducible doublet — and choosing the orientation, $SO(2)$ inside $O(2)$, splits the doublet into $m = \pm1$: the $\tau$/$(e,\mu)$ pattern of §§XXI.4a–4c follows from the selection alone. In the language of the real form itself: $G_{\rm gen}$ is the weight decomposition of the carrier under the *maximal compact subgroup* of the selected split form, the rotations in its equilibrium plane, with no spatial-axis language needed. And the form's noncompact directions do something the compact ones do not: a hyperbolic element of $SO(2,1)_{[N]}$ mixes the axial sector into the transverse pair **[V]**. The compact transport preserves sectors (§XXI.4c); the split transport — Part XVII's scale sector, the other restriction of the one holomorphic connection — does not. That is where the sector-breaking that §XXI.5 showed neutrino mixing requires has a native home **[S]**, and it is the first structural suggestion of what $H_\nu$ might be.

**What this does and does not settle.** It removes the spatial choice: the reduction comes from the rendering of the complex geometry, an operation the theory already performs, and its orientation is already known to be unphysical. It does not settle the occupation — *why a matter field's generation structure is referred to the ambient selected form* rather than to something intrinsic to the field — which is the branch's hypothesis **[S/O]**; and it makes the ambient form the axis, so that within one history sheet $dN = 0$ and no geometric oscillation phase arises — at Galactic scale the selected line is the Galactic normal, constant across the disk — while a neutrino crossing between differently selected regions would see the holonomy of §XXI.4c, which is the kill test in its sharpest form. This is a good way to meet the objection that $N$ "cannot simply be the Galactic axis," not obviously the only one, and it does not make the spatial character disappear: **$N$ remains geometrically a line in $H_u$, but its value is solution-selected and its absolute orientation need not be a flavor observable** — which is acceptable exactly when all measurable flavor data depend only on the invariant decomposition and holonomy. **Environmental universality.** Generation behaves experimentally as an intrinsic internal property, not as a local polarization, so if the selected form belongs to a history sheet the theory must land in one of two regimes: either different selections $[N]$ are merely different realizations of one intrinsic generation structure, or $[N]$ can vary physically but its effects are confined to calculable mixing and holonomy that agree with observation. Anything stronger — environment-driven transmutation of a charged lepton carried between differently selected regions — fails at once. **Kill tests:** if the generation structure must be intrinsic to the field rather than referred to the ambient split form; if the ambient selection becomes observable through generation-coherent holonomy where it varies and no such effect exists; if the split-sector mixing predicts flavor change that is not seen. If the arrow holds, the claim that a generation index emerges from canonical geometry rests on structures the theory had before flavor was mentioned — the rendering of a complex geometry by a real one.

## XXI.4e The operator classes of the selected form, and the typing of $H_\nu$ [T/V/D/S]

**Typing.** A finite split transformation is not unitary on the positive-definite generation inner product, so the neutrino operator cannot be "a boost"; but the *generators* are the right objects. In the basis $(N,e_1,e_2)$ the selected form's algebra $\mathfrak{so}(2,1)_{[N]}$ has the compact generator $J_N$, with $\hat m = iJ_N$ Hermitian of eigenvalues $0,\pm1$, and the two noncompact generators $K_1,K_2$, which on the vector carrier are *real symmetric* and hence Hermitian **[V]**; they obey $\hat m^2 = I-P_N$ and $K_1^2+K_2^2 = 2I-\hat m^2$ **[V]**. So Hermitian operators can be built canonically from both parts of the decomposition, graded by degree in the generators.

**The classes [D/V].** The $SO(2)_N$-invariant Hermitian operators are exactly the functions of $\hat m$ — by Schur, since the weight spaces are lines — that is, ${\rm span}\{I,\hat m,\hat m^2\}$, which is *every* operator diagonal in the weight basis, since $\hat m$ has three distinct eigenvalues. So $M_\ell = aI+b\hat m+c\hat m^2$ with $(a,b,c) = (1776.86,\,-52.58,\,-1723.77)$ MeV **[V]** is not selective: it states only that the charged-lepton masses are diagonal in the compact weight basis — useful, because the compact class *cannot mix* and this is why $M_\ell$ can be non-mixing — while the Koide content lives entirely in the narrower functional form $M_\ell = m_*[\cos(\theta+\tfrac{2\pi}{3}\hat m)+1/\sqrt2]^2$ and the quarter-turn construction. The compact class explains why $M_\ell$ can be non-mixing; the dual quarter turn is what might explain its eigenvalues. The two statements are kept apart. The degree-one class $aI+b\hat m+\mathbf c\cdot\mathbf K$ breaks $SO(2)_N$; a rotation about $N$ carries $\mathbf c\cdot\mathbf K$ to any plane direction at the cost of phases the charged-lepton basis does not see, so it has one mixing parameter $t = |\mathbf c|/b$. It is excluded at current precision: the reflection $e_2\to-e_2$ fixes $K_1$ and maps $\hat m\to-\hat m$, forcing the $e$ and $\mu$ rows of $|U|$ to be images of each other for every $t$, and the best fit to the observed magnitudes leaves RMS $0.076$ **[V]**. The single-plane degree-two class $a\hat m+bK_1+cK_1^2+d\{\hat m,K_1\}$ is *projective* in its coefficients — an overall scale, like an added $\mu I$, moves no eigenvector — so it has three shape parameters against the four oscillation observables $(\theta_{12},\theta_{13},\theta_{23},\delta_{\rm CP})$, and must predict one relation. Fitting the three angles exactly, it does: **the predicted Dirac phase is $\sin\delta_{\rm CP} = 0$, $J_{\rm CP} = 0$** **[V]**, and adding the other compact term $\hat m^2$ leaves it so. The reason is a symmetry. The reflection $e_2\to-e_2$ composed with complex conjugation is an antiunitary operation that fixes $\hat m$, $K_1$, and hence every operator built from the compact weight and one split direction at any degree, $RH^*R = H$, and it fixes the charged-lepton weight basis as well **[V]**: both mass bases are invariant under one antiunitary map, a generalized CP symmetry, so — for nondegenerate spectra, and stated invariantly through the rephasing-invariant Jarlskog quantity — $J_{\rm CP} = 0$ for the whole single-direction class **[T/V]**. "Single direction" is invariant: $K(\phi) = \cos\phi\,K_1+\sin\phi\,K_2$ at any $\phi$ and any degree still conserves CP, since the residual rotation about $N$ carries $K(\phi)$ to $K_1$ and the symmetry survives **[V]**; a $K_2$ *component* is not what lifts it. What lifts it is **a second independent, non-collinear split direction** whose relative orientation the residual $SO(2)$ cannot remove: for $a\hat m+bK(\phi_1)+cK(\phi_2)^2$ the Jarlskog invariant vanishes at relative angle $0$ and $\pi$ (and at $\pi/2$, where the squared second direction is again reflection-symmetric) and is otherwise nonzero **[V]** — *CP violation measures irreducible two-direction structure in the split plane.* With two independent directions the class reproduces all four observables, unconstrained as five shape parameters must be **[V]**. The theorem's scope is exact: the argument uses a Hermitian propagation operator, so it constrains the oscillation angles and the *Dirac-type* invariant; Majorana phases, if neutrinos are Majorana — which the theory has not decided — lie outside it. The subfamily a single scale transport would generate, powers of *one* split generator with the compact weight, $a\hat m+bK+cK^2$, misses the three angles by $2.9^\circ$ and also conserves CP **[V]**. **So the classification is a dichotomy with teeth: one split direction forbids oscillation (Dirac-type) CP violation exactly; two independent directions are needed for it.** Current global fits prefer nontrivial values of $\delta_{\rm CP}$ in some analyses, with the preferred value depending materially on which datasets enter and the allowed ranges broad, and leptonic CP violation is not yet established: the single-direction prediction $J_{\rm CP} = 0$ is experimentally live and sharply falsifiable. If it is falsified, $H_\nu$ must carry two independent split directions. In the theory's own transport language the hierarchy is suggestive: the scalar scale character $\eta$ of Part XIV selects one direction and magnitude — mixing with CP conservation — while a transverse shear $\gamma_{\rm aniso}$ carries an oriented principal-axis structure. But a single shear has one principal axis, and the net symmetric part of *any* split transport is a single-direction boost **[V]**; the two-direction content of a history whose shear axis rotates lives in the relative orientation of successive steps, equivalently in the compact rotation about $N$ that non-collinear split steps accumulate — the split form's own Thomas rotation, $150^\circ$ in a test history **[V]**. A restricted $H_\nu$ that carries it would need the transport's second-order data, pairs of steps, not its net symmetric part. Shear is not to be called leptonic CP violation; whether its two independent components produce two non-collinear split generators is now a well-posed construction. The compact/split pair thus carries the natural division — compact directions stabilizing the charged-lepton sectors, noncompact directions supplying the sector-breaking that mixing requires — and the deeper unification is posed exactly: $M_\ell$ is compact by construction, and the question is which combinations of $K_1$, $K_2$, $\hat m$ the theory's existing split transport generates *before* the data are consulted, and whether they include the second direction **[S/O]**.

## XXI.5 Stationary modes: the generation sectors as critical rays, and the neutrino clue [T/V/S/O]

*The author's intuition is that the three lepton states might be equilibrium spots of a deeper substrate — analogous to the stationary Galactic sheet of Part XVI, with runaway oscillation on tilting away — and that reducing that substrate first and then varying the ordinary action should give the same physics as varying the whole: a commutativity of the two reductions. Its mature form is stationary-mode selection inside a still-quantum theory, not collapse and not a variable $\hbar$. The stationary-ray statement is automatic for any Hermitian operator with distinct eigenvalues, so its content lies entirely in where $M_F$ comes from; the new content is in the commutativity and in the neutrino clue.*

**Critical rays and Morse indices [T/V].** On the projective generation space $\mathbb P(G_{\rm gen})\simeq\mathbb{CP}^2$ define the Rayleigh functional $\mathcal E([z]) = z^\dagger M_Fz/z^\dagger z$. Its critical-point equation is $M_Fz = \lambda z$, so for nondegenerate eigenvalues the three mass rays are the only critical rays — checked for the §XXI.4 operator at the physical point: gradients $10^{-9}$ or below, and the Hessian on $T\mathbb{CP}^2$ has Morse index $0$ at the $e$ ray, $2$ at the $\mu$ ray and $4$ at the $\tau$ ray **[V]** — a perfect Morse function on $\mathbb{CP}^2$, with the lightest a minimum, the heaviest a maximum, the middle a saddle. So *stationary modes*, not three minima. Under the induced projective flow of $i\,dz/ds = M_Fz$ each eigenray is a fixed point while a superposition oscillates through $\mathbb{CP}^2$ with the mass differences (Fubini–Study excursions of order one over $s\sim0.01$ MeV$^{-1}$) **[V]**: three stationary projective modes, off-mode states in coherent oscillatory motion, nothing collapsed. The conjugation $K$ maps the critical rays of $\theta$ to those of $-\theta$ with $|0\rangle$ fixed and $|+\rangle\leftrightarrow|-\rangle$; the construction is covariant under change of the auxiliary axis; and on $G_{\rm gen}$ the critical rays are exactly the three sector lines, invariant under the residual symmetry **[V]** — the stationary picture survives the descent of §XXI.4b. Do not identify this with the Galactic sheet: only the variational pattern is shared.

**The neutrino clue, corrected and sharpened [T/V/D].** Neutrino flavor states are not the charged-lepton mass states; the flavor basis is indexed operationally by the charged-current channels, while propagation is in the neutrino mass basis, and $U_{\rm PMNS} = U_\ell^\dagger U_\nu$ is the mismatch of two stationary structures on one substrate. The natural place for this in the present construction is exact: $M_F$ is a function of the weight label $\hat m$ of the selected axis, $[M_F,\hat m] = 0$ **[V]**, so *any neutrino operator that is also a function of $\hat m$ commutes with it and gives trivial mixing*. Nontrivial mixing therefore requires $[H_\nu,\hat m]\neq0$: **the neutrino mass operator must break the residual symmetry of the charged-lepton axis** **[D]**. The next-simplest candidate — a function of a *tilted* axis's weight, which would make $U_{\rm PMNS}$ the spin-one rotation matrix $D^1$ between the two axes — is excluded at current precision: every row and column of $d^1(\beta)$ has two entries of equal magnitude, no row or column of the observed $|U_{\rm PMNS}|$ does, and the best fit over all assignments leaves a residual of $0.05$ in the sum of squares, an RMS of $0.08$ against uncertainties of a few hundredths **[V]**. So the neutrino sector needs structure on $G_{\rm gen}$ beyond a single axis — a constraint, and the branch's first prediction-shaped statement: if neutrino masses came from a second selected axis alone, mixing would have a symmetry it does not have. What $H_\nu$ is remains open; the surviving symmetries under which to classify it are the conjugation, the third and quarter turns, and rendering covariance.

**Commuting reductions [T/V].** Let the deeper action be $S[\Psi,\zeta]$ with $\zeta$ the internal variable, stationary branches $\zeta_i[\Psi]$ solving $\delta S/\delta\zeta = 0$, and branch effective actions $S^{\rm eff}_i[\Psi] = S[\Psi,\zeta_i[\Psi]]$. Then $\delta S^{\rm eff}_i/\delta\Psi = \delta S/\delta\Psi|_{\zeta_i}+(\delta S/\delta\zeta)|_{\zeta_i}\,\delta\zeta_i/\delta\Psi$ and the second term vanishes by stationarity: *reduce to a stationary branch, then vary, equals vary the full theory, then restrict to that branch* **[T]** — the exact form of the author's commutativity, with no collapse. On a three-branch toy, $S = \tfrac12a\Psi^2+b\Psi\zeta-\cos3\zeta$, the identity holds on every branch to $10^{-6}$ and the effective Hessian equals the Schur complement $S_{\Psi\Psi}-S_{\Psi\zeta}S_{\zeta\zeta}^{-1}S_{\zeta\Psi}$ **[V]**; it fails only where a branch Hessian degenerates — branch collision, the Stokes situation of Part XVI. At the path-integral level three nondegenerate branches give $Z\sim\sum_i\int\mathcal D\Psi\,\Delta_i^{-1/2}e^{iS^{\rm eff}_i/\hbar}$, which is what "three generations as three stationary branches of a deeper functional" would mean; the existence of three saddles does not by itself make three superselected species — their orthogonality, interactions and interference must still be derived **[S/O]**. No variable $\hbar$ is introduced: the control quantity is $\Delta S/\hbar$ or a Hessian eigenvalue, as in Part XVI, and global $S^3(\ell)$ histories are not invoked before a local bridge exists.

**Status and kill tests.** Standard: Rayleigh quotients and Morse theory on $\mathbb{CP}^2$, PMNS as a basis mismatch, the chain rule. Verified: the critical rays and indices for the §XXI.4 operator, the fixed-point flow, the descent, $[M_F,\hat m] = 0$, the exclusion of $D^1$, the three-branch identity and Schur complement. Derived: mixing requires the neutrino operator to break the charged-lepton axis's residual symmetry. Proposed: the three generations as stationary branches of a deeper functional; neutrino flavor as charged-current indexing of the derived sectors. Open: the functional itself, the occupation of the branches, $H_\nu$, and any relation to the aligned-path geometry. The branch stops if the stationary points exist only because a three-eigenvalue matrix was inserted, if the descent fails, if stationarity suppresses coherent superpositions, if neutrino flavor labels must be introduced independently, if the nested reduction changes the spacetime equations uncontrollably, if a variable $\hbar$ is needed, if global histories are invoked before the local bridge, or if one functional is claimed for both galaxy planes and flavor merely because both use stationary points.

| **complex Hopf lift** | $\mathbb{C}^\times\to SL(2,\mathbb{C})\to SL(2,\mathbb{C})/\mathbb{C}^\times$; $6-2 = 4$ ✓ |
| **physical real form** | $U(1)\to SU(2)\simeq S^3\to S^2$; the Hopf fibration, **recovered not assumed** |
| **why not bolted on** | not a coincidence of group names: right multiplication by $\operatorname{diag}(e^{i\theta},e^{-i\theta})$ sends the spinor column to $e^{i\theta}\psi$, so the shell's principal right $U(1)$ action coincides with overall spinor phase — not the spin group's left action (§I.6) |
| **why isotropic** | shell and lift are homogeneous; the fiber is a stabilizer, not a chosen longitude |
| **the centrepiece** | **spin geometry fixes the fiber's topology; gravity fixes only its metric length** |
| **Law I** | Einstein on $B_{\rm grav}$, source $T^{(g)}_b = \sum_{r^{-1}(b)}((dr)^{-1})^*T$ with $r = q\circ\iota$; also fixes $L_H = \Omega L_H^{\rm ref}$ |
| **the metric light feels** | $g^{(G)}_{\rm rep} = r^*g_G$, **not** $q^*g_G$ — the latter is degenerate along the meridian |
| **the arrow** | one-way: $g_G\mapsto L_H = \mathcal{F}[g_G]$. A scalar cannot reconstruct a metric, so no biconditional is claimed; and a future emergence result would be $\hat g\Rightarrow g_G$, not $L_H\Rightarrow g_G$ |
| **closure identity** | $GM_{\rm lift}/c^2\ell = \pi/2$, equivalently $GM_{\rm grav}/c^2\ell = \pi/4$ (§IV.2): a closure-radius comparison, not a horizon |
| **metric decomposition** | $6 = 3+1+2$ on the real shell ✓ — a block count of *components*, not propagating degrees of freedom. $3+1$ from Law I; the last **2 left open** |
| **black holes** | Schwarzschild, or Reissner–Nordström with the ordinary charge, as $R/\ell\ll1$ local comparison solutions; the exact closed-arena hole is open. No second charge |
| **twin theorem** | on $\Sigma_{\rm grav} = \mathbb{RP}^3(\ell)$, $\Delta+3$ has no kernel, since the dipoles are odd: one projective source is one same-sign antipodal pair on the cover (§IV.1) |
| **$v^2$ plateau** | $1.497\,GM_{\rm lift}/\ell = 2.994\,GM_{\rm grav}/\ell$ for a compact source, packing-independent; the Milky Way's own profile, taken isotropically, reaches 220 km/s at $\ell\approx13$ kpc (§IV.8) |
| **Keplerian limit** | correction $r^2/6\ell^2$: about $6\%$ at 10 kpc in the galactic arena — the plateau itself — and $\sim10^{-20}$ at 1 AU. The old $4\times10^{-15}$ belonged to the retired cosmological reading (§IV.10) |
| **Kerr** | complex worldline displacement, $a = J/M$; ring at $x^2+y^2 = a^2$; shells follow the displaced center |
| **rotation vs fiber** | rotation is **not** winding around the fiber; four jobs, four structures |
| **time** | an input, from the Lorentzian real structure |
| **Grassmann parity** | not derived, and blocked by type |
| **Chern class** | $\lvert c_1\rvert  = 1$ on every relevant linking shell, fixed by spin geometry; the **sign is induced globally** by orientation, embedding and restriction — not chosen per shell, not set by occupancy. Not mass-dependent, no $m_{\rm unit}$, no fork |
| **golf ball** | its shells carry exactly what Earth's do. No competition, no crossover, no winding counts |
| **free constants** | $G$ and the arena radius $\ell$, with $\Lambda = \ell^{-2}$ an internal arena constant — no cosmological parameter — plus whatever Law II's dynamics brings. $m_{\rm unit}$, $g_2$ and $\mu(\rho)$ all retired |
| **extremality** | $N\Omega = 1-(M^2-Q^2)/4r^2$ exactly for Reissner–Nordström: **extremal $\Leftrightarrow$ lapse–fiber balance.** The exact relation §III.2 retired is precisely the extremal sector; frozen matter is the sector with harmonic fiber factor and zero surface gravity (§X.2) |
| **bridge twin** | Reissner–Nordström's involution $(r,\hat n)\mapsto(\delta^2/4r,-\hat n)$ is a free isometry, the antipodal map of an $S^3$; quotient an $\mathbb{RP}^3$ geon; the twin recedes to infinity as $Q\to M$ (§X.3) |
| **two factors** | $\Omega = U_+U_-$, $U_\pm = 1+m_\pm/r$, $m_\pm = (M\pm Q)/2$: factor weights sum to $M$ and differ by $Q$; $N\Omega = 1-m_+m_-/r^2$ (§X.7) |
| **subextremality** | $\lvert Q\rvert\leq M\Leftrightarrow m_+m_-\geq0\Leftrightarrow N\Omega\leq1$: a positivity condition, not censorship, which is dynamical. A bridge-twin principle could make it exclusion (§X.7) |
| **ring, one dimension up** | a shift along the meridian gives a singular $S^2$ with charge-like $SO(3)$ symmetry — a singularity of the naked kind, not a horizon (§X.8) |
| **Kerr–Newman** | $Q^2+a^2+\delta^2 = M^2$; horizons $M(1\pm\sin m)$; the subextremal domain is the real range of $m$; no isotropic factors for spinning holes (§X.10) |
| **exact three-sphere** | a single-monopole Reissner–Nordström bridge on a *conformal* three-sphere, in standard Einstein–Maxwell — ends at the poles, throat at the equator. Misses this theory's base by exactly $6/\ell^2$; the closed-base version is nonlinear and open (§X.10) |
| **orbits** | $v^2 = r[m_+(r+m_-)^2+m_-(r+m_+)^2]/(r^2-m_+m_-)^2$; exactly $M/r$ at extremality (§X.10) |
| **rapidity coordinate** | $(M,Q) = \delta(\cosh\mu,\sinh\mu)$ on a timelike cone; factor weights $\tfrac\delta2e^{\pm\mu}$; real $\mu$ covers exactly the subextremal domain. A parametrization, not a mechanism (§X.11) |
| **Tully–Fisher (detached wing)** | not part of the model. Scoped as an optical rescaling rule; exploratory checks only: an arena law gives slope 4, the literal reading fails, and the surviving reading cannot be ordinary lensing magnification — whether it must break surface-brightness conservation or distance duality awaits an observable map (Part XII) |
| **canonical optics (v17 branch)** | an observed event fixed by exact positional agreement and resolution-limited directional agreement; coherence is controlled by what the detector and environment can distinguish — phase holonomy is the cleanest carrier, resolution-limited agreement another (§XIII.3) |
| **agreeing structure (v17 branch)** | rendering one point twice needs a correspondence; appearances forgetting $K_1$ and $K_2$ agree on $T/(K_1+K_2)$; the seed is the first instance (§XIII.4) |
| **dipole clue (v17 branch)** | the in-plane part of our velocity relative to the cosmic background lies $3.4^\circ$ from reversed solar motion, within $15$ km/s; its significance needs a stated null ensemble and is open — a clue, not evidence (§XIII.8) |
| **one structure? (v17 branch)** | not yet: no single literal involution, though an upstairs symmetry is not excluded; time reversal and the plane reflection genuinely shared; a pure phase or sign cannot dim a single ray, and three routes to darkness remain undeveloped (§XIII.8) |
| **bi-connection (20)** | two Weyl-type connections with exchange symmetry on the middle geometry, mean Levi-Civita, same unparametrized null geodesics; its scale character a homothety $e^{-2\int_\Sigma F_A}$ on a real loop, superseded in 20.2 since the real-loop flux vanishes; not the Jacobi squeeze but its $\mathbb R_+$ complement; sign, magnitude and universality open (Part XV) |
| **complex connection (20.5)** | constructed: the holomorphic $\rho_E$-equivariant $\mathbb C^\times$-connection is the analytic continuation of the Hopf connection; $A\vert _{TE} = 0$ (firewall), $F_A(v,Jw) = -F_H(v,w)$ (bridge, exact on the Hopf model); a class's scale character is a Hopf flux at first order, $2\epsilon\int\bar F_H(Y,k)\,ds+O(\epsilon^3)$, over the imaginary displacement $Y$, a Jacobi field vanishing at real endpoints — conjugacy the necessary gate; no new independent field, $F_A$ fixed by the full $\mathcal A_H$; on the local model the decisive component is $-k\cdot\nabla a_{\rm mer}$ in a meridian-invariant gauge, free; its moment along a class is symmetry-allowed exactly for opposite twin parities, the parity law derived from twin equivariance, the sign theorem $\bar F_H(N,k)\ge0$ open; the round smooth self-return in the scale kernel with the global-normal convention, which passes the recurrence-age test; scale kernel distinct from visual kernel; conditional on the descent (§XV.7, §XV.3) |
| **doubled transport (20)** | the closed-time-path dictionary maps the Born pair onto the Weyl pair line by line; one complex holonomy, phase for interference and scale for dilation; offloading two paths into two connections is exact, its obstruction the curvature (§XV.6) |
| **null-history principle (20)** | light and gravitational waves share cone, gate and class at leading eikonal order, differing as representations; test $\eta_{\rm EM} = \eta_{\rm GW}$, anchored by GW170817 (§XV.0, §XV.4) |
| **opening direction (22.2)** | at coincidence the blow-up's exceptional divisor records the opening direction $[Y]\in\mathbb P(K_\Xi)$, ill-conditioned near coincidence and free only to the extent of the Jacobi nullity — a circle in the round arena, a point per member in the lumpy one; axes in $\mathbb{RP}^2$ reach history openings by $\mathfrak p_\Xi:[N]\mapsto[P^\perp_\Xi N]$; the split real structures form one conjugacy orbit $SO(3)/O(2)$, the member solution-selected, so no plane is pre-installed; the Galactic plane at full scale and the Kerr equatorial plane in the aligned regime by one rule; the Newman–Janis displacement is a displacement into the split real form of $\sigma_J$, compatibility derived and selection open, the spin's perturbation $\sim a/r$ and its effect on the selected direction set by the orientation gap; the lumpy arena's round focus splits into two simple conjugate points with one-dimensional kernels (§XVII.9) |
| **matter (26.10)** | two bundles kept apart, $\mathcal S_{\rm spin}\to\mathcal P_m$ and $S_D\to B_{\rm rep}$; the spinor bundle on $\mathbb R\times S^3(\ell)$ topologically unambiguous, its gluing to the shell open; two spin structures on $\mathbb{RP}^3(\ell)$ classifying spinorial descents, the twin sign their choice, spectrally visible; the action and statistics open under the anti-ad-hoc rule; the dual-factor Koide clue — $m_n = m_*(\cos\theta_n+1/\sqrt2)^2$ on the spin-one carrier, $2/3$ downstream, the three as the weights of the selected axis on the native carrier — the generation space its multiplicity space, Lorentz-scalar, sector-preserving under transport with holonomy $\mp\Omega$ on the transverse sectors — the third turn as channel-before-role on the sector phases, the cycle not a shell operation; the reduction from the split real-form selection, orientation-free; the compact class every weight-diagonal operator, the split generators Hermitian and sector-breaking, degree one excluded, one split direction conserving oscillation CP exactly and two independent non-collinear directions required to violate it; the sectors as critical rays of Morse indices $0,2,4$, commuting reductions exact, and neutrino mixing requiring a symmetry-breaking $H_\nu$ with a single tilted axis excluded (Part XXI) |
| **spin histories (25.14, frozen)** | the fiberwise spatial-separation blow-up with divisor $\mathbb P(H_u)\simeq\mathbb{RP}^2$ and orientation cover $S(H_u)$ as the slot, ubiquitous as a candidate; the equivariant identification ${\rm Spin}(H_u)\simeq SU(2)_{\rm shell}$ with its Hopf–Bloch bundle as Part I's construction; the conjugate structure the orthogonal-partner involution; the shell's antipodal sign the $2\pi$ rotation, its identification with the global twin open; $\mathcal A_H$ the Berry connection exactly for the canonical representative, a pullback constraint on its deformations; the $2\pi$ sign, Born overlaps, Wigner rotation and exact Thomas precession on the shell's groups; the bundle $\mathcal S_{\rm spin}\to\mathcal P_m$ over $B_{\rm rep}$ with its connection constructed from the Levi-Civita spin lift and the $\rho$-horizontal boosts, passing the BMT benchmark for every $g$ with nothing added; the on-shell Dirac constraint the identity of the lift, $u = \sqrt m(B^{-1}\xi,B\xi)$, the negative-frequency $v$ family its graded $j$-conjugate with completeness in the paired mode space $\mathscr H^{\rm free}_p$, the representation $(g^\dagger)^{-1}\oplus g$ on the displayed blocks; the internal parity intertwiner $\rho$, time reversal $j$, charge conjugation the chirality-graded composite, the c-number $CPT$ composite the linear $-\gamma^5$; the counterfactual meridian-KK transport killed — leaking, non-BMT, $g = 1$ on null geodesics, half of Dirac's; the Weyl sectors algebraically available by a derived chirality index, the chirality identification conditional; the electron identification open; the axis space $\mathbb P(H_u)$ canonically the split family $\mathcal R_{\rm split}$, one directional slot read split-unoriented for Kerr's $[J]$ and compact-oriented for the Bloch state, representation firewall kept; spin as the projective boundary of a resolved relational degree of freedom recorded as a question (Part XX) |
| **triality (24.5)** | the Kerr–Newman weights as phase-blind intensities of a state in $\mathbb{CP}^2 = S^5/U(1)$, the moment map's image the simplex with torus fibers of relative phase; $S_3$ the Weyl group of the hypothetical flavor $U(3)$, which the shell does not supply — its carrier has $SU(2)\times U(1)$ and Weyl group $\mathbb Z_2$; a mixing-matrix rendering under which relative phase repacks the budget within a fixed sum; the Hopf phase the common phase, the relative phases inert under it; the theory's own carrier — ${\rm Sym}^2$ of the shell, weight two, twin-even, the complexified 3-space — with real frames as the rendering hypothesis, the polarization $p$ classifying the reachable budgets exactly by Schur–Horn, single-spinor states null and excluded, the channel state a conjugate-lift superposition; Kerr–Newman's source the complex axial datum $\zeta = \delta+ia$ — rod and Newman–Janis displacement, spacetime's own $i$, $p = 1$ — so spin pairs with gap and charge stays the meridian's; the selected axis breaking $SO(3)$ to $SO(2)$ with $\mathbb Z_4\times\mathbb Z_2$; the budget explained by Law I's Kinnersley symmetry — Harrison charging boosts the split pair $(M,Q)$ and leaves $\zeta$ untouched, so $M^2-Q^2 = \vert\zeta\vert^2$, one complex and one hyperbolic degree of freedom, the Ernst space the non-compact dual of $\mathbb{CP}^2$; the pair map $Av-B\bar v$ canonical through the quaternionic structure, the Hopf phase rotating the pair-built vector about the axis, $p = {\rm sech}\,\eta$, Kerr–Newman the equal-weight states, $\zeta$ charging-invariant; the $\mathbb Z_2$ step a magnitude involution — optical trace reads $\delta^2/r^4$, the RN Ricci tensor $Q^2/r^4$, budget $(M/r^2)^2$, closure under the field equations — with the readout exchange a proposal; all three readouts of one fall-off and three representation types, so $S_3$ is pre-rendering only; two optical Ricci eigenvalues, not three; the firewall — symmetry before labels (Part XIX) |
| **repacking (23)** | $R_{\rm opt} = -6\delta^2/r^4$: nonextremality is the trace of the optical Ricci, extremality optical scalar-flatness; the optical conformal factor obeys $\Delta W = \tfrac34\delta^2W^5/r^4$, harmonic at extremality; Majumdar–Papapetrou optically scalar-flat with three flat ends; Couch–Torrence an exact optical isometry with the identifier $24x^6/(1+x)^{12}$; the budget a simplex, one centrade with three channels; the fork settled by Law I, the twin pair the guaranteed budget; identity as a rendering groupoid whose degree $-1$ identifications invert the fiber — the twin parity and the throat exchange, not the inversion pair; the static two-body sector same-sign joint extremality with no continuous compensation; the optical layer a conformal invariant of the pre-quotient metric, so conformal repacking is empty and only a change of rendering changes agreeing structure; the dictionary static — the Finsler optical trace of Kerr–Newman fits $-(6/r^4)(\delta^2+c\,a^2)$, charge resolving at extremality and spin not; the optical trace as the mismatch between fiber-length locking and lapse; gauge does not repack; Boltzmannian counting with a measure to derive (Part XVIII) |
| **the central derivation (22.5)** | the compatibility relation $\mathfrak p_\Xi([N])\in\mathbb P(K_\Xi)$ isolates the symmetry normal for a source with a selected reflection plane and the required simple conjugate branches, with an in-plane $\mathbb{RP}^1$ branch; solved over histories through a flattened Galaxy: exact on the plane, $3°$–$13°$ off it, the plane found blind; per-history selection destroyed by the nearest star, the sheet's $\sqrt N$ recovery derived from bounded directional bias with a conditional effective $N$; Kerr's axis reconstructed by the same theorem; spin twists kernels by $a\sin i/b$ in the weak field — the displaced-lens rotation — and by $10.8°$ and $17.3°$ at the photon region's edge at $a = 0.95$ for a source at infinity, validated to $0.04°$; isotropic families select no axis; spin selects the axial geometry globally and only twists histories locally; the canonical normal an order parameter of the collective sheet (§XVII.10) |
| **two real forms (22)** | the complex canonical geometry with the compact $\rho$ and the intrinsic split real-form family $\mathcal R_{\rm split}\simeq\mathbb{RP}^2$, its member $\sigma_{[N]}$ solution-selected; the diamond $U(1)$, $\mathbb R^\times$ over $\{\pm1\}$; one connection restricting to phase and to scale, the split curvature the hyperboloid's area form; the dual shell $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ over $dS_2$; the residual sign is the planar holonomy; the split form of split signature $(3,3)$, whether that explains matter's preference for the compact form open; the reflection theorem: the surviving redshift sector is the polar Galactic normal, with the Hopf data required to carry an axial order parameter along it — the disk's rotation; the connection's oddness assumed, not derived (Part XVII) |
| **plane duality (21.5)** | the plane reflection lifts to the Hopf bundle with fiber inversion, forced by $c_1$; the standard Hopf connection is odd under it, giving Part XIII's parity a canonical enforcer conditional on equivariance, with the plane connection flat and holonomy $-1$; the plane is the intersection of two real forms, the lifted reflection equals $\rho_E$ on the normal mode up to Hopf-phase conjugation, the normal is the odd Hessian mode; two parity kill tests on the systemic dilation, which the meridian mode fails by loop orientation and the normal mode may survive; the normal mode extends across the round arena's classes but a measure-zero family, its extension with lumps open; the meridian, Galactic and per-image normals kept apart (§XVI.8) |
| **visual sheets (21)** | the sky as the projection of an enriched history space; rendered galaxies as stationary sheets; one point on several galaxies when fibers hold several states differing in dual-path data; the round twin image one inverted sky; shear cannot make disks; an emergent normal only as the suppressed direction of sheet selection, never a second kernel of the projection; $\eta$ and $D_{\rm app}$ independent observables on the moduli space of sheets, $D_{\rm app}$ built without $\eta$ or $H_0$, with the population factorization $\eta\simeq f(D_{\rm app})$, $f'(0) = H_0/c$, as the target; locally an arcminute of sky is an AU of source at the ppm conjugate displacement, so coherence is automatic given $L_\eta\gg$ AU and internal structure must be sheet structure if one sheet maps the image; the returned Sun at magnitude $-8$ as a conditional benchmark, suppression $10^{0.4(m_{\rm lim}+8)}$ required (Part XVI) |
| **finite-width test (20.6)** | twin-paired returns do not accumulate $\eta$, so large $z$ must arise within one class, $2\times10^4$ to $2\times10^5$ above the local calibration and outside the proved small-$\epsilon$ regime; determine the finite-$\epsilon$ scale character and whether one class family spans $\eta$ from $10^{-6}$ to order one (§XIV.8) |
| **recurrence-age bound (19.5)** | reaching redshift $z$ by traversal count needs $\pi\ln(1+z)/0.4H_0$ of propagation, $\ell$ cancelling — $10$ Gyr at $z = 0.1$, $76$ at $z = 1$ — against the Galaxy's $13$ Gyr: large $z$ cannot come mainly from $n$ (§XIV.8) |
| **relative monodromy (19.4)** | the screen Jacobi transfer matrix relative to the round arena splits into a rotation $\mathcal F/4$ and a squeeze, noncompact, one-signed, background-free, equal to $\mathcal F/4$ for any compact lump; the class redshift proposed as its island average, decisive test: sign and spread from 8 kpc (§XIV.9) |
| **focusing functional (19.3)** | the renormalized Ricci focusing functional, $\ell\int\delta R_{ab}\bar k^a\bar k^b d\bar\lambda$ with the Killing normalization and the round background subtracted, is non-exact, additive and one-signed for centred classes, and equals $1.8\,H_0\ell/c$ independently of $\ell$ — the coincidence $M_{\rm gal}/h^2\approx2cH_0/G$; universal only for collective classes centred on the Galaxy (§XIV.6) |
| **modular return (19)** | other galaxies as recurrent renderings of the one arena: secondaries break the round degeneracy, about two first-order members per star, not a galaxy count; a return map, islands and a unitary dilation readout are proposals; the flux redistribution and a non-exact cocycle are open (Part XIV) |
| **galactic well (19)** | $GM/c^2\ell\sim H_0\ell/c$ encodes the arena radius ($\beta = 1/4$ selects 7 kpc); the hemisphere flux vanishes by symmetry and the depth is packing-dominated, so the dial's surface is undefined (§XIV.6) |
| **self-return test (18)** | on the closed arena the Sun's own light would return after $2\pi\ell/c$ at the solar constant from every direction, $10^8$ times the night sky, and still at the night-sky level once smeared by lumps: so either light does not return or a return is rendered elsewhere, dimmed by $(D_{\rm app}/\ell)^2$; a modular-return branch is opened as a ledger on that condition (§XIII.10) |
| **conjugate-path optics (18)** | the isolated ray as the zero-kernel limit of the Jacobi operator; on the round arena $L_n = n\pi\ell$ with the first conjugate point at the twin; the gate shut within the solar system; the Photon History Postulate in the readout, first tested by the self-image it must not render (§XIII.10) |
| **bundle-form $\hat g$ (18)** | the parsimonious target: what condition on $\hat g = \pi^*g+R_H^2(d\chi+\mathcal A_H)^2$ yields Law I on the quotient; Berry's phase with a size, $\Delta s_H = (L_H/2\pi)\Delta\chi$; vacuum Einstein on $E$ excluded — its monopole fiber shrinks where the locking grows (§IX.1) |
| **semi-free fiber (17.6)** | the circle's generator vanishes on a restored meridian, so a smooth completion collapses the fiber, $L_H = O(\sqrt\rho)$, as at a Taub–NUT nut; the locking and $\mathcal A_H$ are laws on the regular stratum (§II.2) |
| **research program (17.5)** | ten targets in order of dependence, headed by a definition of unpacking — the bottleneck (end of §XIII.0) |
| **black-hole budget (17.4)** | $r_+r_- = a^2+Q^2$, and Christodoulou–Ruffini: an irreducible, area-fixed mass plus extractable charge and spin; the rapidity at fixed $\delta$ shrinks the area, so it is a change of rendering, not a process (§XIII.5) |
| **local model of $E$ (17.3)** | near each meridian, $\mathbb R_t\times S^1\times\mathbb C^2$ with the diagonal circle: smooth, semi-free, quotient restoring the meridian, unit Hopf class on every linking sphere; global gluing open (§II.2) |
| **gravitational firewall (v17 branch)** | centrades carry localization, propagation carries relations; zero Weyl curvature cannot be imposed on radiation, and the weaker "no autonomous Weyl datum" needs a local split GR lacks (§XIII.9) |
| **prospect holonomy (v17 branch)** | no flat bundle closes in the plane yet fails along the normal; curvature odd under the plane reflection makes the plane flat while opening loops along the normal; exact planar closure also needs trivial holonomy on the projective plane (§XIII.9) |
| **orbital flip (v17 branch)** | a half-turn keeps rotation but cannot break axisymmetry; a handedness flip freezes each rosette into one stationary spiral segment but reverses the rendered rotation half the time (§XIII.7) |
| **emergent charge (hypothesis)** | charge as the antisymmetric readout of a chiral pair whose symmetric invariants set gravity: $M = m_++m_-$, $Q = m_+-m_-$, $\delta^2 = 4m_+m_-$, with the complexified meridian's modulus giving the ratio, $m_-/m_+ = \lvert w\rvert ^2$. Exact as algebra; the readouts are open **[S/O]** (§X.0); the rapidity cannot be the spinor pair's gauge freedom in any construction reproducing it, and only the swap $Q\to-Q$ is invisible to gravity |
| **the $O(2)$ answer** | the quotient does not itself enlarge $U(1)$: untwisted Maxwell descends as $U(1)$ but admits no isolated charge, and only a descended isolated charge needs twisted descent, hence $O(2)$. Global equivariance of that kind duplicates electrostatic energy in active mass — bounded by lunar ranging at $\eta\lesssim5\times10^{-11}$ within the summed architecture — or, if descended, makes the twin luminous (§X.13) |
| **the branch's fork** | structural charge of the bridge, geon or meridian-momentum type is incompatible with the barren-twin Maxwell assignment: three choices, and one sharp question — does the projective quotient enlarge $U(1)$ to $O(2)$? (§X.12) |
| **extremality angle** | $(Q,\delta) = M(\cos m,\sin m)$: real $m$ subextremal, imaginary $m$ super-extremal. The two-factor formula on a conformal $S^3$, made exact, is the bridge with radius $\delta/4$ — no room for amplification (§X.9) |
| **meridian displacement** | real displacement cannot carry charge: gravity is blind to it — the seed (§X.4) |
| **extremal barren twin** | in the $R/\ell\ll1$ comparison limit, geometrically extremal and electromagnetically empty: an observer there infers a Maxwell-form stress with $Q_{\rm eff}^2 = M^2$ and no Maxwell field — gravitational stress without Maxwell matter (§X.6) |
| **the static limit** | pencil **[S]** $+$ quotient $S^3/\pm = \mathbb{RP}^3$ **[D]**. The limit as a whole is *not* derived — only its quotient, and only given the pencil |
| **Structure III** | a connection structure, **not a law**, on the regular stratum: no field equation, $\lvert c_1\rvert  = 1$ on linking spheres with induced sign, two local cross-components with $A\mapsto A+d\lambda$ unquotiented |
| **the obstruction** | $H^2(S^4) = 0$: Law 0 needs the secondary meridians removed, as every earlier version did |
| **chiral bridge** | $SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{\rm shell} = \Delta SL(2,\mathbb{C})\subset SL(2,\mathbb{C})_L\times SL(2,\mathbb{C})_R$; SD Taub–NUT shells are Hopf. But reciprocal warp factors and Berger squashing ($c/b = 2m/(r+2m)$) block the metric, and $u$ is presupposed, so no time (§XI.3) |
| **Kirchhoff at the poles** | Hopf classes on the $2N$ meridian arcs sum to zero, so outward classes at all equatorial crossings cancel in total: **a single meridian's two crossings carry opposite induced signs; several meridians are not paired crossing by crossing.** A sign *pairing*, independent of occupancy. Barrenness would follow only from a matter-selection rule the theory does not contain (§V.4) |
| **primitive data** | $E$ with its real-analytic structure, and a complexification canonical as a germ along it (§0.4); the regular stratum $E^\circ$, on which the circle acts freely and the principal connection $\mathcal A_H$ lives (§II.2); a projectable $\widehat{\mathcal{U} }_{\rm mer}\subset TE$, whose image $d\pi_H(\widehat{\mathcal{U} }_{\rm mer})$ is the meridian structure; and $\rho_E$, an antiholomorphic involution of $E_\mathbb{C}$ with $E = \operatorname{Fix}(\rho_E)$, compatible with $U(1)_H$ via $\lambda\mapsto\bar\lambda^{-1}$ |
